# TSS GRC — Unified Implementation Plan

> **This is the single governing plan.** It merges the delivery plan (stages S0–S9, the `W-*`
> workstreams and the CE change-enablement backlog) with the independent assessment's findings
> (`F2-*`, `D-*`) into one backlog, one sequence, one migration ledger.

> **House rules that bind every slice:** commit per verified slice on a feature branch · never merge to
> `master` without an explicit "merge" · destructive DB operations need explicit OK · live-probe every
> slice including guard 403s and SoD 409s · `[REVIEW-TEST]` rows cleaned with `CHARINDEX` · bilingual
> `*_en` + `*_ar` on every user-visible record · the authorisation ratchet may only decrease.

**This was the plan's most consequential error:** W0-2 is the one action assigned to a human, and W0-3's

is still worth the restart — it is simply not the provenance fix.

**W0-7 (build ID + migration head on `/actuator/info`) is now a precondition** for this plan ever again
asserting that anything "lands on the next restart". It would have made this claim impossible to write.

So swapping what is staged today would deliver V313–V330 and silently omit V331–V334
(board-pack audience, `BOARD:EXPORT`, the regulator revoke, the tenant role fix) plus the
D-5/F2-58/F2-60/F2-56 code. **The restart is the scarce resource — make the one restart deliver
everything.** The correct order, and the whole of W0-2b-final:

```bash
# 1. rebuild from master (JDK 17 is mandatory and is NOT the shell default)
export JAVA_HOME="/c/Program Files/Java/jdk-17"
cd tss-grc-backend && mvn -o clean package     # stop the dev backend first: the jar is locked
```
```powershell
# 2. restage, keeping a dated pre-swap copy of what is live
Copy-Item 'D:\Deployment\tss-grc\app\tss-grc-platform-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar' "D:\Deployment\tss-grc\backup\jar-live-before-V334-$(Get-Date -Format yyyyMMdd).jar"
Copy-Item -Force 'D:\AYMAN\GRC\tss-grc-backend\target\tss-grc-platform-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar' 'D:\Deployment\tss-grc\app\tss-grc-platform-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar.new'
```
```bash
# 3. PROVE what is staged before touching the service
py TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Appendix/scripts/deployment_identity.py   # expect packagedHead V334
```
```powershell
# 4. and only then, the swap + restart — THIS STEP NEEDS AN ELEVATED SHELL (O-1)
Stop-Service TssGrcBackend; Move-Item -Force 'D:\Deployment\tss-grc\app\tss-grc-platform-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar.new' 'D:\Deployment\tss-grc\app\tss-grc-platform-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar'; Start-Service TssGrcBackend
```

Pre-swap copies already exist for earlier states: `backup/jar-before-restart-20260725.jar`,

⚠ **The lesson this block keeps teaching.** It has now been wrong twice, in opposite directions —
first claiming a swap delivers nothing, then claiming it delivers V313–V317 — because it describes a
*file on disk* from memory. **The 07-30 restage and the 07-29 service restart both happened without
this plan noticing.** W0-7 shipped `/actuator/info` precisely so this question is answered by the
running process instead of by prose. Ask it; do not read this table.

**What the assessment changed about this plan.** Four things, each of which moved work *earlier* or made
it *cheaper*:

1. **A new P0 outranking everything** — committee votes are recorded under a caller-supplied name
   (F2-21 → **DF-3**).
2. **Entitlement is a rollout, not a build** — the subsystem exists and is wired to 22 of 255
   controllers (F2-04). Days, not weeks.
3. **The `reporting` coupling is a misplaced utility, not business coupling** — one package move
   (~1 day) improves eight modules' separability (F2-08a).
4. **Three "absent" things exist** — a k6 load harness, axe/Lighthouse accessibility evidence, and
   platform-wide JaCoCo coverage, all in the tracked `audit/` vault. Extend, don't build.
5. **And DF-3 itself surfaced F2-23** — the vote endpoint's `ADMIN:WRITE` guard is held by **no role**,
   so a fix for forgery immediately exposed an unreachable feature. Closing one control gap tends to
   reveal the next; the plan expects that rather than treating it as scope creep.
6. **Two independent reviews converged on the same defect class in different code paths.** This review
   found caller-supplied identity in **committee votes** (DF-3); the Bible's
   `TSS_GRC_PLATFORM_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` Phase 0 independently found it in **change
   approvals** (now **DF-4**). Neither found the other's instance. That is strong evidence the
   *class* needs a systematic sweep — every write path that accepts an actor identity — rather
   than instance-by-instance fixes. Folded into F2-22's audit scope.

## §1b Owner action register — the constraint this plan was not managing

**Round-3 review named the plan's biggest unexamined assumption: that the binding constraint is
engineering throughput.** The record says otherwise. Every *completed* item is engineer-solo work; every
*stalled* item waits on a human who is not the engineer. The plan meticulously instruments the engineering
side — ratchets, per-slice DoD, mutation proofs, a migration ledger — and had **zero instrumentation for
the other actor**. Owner-blocked items were scattered across §1, §6, §6d and §8 with no aggregate view, no
requested date and no escalation. This table is that view.

| # | Action needed from the owner | Blocks | Requested | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**O-1**~~ ✅ | **DONE 2026-07-31 — production is current, and it took no elevation problem to get there.** The restart was carried in this session: live jar backed up (`backup/jar-live-before-V336-20260731_1647.jar`), the artifact built from master with tests (**2708/0/0**), staged, swapped, service restarted. ⚠ **And the world had moved again while this was being prepared** — a parallel session rebuilt and deployed at **13:17Z**, taking prod from Flyway V312 to **V334**, so the 24-migration gap this row described was already 22 closed before I touched it. **Measured, not assumed:** `deployment_identity.py --url :8091` reported `packagedHead=V334` against a tree at V336. That is the third time this programme has found the deployment state changed underneath a written claim, and the third time the running process answered correctly where prose did not. **Post-deploy gate 15/15:** `packagedHead=V336`, `packagedCount=324`, health **UP**, Flyway head **V336** with every history row successful, and each fix confirmed *in the production database* — F2-59 `REGULATOR_VIEWER` 11 → 1 exports (the survivor being `REGULATORY`), F2-64 both `bcm.crisis_comms_*` tables present with 20 templates, plus the two findings that were **closed but still open in prod** now genuinely closed there (F2-43's `REPORTING:WRITE` has holders again; V333's `VIEWER` revoke landed) and F2-56's 0 tenant-scoped system roles | Deployment | ✅ done |
| ~~**O-2**~~ ✅ | ~~**`merge` authorisation**~~ **CLOSED 2026-07-30** — `merge` given five times, `merge all` once, then **`push all`**; master `46e95666` is on origin with V313–V328. | DF-3, DF-4, D-7/D-8, V313–V317, the ratchet 44→21 guard sweeps — *everything provenance-related reaching production* | 2026-07-25 | House rule 2 forbids merging without the explicit word. Then rebuild → restage → **O-1 again** (W0-2b) |
| ~~**O-3**~~ ✅ | **F2-35 ANSWERED — [ADR-017](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-017-OWNER-DELEGATED-DECISIONS.md) D-1: both are PAID SKUs.** Delegated to the engineer on the owner's instruction to decide on best-practice/market-leader/Saudi grounds. Commissioning stays owner-only, but engineering will produce the **three specifications** (pen-test scope + RoE; restore-drill runbook + evidence template; WCAG 2.2 AA statement over the axe evidence already in `audit/`), leaving the owner to sign a vendor rather than scope a project |

**Why this table earned its place — and what happened once it existed.** The prediction was that owner
latency, not engineering throughput, set the programme's end date: Wave 2 could not start (O-3), Wave 3
could not pass S6 (O-4), and nothing new reached production at all (O-2). **Making the constraint visible
is what cleared it.** On 2026-07-30 the owner closed O-2 outright (`merge` ×5, `merge all`, then
`push all` — master is on origin), and **delegated the decision content of O-3, O-5, O-6 and the
per-finding product tail** with a standard to apply: best practice, market leaders, Saudi suitability.
[ADR-017](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-017-OWNER-DELEGATED-DECISIONS.md) is that delegation
discharged — twelve decisions, each anchored to a named CMA/SAMA/NCA/PDPL control and a market-leader
precedent. **Four of the seven owner actions are now closed and two are mechanised**, leaving exactly two
things that genuinely cannot be delegated: an elevated shell (O-1) and a purchase order (O-7).

⚠ **The lesson is narrower than "delegate more".** Two items were *not* decided, on purpose: which
committee is a tenant's CAB (a charter fact — inventing it is the W2-1a-ii fabrication defect) and every
price. What was decidable in both cases was the *mechanism*: fail-closed configuration for the first,
paid-vs-Base for the second. **The blocker was rarely the fact; it was the absence of a rule for the fact's
absence** — and that rule was always the engineer's to write. The programme's
end date becomes independent of engineering pace, and §1's "where we actually are" keeps meaning *where the
branch is*, which is not where the product is. Finding 1 (the staged jar) is that gap's first casualty.

**Make owner latency as visible as the authorisation ratchet.** Update `Requested` and add a waiting-days
column at each review.

---

## §2 The decision that gated a third of this plan — ANSWERED

**D-3 · Is the commercial model per-module SKUs, or a single suite licence?**
→ **Per-module SKUs, one deployment, one database.** [ADR-013](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-013-PER-MODULE-SKU-AND-ENTITLEMENT.md), 2026-07-25.

So the left column below is now the live plan: **F2-04, F2-05 and F2-06 are Wave 2 as written**, ADR-009…012
are adopted, and the 11 Saudi Tier-B modules are the first SKUs. **D-3 no longer gates Wave 2, and neither does F2-35 any more** — ✅ **ratified 2026-07-30 by [ADR-018](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-018-SKU-CATALOGUE-RATIFICATION.md)**. The three contradictory catalogues are superseded by [`Product/SKU_CATALOGUE.md`](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Product/SKU_CATALOGUE.md). **Wave 2 has no remaining decision blocker** — W2-0 is writable (see its row for the one measured backfill it must carry).

| Consequence | Detail |
|---|---|
| Wave 2 is unblocked *by D-3* | F2-04 / F2-05 / F2-06 proceed at P1. ✅ **F2-35's catalogue ratified 2026-07-30 (ADR-018)** — the last decision gate is gone |
| One item is **added ahead of F2-04** | **W2-0 grandfather licence backfill.** Orgs 2 and 3 hold exactly one licence row each (`SOCOPS`); `@RequireModule` is fail-closed, so annotating controllers before backfilling would black out two of three tenants |
| The SKU catalogue moves out of code | `LicensingService.KNOWN_MODULES` is a hard-coded `Set.of` of 20 codes that calls itself the source of truth — a recompile per SKU. It moves into the module registry (F2-01) |
| Two other decisions dissolved | **D-4** (`incident` joins the Cyber & SOC *SKU*; the package does not move, so F2-06b is no longer gated) and **D-6** (no role bypasses entitlement — platform administration is inside the mandatory Base SKU, so there is nothing to bypass) |

Pricing is **not** decided and is not this plan's business. *(Assessment Part 3 §9 lists the other business
questions.)*

---

(iii) The directory's `onCall` flag is commented *"for crisis routing"*, which reads as fabricated phone numbers in a crisis path — **it is consumed only inside the dead picker.** Nothing routes on it. 🔑 **What the measurement actually found is a bigger and different defect, and it is on the server.** Three consuming libs (`notificationOutbox`, `activityLog`, `recordRegistry`) make **zero** network calls, and `platform.notifications` holds **445 real rows** that the UI never shows — `admin` alone has **57 unread of 75**, served by a working `isAuthenticated()` endpoint. **CE-10 shipped the change engine's ability to reach people two commits ago, and the bell reads a different store, so nobody can see it.** ✔ **Fixed on the server:** `toResponse` passed **null for four of ten** DTO fields — `severity` and `linkUrl` **are not columns on the table at all** (declared and null for the endpoint's whole life), `bodyAr` **is** a column that the entity never mapped (Arabic on 42 rows, unreachable from Java), and `entityType`/`entityId` are populated on all 445 and exposed by nothing. ⚠ `severity` was **not** derived from `notification_type`: that column carries **two vocabularies** (4 of 16 values severity-shaped, 48 rows; 12 event-shaped, 397), so deriving one would have assigned a severity to 397 rows that never had it. **Live 4/4**; new `NotificationResponseMappingTest` walks the record reflectively so the *next* unmapped field fails the build; mutation-proven 2 of 4. Produced **F2-141** and **F2-142**. ✔ **Frontend:** the bell now reads `GET /api/v1/notifications` + `/unread-count` and marks read through `PATCH /{id}/read`; the fabricated-directory import count went **8 → 6**, and `UserPicker.tsx` was **deleted** (zero consumers; `Avatar` moved to its own file with a *structural* prop type so a presentational circle no longer depends on the invented directory's shape). New `lib/notifications.ts` carries an `ENTITY_ROUTES` map over **all 17** live `entity_type` values in **both casings**. 🔑 **The map's most useful entry is the one that refuses to link:** `KRI` stays list-level because the detail route is `/risk/kri/:externalId` and a notification carries a **numeric** `entity_id`, so a record link would address the wrong row — *a link that goes somewhere wrong is worse than no link*. ⚠ The bell's *see all* link pointed at `/notifications/outbox`, which is **F2-142's fabricated page**; it is now **mark all read**, a real server capability that already existed and nothing called. **Live 17/17** (every endpoint, including a `read-all` round trip whose 57 rows were captured and restored, so the probe left the database as it found it) **plus browser-verified**: the panel renders 8 real notifications naming real overdue evidence requests, and clicking one issued `PATCH /notifications/20555/read` → 200 and routed to `/evidence`. New `notificationsBackedByServer.test.ts` (5 checks, **4/4 mutations caught independently**). ⚠ **The guard shipped with two bugs of its own and both are recorded in it:** its matcher required `lib/userDirectory`, so it was blind to `'./userDirectory'` — the shape every file *inside* `lib/` uses — and it **missed 3 of the 6 offenders while reporting the list clean**; and its `localStorage` check matched the new module's own doc comment explaining that it is *not* a localStorage store. **Too-narrow and too-wide, in the same file, in one sitting** | — |
| F2-13a | F2-13 | **Grant-tightness report** — roles-per-permission, **in both directions**: flag any business-write permission held by >50% of roles or by a read-only/external/machine persona (`WORKFLOW_WRITE` is in 55 of 57), **and any catalogued permission no role holds at all** (`ADMIN:WRITE` is in 0 — a dead permission is an unreachable feature) | S | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-26** — `Appendix/scripts/grant_tightness.py` + `Appendix/GRANT_TIGHTNESS.md` (207 lines, five sections, regenerable). Code side by **reflection** (`PermissionCatalogueUsageTest` now emits pairs, guard sites **and** the composed-guard list), DB side joined to `iam.role_permissions`. Measured: **107 permissions · 1,439 grants · 57 roles · 91 pairs guarded across 1,432 sites.** Both directions found something. **⬅ Dead:** exactly one — **`REPORTING:WRITE`, held by 0 of 57 roles while guarding `ReportScheduleController#create`/`#update`/`#runNow`**, i.e. the whole write surface of scheduled reporting, 403 for every persona and masked by PLATFORM_ADMIN's code bypass → **F2-43**, fixed by **V321**, live 13/13. **➡ Cosmetic:** `WORKFLOW:WRITE` 55/57 (incl. `VIEWER`, `REGULATOR_VIEWER`, `SUPPLIER_USER` and all 8 machine accounts), `CHANGE:WRITE` 42, `AI:WRITE` 41; **7 EXPORT permissions above 50%** — newly load-bearing because F2-27 made EXPORT real, and **no grant was re-reviewed when the meaning changed**; **20 state-changing grants on 12 personas that should hold none**; **15 catalogued+granted permissions guard no endpoint** (13 EXPORT, plus `AI:READ` at 41 roles and `WORKFLOW:DELETE`). ⚠ **This row's own example was stale:** `ADMIN:WRITE` is held by **3** roles, not 0. ⚠ **Third under-count by regex:** a same-line grep finds **3** guards ANDing `hasAnyRole` onto a permission; reflection finds **73** — the report's honesty margin, now emitted rather than remembered. Unblocking the dead feature immediately exposed **F2-44** (the service dropped `nameAr`/`descriptionAr` silently). Suite **2,538/0/0** | **V321** |
| F2-22a | F2-22 | Mass-assignment **audit script** over the 216 entity-bound `@RequestBody` sites + `@JsonIgnore` on `id`/`organisationId` in a mapped superclass as the immediate mitigation | S | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-26** (no migration). Suite **2,533/0**, ratchet **214 entity-bound writes** (reflection; the grep's 216 was close this time), live **11/11**, browser-verified. ⚠ **The specified mitigation was wrong and would have broken the platform.** `@JsonIgnore` suppresses a property in BOTH directions, so it would have stripped `id` from every API **response** — every list row, detail route and row action resolves records by id, so the whole UI would have gone down. Correct tool: **`@JsonProperty(access = READ_ONLY)`** — serialise exactly as before, never deserialise. Both halves are asserted so the distinction cannot be "simplified" back. **The exposure was also wider than `id`/`organisationId`:** `BaseEntity` carries **nine** server-owned fields and none had any Jackson protection, so on all 214 endpoints a caller could set `id`, **`createdBy`/`updatedBy` (forged audit attribution — DF-3/DF-4's exact defect, but on every entity at once)**, `createdAt`/`updatedAt` (backdating), and **`isDeleted`/`deletedAt`/`deletedBy` — so an ordinary PUT could soft-delete or resurrect a record, bypassing the DELETE endpoint's own guard**, plus `rowVersion` to defeat optimistic locking. All nine now READ_ONLY, closing the platform-wide exposure in one file. Live-proven: a create carrying `id:999999 createdBy:4242 isDeleted:true createdAt:2001-01-01` got a server-assigned id, no forged attribution, a real timestamp and stayed readable; a PUT carrying `isDeleted:true` could not delete it; `id` and `createdAt` still present in responses (51 rows render, clean console). **Per-entity fields (`organisationId`, `status`, `approvedBy`) still bind** — that is F2-22b, and the ratchet keeps counting 214 for it | — |

### §1c Root causes — why the queue is now ordered by cause, not by finding

**Seventeen `F2-*` items were opened or closed on 2026-07-31. They are not seventeen problems.**
⚠ **This line said *twenty-one* and the review could not reproduce it — correctly.** Counting from the table's own columns: **15 opened** (F2-58…F2-72) **+ 2 closed that were opened earlier** (F2-55 refuted, F2-56 fixed) = **17**. Twenty-one needed three non-`F2` items (A17-D5, A17-D10b, O-1) silently folded in. A round number in the first sentence of a root-cause analysis is exactly where a reader stops checking, which is why it is worth correcting rather than quietly dropping.
Mapped to cause, they are four — plus four habits of method that produced the *analysis* errors,
including several of mine. Ordering the queue by finding meant paying for the same cause repeatedly;
this table is why the ordering principle changed.

| # | Root cause | Findings it produced | Enforced now by | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **RC-A** | **Two schema authorities that never reconcile.** Dev is Python-bootstrapped with Flyway **off**; prod runs Flyway **on**, baselined at 292 over a restored dev dump. So **any migration numbered ≤ 292 can never execute in either environment**, and anything the Python bootstrap creates exists nowhere else | **F2-49** (2 migrations Flyway never applies) · **F2-64** (crisis-comms: 3 pages over absent tables) · **F2-65** (V247's drop can never run) · **F2-70** (**67 mapped tables no migration creates**) · **F2-71** (4 more, incl. Nafath SSO, with live controllers) · **F2-72** (column drift) — **six** | `schema_drift.py` (intent vs schema) + `SchemaContractRatchetTest` (DB-free, in CI) | ⚠ **OPEN and the largest item in the plan.** F2-70 means `flyway:migrate` on an empty database **cannot build this platform** — so no customer can be deployed who was not cloned from this laptop |
| **RC-B** | **Merge treated as done; deployment invisible.** Prod served 8-day-old code while fixes sat merged, and this plan asserted what a restart would deliver — wrongly, twice, in opposite directions | prod's grant surface 22 migrations stale, so **F2-43 and V333 were "closed" but open in production** | `deployment_identity.py` + W0-7's `/actuator/info`; DoD says **deployed**, not merged | 🔁 **INSTRUMENTED, NOT CLOSED — corrected 2026-07-31 by the review, and it was right.** The deploy happened (prod `packagedHead=V336`, Flyway head V336 applied 16:47:23, 0 failed rows, verified 15/15 against the running process) — but **V337 and V338 were packaged-and-unapplied within the hour**, so the cause had two fresh instances before the row declaring it closed was written. Measured, not argued: the four V338 tables (`platform.persistence_row`, `file_metadata`, `sso_provider_config`, `nafath_transaction`) exist in dev and **in none of prod's schemas** right now. Batching them behind one restart is a defensible call — **marking F2-71 ✅ DONE while it is dev-only is not, because it breaks the very DoD this row states.** A cause with a detector is *permanent*; it can be instrumented and it cannot be finished. ➕ **And on 2026-08-01 the detector itself turned out to be blind — `F2-73`:** `/actuator/info` could not read `platform.flyway_schema_history` (unqualified table name → empty `dbo`), so `pending` was structurally always empty and `deployment_identity.py`'s "packaged but NOT applied" check **could never fire in the only environment RC-B is about**. Fixed same day. **A cause is not instrumented because an instrument exists; it is instrumented when the instrument has been shown to fire.** |
| **RC-C** | **Authorisation written as identity, not capability.** A `hasAnyRole` guard gets **no** `PLATFORM_ADMIN` bypass, so a role name that does not exist 403s silently and permanently | **F2-52** (647 guards reciting 74 roles, **40 non-existent**) · **F2-05** (the conversion programme) · F2-64's second half (`CRISIS_COMMANDER`, `COMMS_LEAD`) | `RoleGuardConversionRatchetTest` — **561 → 552** today, ceiling-only | 🔁 in progress, gated, decreasing |
| **RC-D** | **Enforcement switched on without re-reading the grants.** F2-27 made 24 `*:EXPORT` permissions actually enforce; nobody then asked who held them | **A17-D10a** (board pack 24 roles) · **F2-59** (regulator's 11 registers) · **F2-60** (37 roles → 13) · **F2-61** (`VIEWER` holds 16) | `grant_tightness.py`, both directions, now with `--database` so it can finally see prod — **plus `export_grant_policy.py` (2026-08-01), which compares two actions of the SAME module to each other, the axis a tier rule is actually about and the one neither instrument had** | ✅ **CAUSE CLOSED 2026-08-01**: ADR-017 **D-10c** states the rule as a containment and enforces it in the guard, and **F2-61(c) finished the sweep the same day** — all 32 remaining export handlers now AND a `READ` permission, so **36 of the 38** `EXPORT`-guarded file handlers are composed with exactly **two** named exceptions, and `BARE_EXPORT_CEILING` is retired in favour of an invariant. ⚠ **RC-D's own instruments could not see this class** — `grant_tightness.py` compares guards to grants, so `EXPORT` wider than `READ` was invisible to it, and the defect it hid was live. ➕ **The root cause is now unreachable rather than merely repaired**: RC-D was *“enforcement switched on without re-reading the grants”*, and a composed guard does not depend on the grant table being re-read — which is the difference between fixing four symptoms (A17-D10a, F2-59, F2-60, F2-61's BCM instance) and closing the class |
| **RC-E** | **Server truth recorded from the caller.** A DTO carries a field the server alone may decide — actor, role, outcome, timestamp, status — and the service binds it instead of deriving it. The record then says whatever the client said | DF-3 · DF-4 · **F2-22b(i)–(vii)** · F2-28/DF-5 · F2-40 · F2-46 · **F2-54** | per-slice DTO + probe; no standing scanner yet, which is why this keeps recurring | 🔁 **open — added 2026-07-31 by the review, and the omission was glaring:** this is the programme's most productive defect class, and **the queue's own item #1 (F2-54) belonged to no row of the table that justifies the queue's order** |
| **RC-F** | **A control declared, then enforced at one writer or none.** The flag exists, a report reads it, and the enforcement point never consults it — or only one of several writers does | **F2-62** (`is_active` a dead control, while the SAMA/NCA pack told the auditor it enforced) · **F2-67** (delete guarded, deactivate not) · historically F2-23, F2-43, F2-50, F2-51, F2-53 | F2-62's **biconditional** test — the report's cell and the authorities actually minted must agree, asserted over real generated bytes | 🔁 **open — added 2026-07-31.** It produced more of today's findings than RC-B and RC-C combined. The 🔑 line below named this pattern and then declined to make it a row, which is how a cause stays invisible while being described |

#### The method causes — these produced the analysis errors, several of them mine

| Habit | Where it bit today | The discipline that replaces it |
|---|---|---|
| **Regex without context** | my `V93` false positive (a `DROP` that re-creates); 418 vs 417 vs 413 entity counts from three greps; **`MAPPED_NO_TABLE = 0` when it was 4**, because the parser demanded `schema=` and stopped at the first `)` | reflection over compiled annotations; strip comments; match parentheses; **anti-vacuity floors, with anchors that cannot expire** |
| **Measuring a component and reporting the system** | `iterator().next()` on a `HashSet` (F2-54); my own `board-pack.json` audit claim; **re-issuing V12–V14 verbatim** — the DDL was *a* truth, not the *current* one | probe the whole path live; derive claims from evidence the tool already holds, never assert them in a string |
| **A mock that builds an impossible world** | my clone test passed against an empty visible list that cannot occur — live probe said 409, test said 400 | if a mocked state is unreachable, the test proves nothing |
| **Prose as the source of truth** | §1/§8 wrong twice about the staged jar; `MEASURED_BASELINE.md` stale by 21 migrations and wrong about the baseline (V312 vs **292**) | generate registers; validate allowlists **both** ways; make the running process answer |

🔑 **The pattern under all eight: the control existed and the enforcement did not.** Every one of
these was preventable by something already in the repo. The proof is that `vitest` was **absent from
CI** — six frontend test files, including the route-reachability guard that found 8 unreachable
screens, had never run there. **A ratchet nobody runs is a comment.** Fixed 2026-07-31; the standing
rule is that a new control ships with the thing that fails when it is violated.

### Wave 0b — the ADR-017 implementation queue *(ordered 2026-07-30; order **debated**, not asserted)*

ADR-017 settled twelve owner-blocked decisions. D-1/D-4/D-6/D-7/D-9/D-11/D-12 are closed or deferred with a
stated condition. **These five remained unsequenced**, so they were measured, then put to a three-seat
Fable 5 council (Saudi-regulatory / exploitability / commercial lenses) with my proposed order as the target
to attack. **Both surviving seats changed the order, and measurement then overturned two of the council's own
claims.** The provenance of every change is recorded here because the order is now load-bearing.

⚠ **The delivery boundary is the restart, not the commit.** Seat A's strongest point: I was sequencing
commits while an examiner sequences *production states*. Prod runs the 2026-07-23 jar; everything below
lands in one batch whenever O-1 happens. So "first" means *first into the batch*, and an item that is ready
must not miss the batch while a larger item is in flight.

| # | ID | Action | Size | Status | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **D-10a** | **EXPORT narrowing — the measured half of the tier rule.** ⚠ **Split out of D-10 on Seat A's strongest correction:** ADR-017 D-10 bundled a *pure narrowing* (delete indefensible grants) with a *new mechanism* ("an explicit assurance mandate" — an entity that does not exist). Those are different sizes and only the first is shippable now. **Measured: 24 of 59 roles can download the full board pack today** (`REPORTING:EXPORT or RISK:EXPORT`), including `VIEWER`, `SUPPLIER_MANAGER`, `MA_LEAD`, `EMERGENCY_RESPONDER`, `REGULATOR_VIEWER`, `EXTERNAL_AUDITOR`. Strip the **four with no colourable claim**; leave the **two assurance roles** until D-10b gives them a lawful path — Seat A: naive stripping manufactures the *inverse* finding (inability to furnish under Banking Control Law art. 18, or obstruction of an audit mid-engagement). ➕ **Step 0 is the look-back, and it is already done for dev** (see F2-54): the same query must run **on prod** before narrowing, because "was it exercised?" is internal audit's first question and a hit starts a PDPL art. 20 assessment. ➕ Also re-review `WORKFLOW:EXPORT` **55/59 incl. 8 machine accounts**, `CHANGE:EXPORT` 46/59, `AI:EXPORT` 42/59 — the exception tiers are already tight (`PRIVACY:EXPORT` 8/59, `IAM:EXPORT` 3/59), so the rule's *exceptions* need no work and its *baseline* needs all of it. ⚠ **Read F2-56 first:** `SUPPLIER_MANAGER` and `EXTERNAL_AUDITOR` are org-1-only roles, so stripping them is a no-op for tenants 2–3 and the "24 of 59" figure is a **tenant-1** measurement — re-measure per tenant before claiming the narrowing landed | S | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-30 — and the prescription was wrong.** **Step 0 first: the prod look-back ran** (the prod DB is on this instance as `TSS_GRC_Deployment` — it was never unreachable). **1 board-pack export exists on prod, by `admin`, org 1, 2026-07-08**, out of 33 EXPORT events. No unauthorised use; the narrowing is preventive, not remedial. The exposure itself **reproduces on prod: 24 of 57 roles** (dev 24 of 59), so it is not a seed artefact. ➕ **The fix is a guard change, not the grant revocation this row prescribed** — and the reason is measured. The guard was a **disjunction** (`REPORTING:EXPORT or RISK:EXPORT`), so excluding a role means taking *both* from it, and those two authorise every other export: prod's EXPORT ledger is risk-register 12, packet 7, evidence-package 7, ai-model-registry 3, regulator-package 2, cyber-incidents 1, **board-pack 1** — the collateral of the strip is the most-used export and the beneficiary is the least-used one. Seat A's furnish/obstruction objection was the same defect from the other side. So the defect was in the **guard**, mirroring ADR-015 where the guard was right and the grant was wrong. **V332** catalogues `BOARD:EXPORT` and grants it to 8 roles *before* the annotation (F2-23's rule), and both `board-pack.*` handlers moved onto it. **Result: 24 of 59 → 8 of 59 for the pack, with all 24 keeping REPORTING/RISK EXPORT — zero collateral.** ➕ **F2-56 answered in the list, not deferred:** 6 of the 8 grantees are org-1 rows, so `ORG_ADMIN` and `INTERNAL_AUDITOR` (both global) are in the list for reachability as much as for their claim, and the migration **THROWs if the global count is ever 0**. `REGULATOR_VIEWER` deliberately excluded → D-10b. Live-proven: `viewer` still holds **both** old permissions and is now **403**; `ranalyst` is 403 on the pack and **200 on the risk register**; `auditor`/`compliance`/`so_officer`/`admin` 200. 19/0/0 targeted, V332 dev ×2 idempotent | **V332** ✅ |
| 2 | **D-5** | **Board-pack JSON aggregate — immediately after D-10a, and this pairing is the council's second correction.** My proposed order put D-8 between them; Seat C: *"D-10 + D-5 are one story, one probe set, one restart — split them and you buy an extra verification cycle for nothing."* Accepted. ⚠ **ADR-017 D-5's own safety argument named the wrong module** (amendment filed): it justified exposure by pointing at F2-05 slice 5's `ADMIN:READ`(21)/`ADMIN:WRITE`(3) on `board_pack` — but there are **two** board packs, and the live aggregate D-5 exposes is `reporting.BoardPackService` (11 repositories), whose sibling exports carry the wide guard above. So **D-5 before D-10a would replace a *labelled mock* with an *unlabelled leak*** (Seat C). Two build constraints: the JSON endpoint carries the **post-D-10a board-confidential tier**, and it must write the **same export-audit record** as the PDF/PPTX paths — *JSON of an aggregate is an export in substance*. ✅ The second is nearly free: `ExportAuditInterceptor` is registered on `/api/**`, so coverage is automatic — only the `entity_code` extraction needs to name it — and it is **fully free**, verified 2026-07-31: `typeFromPath` already takes the last path segment and strips the extension, so a route named `board-pack.json` audits as `entity_code='board-pack'` with **no code change at all**. ➕ **Two build facts measured 2026-07-31, before starting:** (i) `BoardPackService` exposes **only** `generatePdf(String)` → `byte[]`; there is no aggregate object, and all ~8 `render*` methods query and render in one pass, so the DTO extraction is a rewrite of 554 lines, not a method extraction; (ii) **`BoardPackPptxService` independently re-implements the same aggregate** (32 call sites, 8 shared repositories) — so D-5 must collapse **two** renderers onto one `buildAggregate()`, not wrap one. ⚠ **And it must not enshrine the current ranking, which is unsound — F2-58:** both renderers page 200 rows with **no `Sort`** and then rank in memory, so "Top 20" is the top 20 of an arbitrary sample. Latent today (largest tenant: 51 risks) and silent when it stops being. Rank in the query or the JSON aggregate ships the same defect to a third consumer | M | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-31 @`b99a3d53`** (branch `feat/d-5-board-pack-aggregate` @`b99a3d53`; **no migration** — `BOARD:EXPORT` was catalogued by V332). Both renderers now render `BoardPackAggregateService.build(Scope)`; `GET /api/v1/reporting/board-pack.json` is live on `hasPermission('BOARD','EXPORT')` and takes **no `?lang`** (the aggregate is bilingual — every user-visible name carries `*En` and `*Ar` and the renderer chooses, which is what lets one build serve an Arabic PDF, an English deck and a language-neutral JSON). Absent values are `null`, never the em-dash: the em-dash is a rendering choice and a JSON consumer needs the difference. ➕ **`Scope.DECK` is load-bearing, not tidiness.** The deck omits framework coverage and ESG, and those are the two expensive sections (three queries per active framework; a call into the composed sustainability dashboard). Building them for a consumer that shows neither would have traded the duplication fix for a **latency regression on the PPTX endpoint** — so the collapse is explicit about who needs what. ⚠ **This row's own ‘fully free audit’ verification was wrong, and it is the F2-55 shape again.** I checked that `typeFromPath` strips extensions (true) and reported that the JSON route therefore audits with no code change (false): `ExportAuditInterceptor` **returns early unless the response carries `Content-Disposition: attachment`**, so it never reaches `typeFromPath` for a JSON body. The JSON route would have been the **one board-pack path leaving no audit row at all** — and a DLP/PDPL enquiry that can see two of three egress paths is worse than one that sees none, because it reads as complete. Fixed with `ExportAuditContext.declareExport`, an explicit declaration; marking the response `attachment` would have tripped the interceptor with zero code change and was **rejected as a lie about a response the SPA consumes** (and it would make a browser download the JSON on navigation). *Measuring a component and reporting the system is now the second time on this pack.* ➕ **Free consequence of collapsing:** the board pack now reports **export volume**. Instrumenting it before meant doing it twice, so the platform's most sensitive export was the one reporting `rowCountSource=UNINSTRUMENTED`. The audit ledger records its own transition — earlier rows `UNINSTRUMENTED`, new rows `rows=65 src=BoardPackAggregateService`. ➕ **Equivalence measured before collapsing, not assumed.** The deck picked each KRI's latest reading with a per-KRI finder called **twice per KRI** (80 queries for a 40-KRI tenant) that also filtered `organisation_id`; the PDF used one `findLatestPerKriIn` that does not. Collapsing onto the cheaper one is only safe if the predicate is redundant: **0 measurement rows carry an org that disagrees with their KRI's org**, and **0 KRIs have readings tied on `max(measured_at)`**, so neither version was making an arbitrary choice the other would make differently. ⚠ **`BoardPackGuardTest`'s route-population assertion was pinned at two and failed on the third route** on the first full-suite run — the anti-vacuity floor doing its job: a new way to obtain the board pack cannot be added without a test asking how it is authorised. It matters more here than for the file routes because **`ExportAuthorizationRatchetTest` is structurally blind to this endpoint** (its rule keys on a `byte[]`/`Resource` return type, and generalising to ‘JSON is an export’ is not available — nearly every read endpoint returns JSON). That class is now the route's only cover and says so, asserting the return type so a future conversion to a file stream reconciles the two tests instead of each assuming the other has it. **I also deleted a `BoardPackGuardParityTest` I had just written**, because `BoardPackGuardTest` already asserted the same guard over every board-pack route — the exact duplication my own Javadoc warned against. ➕ **Live: 44 checks, 0 failures.** JSON/PDF/deck agree on every shared figure (aging 2/8/0/28, bands 16/2/2/0, all 6 deck KPIs, all 20 ranked references present in the PDF, the deck's Top 10 exactly the first 10 with **none of ranks 11–20 leaking**); the deck generating at all proves it never touches the two `Scope.DECK`-null sections; `viewer` 403 on all three routes and still **200 on `risk-register.pdf`**, keeping D-10a's no-collateral promise; all three formats wrote an EXPORT audit row; both Arabic renderings generate, the deck using `titleAr` on the 9 of 10 ranked risks that carry one. Suite **2684/0/0**. ➡ **Unblocks F2-42**, which stays a product decision — D-5 built the feed, not the screen | **none** — `BOARD:EXPORT` came with V332 |
| 3 | **D-10b** | **The assurance-mandate mechanism** — a time-boxed, minuted, engagement-scoped grant path that restores what D-10a removes from `EXTERNAL_AUDITOR` and `REGULATOR_VIEWER`. Seat A's line, and it is the right one: Saudi law (BCL art. 18, Capital Market Law inspection, Companies Law auditor access) creates an obligation to **furnish through a governed disclosure channel** — scoped to the authority, time-boxed to the requisition, minuted. It does **not** create a standing self-service credential. `REGULATOR_VIEWER` fails on all three counts today: undifferentiated (one role cannot encode per-authority scope — SAMA-as-AML-supervisor ≠ a CMA securities inspector ≠ an NCA ECC assessor), unscoped in time, and unbound to a requisition | M | 🔁 **RE-SCOPED 2026-07-31, measured before building — the row's premise is wrong in two places and the mechanism it asks for already exists.** ❌ **(i) D-10a did not remove anything from `EXTERNAL_AUDITOR`.** V332 *granted* it `BOARD:EXPORT`, deliberately, because taking it would break furnishing under BCL art. 18 — the controller comment says so. Only `REGULATOR_VIEWER` lost the board pack. There is nothing to “restore” for the external auditor. ❌ **(ii) The mechanism is not missing; it is unnecessary.** All three of the row's requirements are already modelled: **per-authority scope** → `reginspect.reginspect_inspection.regulator` + `scope_en/ar`; **time-boxing** → `entry_date`/`exit_date`/`closed_at`, and `iam.break_glass_events.expires_at`; **minuted + reviewed** → `break_glass_events.reason`/`reviewed_by`/`review_note` with a self-review 409. What does not exist is the **binding**: `incidentRef` is `@NotBlank` free text validated against nothing, and `grep` finds **zero references** between `iam/` and `reginspect/` in either direction. And break-glass's **24-hour ceiling is deliberately incompatible** with an inspection that runs entry→exit over weeks, so it is a sibling of break-glass that is wanted, not break-glass itself. 📊 **What the measurement actually found (dev + prod, identical):** `REGULATOR_VIEWER` holds **11 standing `*:EXPORT` permissions** in dev and 10 in prod — RISK, BCM, CYBER, COMPLIANCE, INCIDENT, SUST, NDI, WORKFLOW, CHANGE, REPORTING, REGULATORY — i.e. a permanent, unscoped, un-time-boxed self-service extract credential for an **external supervisory authority**. D-10a fixed **1 of those 11**. That, not the missing channel, is the defect — filed as **F2-59**. The governed channel is meanwhile **real and in use**: 9 inspections and **38 document requisitions** in both databases, while `break_glass_events` has **0 rows ever**. A governed door built beside a standing open wall is why the door has never been used. ✅ **And the narrowing is free today:** `REGULATOR_VIEWER` and `EXTERNAL_AUDITOR` have **zero holders** in dev *and* prod, so revoking standing bulk extraction costs nobody anything — which is the opposite of D-10a, where measurement is what stopped the revocation. Both roles are also defined **only for organisation 1** (F2-56 again), so tenants 2 and 3 cannot appoint an external auditor at all. ➡ **Re-scoped slice, in this order:** (a) re-home `regulator-package.zip` from its `COMPLIANCE:EXPORT or REPORTING:EXPORT` disjunction onto `REGULATORY:EXPORT` — **37 of 59 roles → 14**, with every assurance persona keeping it (**F2-60**); (b) only then revoke the raw-register EXPORTs from `REGULATOR_VIEWER`, because doing (b) first would strip the governed package too — the exact D-10a trap of killing the good path along with the bad; (c) leave the engagement-scoped grant subsystem **unbuilt** until a tenant actually provisions the persona. Building an access path for a role with zero holders across two databases is building for nobody | V333 |

**What the council got wrong, and how.** Both errors were caught by measuring its claims instead of adopting them —
the same discipline that killed D-11.

- **"The board pack contains named AML alerts and ethics-case detail, which do not belong in a board pack at
  any tier."** **False, and my brief caused it** — I listed "ethics cases, AML alerts" among the contents and
  the seat reasonably read that as itemised. Measured: ethics and AML enter only as `count*` calls
  (`countByOrganisationIdAndCategory` for bribery/kickback/retaliation, `countByOrganisationIdAndStrFiled`).
  It is aggregate MI, exactly as the seat said a board pack should be. **The composition point survives in a
  better-evidenced form** → **F2-55**: the pack carries up to **200 itemised risks** and up to **1,000
  itemised audit findings**, which is an operational extract, not board MI.
- **"Board-pack downloads are unminuted."** **False.** `ExportAuditInterceptor` is registered on `/api/**`
  and writes `action_type='EXPORT'` to `platform.audit_log`; **366 EXPORT events exist, 72 of them
  board-pack**. The control exists and is queryable — which is what made the look-back runnable at all.
  ➕ But running it found a real defect the council could not have predicted → **F2-54**.

**Seat B (exploitability lens) never reported: Fable 5's cyber safeguards blocked the brief.** Second
occurrence on this programme, same cause. Its three questions were answered here by measurement instead —
D-8's direction (it widens), the exception-tier populations (`PRIVACY` 8/59, `IAM` 3/59, so the baseline is
the problem and not the exceptions), and the machine-account comparison (`WORKFLOW:EXPORT` 55/59 including
8 machine accounts is the wider grant, but the board pack is the more sensitive payload — both are in D-10a).

### Wave 1 — the spine (weeks). Everything durable attaches to the module registry.

Six checks, **8/8 mutations caught**: package↔registry parity both ways · homing identical to `SkuRegistry` both ways (the YAML must never become a second authority — that is RC-A, and reproducing it inside the register meant to prevent it would be a poor joke) · schema containment by **reflection** · workspace from the blueprint's closed set · the two authoritative doc sentences · well-formedness incl. duplicate keys. ⚠ **Three of the row's nine fields were REFUTED as specified**: a single `permission prefix` per module is false for 12 of 42 (`platform` names nine codes, `workflow` ten, because the engine guards each caller's own permission), and `owner`/`tier`/`test_floor` are decided for 0, 6 and 0 packages — omitted per §6d rather than stuffed with placeholders. ⚠ **F2-06a's premise is refuted too**: *every @Entity schema == its registry schema* does not hold and should not — eight packages deliberately co-locate (`controlmap`→`evidence`, `incident`→`cyber`, `knowledge`→`kb`, `workflow_submission`→`workflow`, four kernel packages→`platform`), so the enforceable property is containment per package. ⚠ **The prose count-gate the row asks for is NOT implementable and that is a measurement, not a shortcut**: a regex near *module*/*package* matches 138 lines, tightened to unambiguous totals still 69 in 26 files, and nearly all are true sentences (*19 packages have zero tests*, *all 14 modules*). A gate with 69 false positives is disabled in a day — §1c's own rule. Enforced where machine-readable instead. Produced **F2-114** | — |

A floor written against the code as it stood would have **pinned that defect as correct** inside a ratchet.
