### Wave 2 — make the commercial model real *(D-3 answered by ADR-013; ✅ **F2-35 ratified 2026-07-30 by [ADR-018](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-018-SKU-CATALOGUE-RATIFICATION.md)** — 15 SKUs, a non-licensable Base, all 58 packages homed. **Wave 2 is decision-clear**; what remains are the measured prerequisites in each row)*

| ID | Src | Action | Size | Status | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W2-0** | ADR-013 | **Grandfather licence backfill — must land before ANY `@RequireModule` annotation.** Orgs 2/3 hold one licence row each, and the aspect is fail-closed. **Grant-all-then-tighten**, not license-what-has-data (which false-negatives on read-only, empty and seasonal usage → 403s for paying customers). Also fix new-org provisioning: nothing creates licence rows on organisation creation, so post-sweep every new tenant is born blacked out (F2-32) ⚠ **and it cannot be done through the licensing API**: `LicensingController.upsert` overwrites the body's `organisationId` with the caller's tenant, so a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` licenses only the org they are switched into — live-verified, a request naming org 2 returned a row for org 1. **F2-45**, found by F2-22b(i); sequence it immediately before this row, or drive the backfill from a migration. Re-verified 2026-07-26: orgs 2/3 hold exactly one `SOCOPS ENTERPRISE` row each, org 1 holds all 20 `KNOWN_MODULES` | S | ✅ **DONE 2026-08-01, V344** (branch `feat/w2-0-licence-backfill`; suite **2760/0/0**; live **28/28**; `Appendix/scripts/licence_coverage.py` exits **0** on dev and **1** on prod, which is the honest state — prod gets V344 on its next restart). **22 → 57 rows, 35 created**: org 1 needed 7, orgs 2 and 3 needed 14 each. **F2-45 is closed in the same commit and it was not the defect the row described.** The row says a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` “licenses only the org they are switched into” — true, and the sharp end is that the endpoint **answered 200 for the wrong organisation**: a body naming org 2 produced a row for org 1 and said nothing. *A refusal would have been safe; silently succeeding against the wrong tenant is what gets discovered by a customer.* The body is now honoured (licensing is the platform's one legitimate body-supplied tenant per F2-22b), the target organisation must exist — there is **no FK** from `organisation_licenses` to `platform.organisations`, so an unchecked id writes rows nothing will ever read — and `disable` takes the same optional parameter, because **a licence you can grant across tenants and cannot revoke across tenants is worse than one you can do neither to**. ⚠ **The root cause is not what this row assumed.** It says “nothing creates licence rows on organisation creation”. Measured: **nothing creates an organisation.** `OrganisationController` has no POST and **no code anywhere calls `save` on `OrganisationRepository`** — all eight consumers read a name for an export header. The actual cause is one line of V97, which seeded rows with `organisation_id = 1` as a **literal**; every tenant added after it got nothing, and nothing noticed because the only enforced code (`SOCOPS`) was hand-granted to each new org by a later migration. *A seed written for one tenant is a provisioning bug that only surfaces when there is a second tenant.* Filed as **F2-81**. ➕ **And probing this slice found a live defect in the endpoint it was fixing — F2-82, fixed here.** `OrganisationLicense` doubles as the request body and initialised `tier = "PRO"`, `validFrom = now()` and `enabled = true`. `upsert` applies a partial update behind `if (body.getX() != null)`, so a defaulted field is never null and **every partial update silently overwrote all three**: re-enabling one tenant's ESG licence downgraded it ENTERPRISE→PRO (live-verified), a patch to `notes` reset the contract start date to today, and — the sharp one — **any unrelated edit silently re-enabled a module the business had deliberately disabled**, undoing the one commercial control this table carries. ➕ Also shipped: the flat 20-code `KNOWN_MODULES` is split into **`SELLABLE_MODULES` (the 15 ratified)** and **`RETIRED_DESTINATION` (12 codes rows still name)**, and `upsert` now refuses to create a *new* row for a retired code while still allowing an existing one to be wound down. *While the set was flat, “what may be assigned” and “what may be sold” were the same string and could not disagree out loud — which is how F2-35 happened.* ➕ Deliberately **not** added: a cross-tenant licence *read* endpoint. A `PLATFORM_ADMIN` already reads any tenant's licences by switching into it, so a new endpoint would raise the F2-05 role-guard count to buy a round trip. ➡ **Unblocks F2-04**, whose own row already warned that its cited fixture would not survive this — it did not: orgs 2/3 no longer hold `SOCOPS` alone. Three things the ratification hands this row: **(i)** the target is the **15 ratified codes**, not the 20 legacy ones — 7 legacy codes (`EVIDENCE`, `REPORTING`, `AUDIT_LOG`, `PLATFORM_ADMIN`, `CONTROL_LIB`, `POSTURE`, `AUTOMATION`) retire into the non-licensable Base (R4) and must **not** be backfilled; **(ii)** exactly **one** retirement needs a grant, measured not guessed — **`NDI` → `DATA_AI_GOV`, org 1 holds the predecessor and no successor**; the other four (`POLICY`/`REGULATORY`→`COMPLIANCE`, `SCM`→`TPRM`, `INCIDENT`→`SOCOPS`) are safe because org 1 already holds both sides; **(iii)** `SOCOPS` must not change — 22 live `@RequireModule` annotations and the only licence rows orgs 2/3 have. ⚠ **Read F2-56 first**: a SKU a tenant can buy but has no role to operate is not sold | **V344** *(this row said `V332+`; V320 was long since applied and the cell was stale)* |
| **F2-04** | F2-04 | **Entitlement rollout** — extend `KNOWN_MODULES` 20 → 58 from the registry; `@RequireModule` at class level on every business controller; wire the existing `moduleEnabled()` FE helper into navigation + route registration; **licence backfill migration before enforcing**; cross-tenant test. ⚠ **The cited fixture DID NOT survive W2-0, as predicted — V344 landed 2026-08-01:** "orgs 2/3 are already seeded for exactly this" was true (one `SOCOPS` row each in dev *and* prod) and grant-all-then-tighten removed it — **all three organisations now hold all 15 ratified SKUs**, and per F2-32 nothing provisions licences at org creation, so a fresh org is fail-closed on everything. **The test must create its own org + licence rows** | M | ✅ **DONE 2026-08-01 — both halves. (a) server-side enforcement, (b) the frontend gate.** Branches `feat/f204-entitlement-rollout` then `feat/f204b-frontend-entitlement-gate`; suite **2773/0/0**; live probe **14/14** with a control group. **22 → 197 annotations across 252 controllers** (`incident`'s 2 controllers were missed even inside the one wired SKU); 55 Base controllers correctly carry none (R4). ⚠ **The row's first prescription is superseded and must not be followed:** *"extend `KNOWN_MODULES` 20 → 58"* pre-dates ADR-018 — 58 packages do not become 58 codes, they become **15 SKUs + a non-licensable Base**. The map is now data in main code (`SkuRegistry`), not 197 scattered judgements, because F2-01's registry and the FE both need it. ⚠ **What the row does not mention nearly cost a regulator-mandated channel.** Four controllers mix `permitAll` endpoints in with authenticated ones — the **Nazaha/SAMA/CMA whistleblower portal**, the SAMA consumer-complaints portal, the vendor BCP attestation portal and the PDPL Art.12 transparency list. A class-level annotation on any of them would have 403'd an anonymous submitter, because the aspect fails closed on a null tenant. Rule adopted: **an endpoint reachable without authentication cannot be entitlement-checked, because entitlement is a property of a tenant and an anonymous caller has none** — scoped by `SecurityConfig` (which ends `.anyRequest().authenticated()`, so the aspect only ever sees anonymous callers on paths that config declared public) and gated on `RequestContextHolder.getRequestAttributes()` so schedulers keep failing closed. Mutation-proven in both directions. ➕ **Two findings, both from the sweep.** **F2-84 (fixed here):** the entitlement guard **had never once returned 403** — `ModuleDisabledException` leans on `@ResponseStatus`, whose resolver runs *after* `@RestControllerAdvice`, so the catch-all answered **500 `errors/internal`**; in prod SEC-CFG-001 strips the detail, so "you have not bought this module" reached the customer as an outage. The mechanism was already documented three handlers away for `ResponseStatusException` and never generalised. It was unreachable until now: entitlement covered only `socops`, every tenant holds `SOCOPS`, and there are **no socops controller tests**, so the failure path had never executed. **F2-83 (open):** asking whether the anonymous endpoints *could* be entitled exposed that they cannot even identify their tenant — `PUBLIC_PORTAL_DEFAULT_ORG = 1L` in both portal services, and live-verified with 4 organisations present, **6 of 6 anonymous submissions were written to organisation 1**. ➕ Ratchets: `EntitlementCoverageRatchetTest` (6 tests, both directions + 3 anti-vacuity floors) and `ENTITLEMENT_SURFACE_FLOOR` **22 → 197**. <br><br>**—— F2-04b, the frontend gate (2026-08-01) ——** FE suite **160/160** (+75), `tsc` clean, backend **+3**; live-verified in the browser against a real licence revocation. `lib/entitlement.tsx` holds a **path-prefix → SKU** map (77 prefixes) consumed by exactly two callers: `filterNavByEntitlement` in the sidebar and one `ModuleGate` around the router `Outlet`. ⚠ **The row's second prescription needed correcting too.** It says *"wire the existing `moduleEnabled()` FE helper into navigation + route registration"* — but `@Route` gating at that grain means 328 wrappers, one per `<Route>`, each forgettable. The gate is instead **one** component over a prefix map, which is the grain the server already uses (package → SKU, never endpoint → SKU) and cannot be omitted on a new route. ⚠ **The map was measured, not guessed.** Each page's own `/api/..` literals plus those of the named `lib/*` exports it imports were resolved against the backend's `@RequestMapping` + `@RequireModule` pair, so every SKU comes from the annotation that will actually refuse the call. **Six assignments the URL scheme alone gets backwards**: `/rca` and `/incidents` → **SOCOPS** (the sidebar files both under the *Risk* pillar), `/cyber-pdpl-bridge` → **PDPL** (`/api/privacy/cyber-bridge`, not CYBER), `/framework-controls` → **CYBER**, `/resilience-tests` and `/third-party-cyber` → **TPRM**. *A nav pillar is a UX grouping and does not imply a SKU.* ➕ **Two things the derivation itself taught, both recorded in the test file.** A first attempt followed component imports transitively and reported **every** route as calling 11 SKUs — `lib/api.ts` is a 3,658-line barrel holding 579 API literals, so one shared `<EvidencePanel>` handed every page the whole platform's surface; per-named-export attribution fixed it. And **static call-graph analysis was the wrong tool for the last mile**: module dashboards call only Base aggregates, so `/sharia/dashboard` and `/fraud/dashboard` resolve to *no* SKU. Gating on "what the screen is about" rather than "which API it happens to call" is the commercially correct answer, and the derivation informs it without deciding it. ➕ **Fail-open in every direction, deliberately**: unmapped path, list not yet loaded, request failed → shown. A wrong *show* costs a readable 403; a wrong *hide* removes a paid feature with no error anywhere. **No PLATFORM_ADMIN bypass** — checked, not assumed: `LicenseGuardAspect` consults only `isModuleEnabled`, the opposite of `canExport`/`canApprove`, and each is consistent with its own enforcer. ➕ **`publicPaths.ts` — F2-04a's anonymous rule, carried to the UI.** `BARE_PREFIXES` moved out of `AppLayout` so one list answers both "does this route get chrome?" and "may it be licence-gated?". Without it a UI gate would refuse `/ethics/whistleblower/submit` whenever the *host* tenant lacks `ETHICS` — reintroducing the Nazaha/SAMA outage one layer above where any server fix can reach. ➕ **Two defects fixed in passing.** (i) `licensing.ts` **and** `LicensingPage`'s class javadoc both still asserted F2-45's falsified claim — that the backend *"ignores any value sent from the client"* — which was never a safety property but the silent cross-tenant write itself; *a fix applied to one file is not a pattern until it reaches the others that repeat it.* (ii) The licensing screen offered **Enable** on all 27 catalogue codes, but `upsert` refuses to create a row for the 12 retired ones — **a button whose only possible outcome was an error toast, on 12 of 27 rows**; retired codes with no existing row now render *Retired*, keeping `Disable` on any row that exists so it can still be wound down. ➕ **`moduleEnabled()` finally has a caller** — the helper F2-04 named, orphaned for four months. An earlier draft of the provider reimplemented the membership check and left it orphaned, which would have reproduced the finding while appearing to fix it; a test now asserts the call site. The same draft also listened for a `grc:org-switched` event **that nothing dispatches** (the switcher hard-reloads) — caught by checking before wiring. ➕ **Cross-language ratchet**: `FrontendSkuVocabularyTest` reads `RATIFIED_SKUS` out of `licensing.ts` and asserts set equality with `SELLABLE_MODULES`, so the chain now closes **ADR-018 ↔ SELLABLE_MODULES ↔ SkuRegistry ↔ 197 annotations ↔ the FE gate**. A code only in Java is sold but never gated; a code only in TypeScript hides a module from *everyone*, permanently, with no error — the F2-23 shape pointed at revenue. **Live evidence** (org 1, ESG revoked then restored via the real admin endpoint): sidebar **15 → 14 sections** (the *ESG & Climate* header itself gone — an empty group advertises what you are hiding), ESG links **5 → 0**, total nav links **133 → 128**, `/esg` → the *Module not licensed* screen naming `ESG` with the URL preserved, in **both** Arabic and English; `/risk` still rendered *Enterprise Risk* throughout (control group); the licence row restored byte-identical, grandfather note intact. **13/13** API checks. ⚠ The console showed `ReferenceError`s during the work — all stale Vite HMR artifacts from intermediate edit states, proved stale with a sentinel: after a clean reload the buffer held **zero** errors, every one of them ordered *before* the marker | **none** *(this cell said `V320–V321`; both were applied long ago and the sweep needs no schema change — the licence rows it enforces against arrived with V344)* |
| F2-06b | F2-06 | Schema moves, in ownership order: **`integrations` out of `platform` first**, then `controlmap`. *(The `incident`→`cyber` question is **dissolved** — D-4/ADR-013: `incident` joins the Cyber & SOC SKU and the package does not move.)* Compatibility view for one release + rollback recipe | M each | ⬜ | V332+ (see §4) |
| **F2-09** | F2-09 | **Schema-convergence CI job** — Flyway `V1`→head on an empty DB, dump, diff against the Python-bootstrapped dev schema; fail on any un-allow-listed difference | M | ✔ **CI HALF DONE 2026-08-06** — three assertions in `SchemaContractRatchetTest`, **hermetic**: they answer F2-09's question from committed evidence only (the generated `TABLE_DISPOSITION_REGISTER.csv` against the migration folder's `CREATE TABLE`s), so they run in `mvn -o clean package` with no SQL Server — which the DB-building script cannot. 🔑 **Set-based, never count-based, and that is the whole point**: the migrations CREATE **470** distinct tables and the register lists **470** live in dev, *and three differ in each direction*. The cheapest possible version of this gate — comparing the two totals — reads clean, which is exactly how F2-70 read as closed while [[F2-140]] was true. Both populations are **pinned by name**, because a count of three tolerates any three. Third assertion: a residue table may never **acquire a reader** — the toleration (ADR-017 D-2: no DDL) is valid only because no JPA entity and no Java reference touches them, and the day one does, “no migration creates it” becomes F2-146's broken fresh install. ⚠ The plausible tidy-up is treating `rows_dev = 0` as absent; **45 of the 470 live tables are empty** and it moves all of them into the orphan list (mutation-tested: 3 → 48). Emptiness is not absence. **6/6 mutations caught**; suite **3,382/0/0**. ⬜ **Still open**: the column-and-constraint half, which needs the real two-database diff (`Appendix/scripts/schema_convergence.py`, minutes to run) — a static scan cannot compare column definitions or constraint bodies | — |
| F2-13b | F2-13 | Tighten the over-broad grants the Wave-0 report surfaces, starting with `WORKFLOW_WRITE`; per-tenant impact query before each change | M | 🔶 **the B1 `WORKFLOW:WRITE` row DONE 2026-07-29 — and it was a live 403-should-have-been, not a report row.** `WorkflowRequestController#approve` and `#reject` were guarded by `WORKFLOW:WRITE`, **held by 55 of 57 roles**, so VIEWER, REGULATOR_VIEWER, EXTERNAL_AUDITOR, SUPPLIER_USER and all eight `*_INGEST` machine accounts could decide any request — **in the engine 14 modules route their approvals through**, which is what makes this the most permissive approval path on the platform rather than one endpoint. ⬅ **The fix was an inconsistency to remove, not a control to invent:** `WORKFLOW:APPROVE` already existed, was held by 3 roles, and already guarded the same act in **three sibling controllers** (`ApprovalRequestController#transition`, both `ApprovalChainController` decisions, `WorkflowSubmissionController`) — only the request lifecycle used WRITE. Same shape as CM-C2 and SCR-003. **A grant migration was half the fix**, per F2-13a's third transferable result (*enforcing a permission is also a re-grant decision*, and F2-43 is what happens when that is missed): lifting the guard alone would have left approval to 3 roles and 403'd the platform's documented four-eyes flow on the first attempt, because the standard second approver is a CISO and **CISO held no `WORKFLOW:APPROVE`**. **V324** derives the grant from the platform's own data rather than my judgement — every role that already holds *some* module's APPROVE **and** already holds `WORKFLOW:WRITE` → **3 → 17 holders**; decision authority on a workflow request goes **55 → 17**. Round-trip-proven from the measured baseline (revert to 3, apply, apply: 3 → 17 → 17, exact set asserted) rather than only shown idempotent, because the first apply ran against a DB that already held the rows and so could not distinguish "the predicate selects 17" from "17 rows were lying there". Live **12/12**, `WorkflowApproveAuthorityTest` 3/3, suite **2,593/0/0**. ⚠ **The regenerated report caught this fix's own first draft** — the draft used only the already-an-approver predicate, and `Appendix/GRANT_TIGHTNESS.md` came back with a new section-B4 row: **CHANGE_MANAGER · WORKFLOW · APPROVE · READ not held**. CHANGE_MANAGER (created by V317 for change authority) holds *no other* WORKFLOW grant, so it had been handed authority to decide records it cannot list or open — B4's framing is that write-without-read is correct for a *machine*; for a human persona it is a 403 halfway through a flow, offered as a button. Hence the second predicate, which also makes APPROVE holders a strict subset of WRITE holders, and **verification 3 in the migration now throws if any holder lacks the module's READ** — a grant migration is exactly where the half-grant gets created. The narrative moved into `grant_tightness.py` rather than the markdown, since the report is generated and a hand-edit is erased on the next run. ⚠ **`WORKFLOW:WRITE` deliberately stays at 55**, so B1 still lists it and B3 still counts 20: after the split WRITE means *participate* (create/send/respond/submit/resubmit) and a SUPPLIER_USER answering a vendor attestation or an ingest account raising a request needs exactly that — B3's own defence of those grants in B4 is right, and revoking them to tidy a report row would break real flows. `/review` also keeps WRITE (`reviewer_id` and `approver_id` are separate columns — requiring APPROVE would collapse a two-stage gate into one), as do `/close`/`/reopen`/`/escalate`; all ten lifecycle endpoints are pinned by the test so the answer stays chosen rather than drifted into. **Both remaining B1 rows are now worked, and they closed in opposite directions:** `CHANGE:WRITE` 42/57 was a real self-approval hole (**F2-29**), and `AI:WRITE` 41/57 is **not a defect** — its `/review` sets the suggestion's own status and propagates to nothing, verified live, so 41 of 57 is an appropriate width. **Remaining on this row:** section C is now **fully swept** (see **F2-51**) — **13** rows remain and every one is a product decision, not a defect — down from 15, because **`AI:READ` was one of them and its first answered instance came back "the *feature* was missing its guard"** (**F2-50**): two reads were `isAuthenticated()`, so the checkbox 41 roles held did nothing while a `viewer` with no checkbox read the content. Re-ask the other 14 that way — dead permission, or feature missing its guard? the A1/F2-23 failure mode in reverse — rather than revoking them | M | **V324** |
| **F2-14** | F2-14 | **`ControlHealth` SPI** — each control declares its required configuration and reports `IN_FORCE │ NOT_CONFIGURED │ DEGRADED`; Control Assurance dashboard + `/actuator/health`; audit the 8 config-gated candidates (authorisation matrix, freeze windows, SLA policies, quorum policies, workflow gate maps, `ck_ca_source`, evidence-mandatory flags, escalation targets). **Generalises `ChangeGovernanceHealthService` — and is saleable** | M | ⬜ | — |
| F2-22b | F2-22 | DTOs for the highest-risk entity-bound endpoints (approval state, tallies, statuses); ratchet the count down | L | 🔶 **ranked + tranche (i) DONE 2026-07-26; DTO conversion not started.** **Tranche (i) — the backstop covered the wrong half.** Chasing "is `organisationId` binding exploitable?" led to the control that is meant to make it moot, and `TenantWriteGuardInterceptor` had exactly **one** `@Override`: `onFlushDirty`, the *update* hook. `decide()` compares previous-vs-current org, so it fired only when an existing row's tenant *changed*; an **INSERT** has no previous state and `onSave` was never implemented, so a create carrying another tenant's id was never examined — while `application-prod.yml` turns the bean ON and calls it the backstop "so a single missed per-service guard cannot silently write across tenants". The uncovered half is the likelier one: an update inherits its tenant from the row it loaded, a create takes it from the body. Fixed with `onSave` → `decideInsert()` (own-tenant rule, with null-org and off-request thread deliberately exempt), `SecurityException` → **403 `cross-tenant-write`** (it had **no handler**, so the platform's most serious runtime control surfaced as a 500 despite its javadoc claiming 403), `LicensingService.upsert` wrapped in `allowCrossTenantWrite()`, and **the flag turned ON in dev** — it was prod-only, so the control ran only where nobody could watch it, which is why both defects survived. **Mutation-proven:** remove the controller stamp + the opt-in scope, and a cross-tenant licence insert returns 403 having written nothing; restore both and it is a 200. 15 guard tests, live 12/12, suite **2,552/0/0**. ⬅ **The measurement also falsified the scare number:** `organisationId` is bindable on 148 of 214, but **202 of 202** endpoints exposing it sit behind a service that stamps it from `TenantContext` (441 stamp sites), so there is no live cross-tenant write today — the finding is that the mechanism designed to catch the *first miss* could not see it. ➕ Found **F2-45**: `LicensingController` overwrites the body's tenant, so a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` cannot license any org but its own — **that blocks W2-0's grant-all backfill**. **Tranche (ii) DONE 2026-07-26 — and it was a live P1, not a theoretical binding.** `POST /bcm/cycles {"status":"APPROVED","approvedBy":"the_ceo"}` created an approved BCM assessment cycle attributed to a name the caller chose, and since `updateCycle` refuses to edit an APPROVED cycle the forged record was then immutable except by transition. Three creates (`createCycle`, `createGovernance`, BIA `create`) read `if (status == null) DRAFT` and validated against a vocabulary **containing APPROVED**, so they bypassed the `/transition` state machine that owns approval — its chain, its server-side `approvedBy` stamp, its audit row, and for a BIA its gate that `mtpdHours` is set and `RTO ≤ MTPD`. **An approved BIA with no MTPD is the number every BCP, DR plan and NCEMA submission derives from.** Fixed: `approvedBy`/`approvedAt` `READ_ONLY` on **21** entity-bound types (still serialised — asserted, per F2-22a), and the three creates now **reject** a non-initial state naming the field and the transition path rather than silently forcing DRAFT (silent drops are F2-44). The ranking now skips `READ_ONLY` fields so it tracks progress: **CRIT 54 → 28.** Live 18/18 — the real chain still stamps `approvedBy=admin`, the authenticated actor, not the body's `the_ceo`. Suite **2,557/0/0**. ⚠ **Two corrections worth carrying:** a regex under-counted for the **fourth** time in three days (the annotation script matched 7 of 21 entities because it anchored `private` to line-start; 14 write `@Column(...) private String approvedBy;` on one line), and a probe's 400 was mistaken for proof when it came from `validateScope` running *before* the new guard — the `@Valid`-ordering trap again. ➕ **Three DTO-based services accept a caller-supplied approver** (`BoardPackService` sets `approvedBy` from the request in its APPROVED branch, plus `CptyService`, `TreatmentPlanService`) and are **invisible to this ranking because they take DTOs** — which corrects F2-22b's own premise: *converting an endpoint to a DTO does not fix mass assignment if the DTO carries the same field.* **Tranche (iii) DONE 2026-07-28 — decision/verification/signature provenance, and honestly labelled as defence in depth.** `decidedBy`/`decidedByName`/`decidedAt`/`verifiedBy`/`verifiedAt`/`signedAt` are now `READ_ONLY` on the five entities that exposed them (`AmlAlert`, `CrisisDecision`, `Disclosure`, `CorrectiveAction`, `DataSharingAgreement`). ⚠ **Unlike (ii), no live bypass was found** — measurement first: every `setDecidedBy`/`setDecidedAt`/`setSignedAt` in the platform already takes an actor or `now()`, and `CorrectiveActionService` is explicit (create scrubs the verification fields per SEC-ESG-003, update whitelists around them, only `transition()` sets them from the authenticated verifier). What the annotations buy is that the guarantee stops depending on each service remembering — F2-22a's argument for `BaseEntity`. **CRIT 28 → 19** (54 → 28 → 19 cumulative). ➕ **`decidedByName` was missed by the first sweep** because the target list held `decidedBy` and not its display-name companion — and the *name* is the field DF-3 was actually about, since a display name in a decision log is easier to forge convincingly than a user id. ⚠ **`verifiedBy` deliberately left writable on two DTO paths** (`CapaTaskService.transition` on its CLOSED branch, `VulnerabilityService`) — entity annotations do not reach them, which is the standing correction to this row's premise: *a DTO does not fix mass assignment if the DTO carries the same field.* Follow-up list now five: those two plus `BoardPackService`, `CptyService`, `TreatmentPlanService`. **Tranche (iv) DONE 2026-07-28 — derived outcomes, and it found three live bypasses plus a fourth defect underneath them.** The remaining CRIT are `status`/`outcome`/score fields, and the useful question there turned out not to be "is it sensitive" but **does a derivation already exist elsewhere** — a distinction the ranking cannot see, because it is entity-level and blind to a service-side whitelist. Reading all 19 split them cleanly: `ClimateRiskOpportunity.riskScore` recomputes as likelihood × impact on *both* create and update, `ThirdPartyCyberAssessment.riskScore` recomputes on create and on every posture change, and `NdiComplianceAssessment.complianceScore` is excluded from `update`'s whitelist and then derived by `applyRules` — all three **inert**, and deliberately left alone rather than annotated, since annotating them would claim a fix where there was no exposure. Two had a derivation *and* took the caller's number: **(1)** `POST /api/privacy/processor-assessments` returned **201 with `status=APPROVED`, `overallOutcome=PASS`, `riskScore=1.0` and a forged approver while all eight PDPL Art. 28 controls were still NULL** — the F2-22b(ii) BCM defect verbatim, in privacy, and `if (status == null) IN_PROGRESS` let every other status through. **(2)** `POST /api/v1/tprm/assessments` minted a vendor assessment claiming a **REVIEWED 40-of-40 questionnaire scoring 5% with zero rows in `tprm.questionnaire_responses`**, and `PUT` re-asserted `riskScore` over the rubric's — proven by watching `risk_score` go 17.31 → 1.00 while `computed_score_pct` stayed 17.31, so the register and the rubric disagreed with no trace of which was which. **(3)** `approverName` was caller-supplied on `/approve` **and the SPA sent the literal `'admin'`**, with the controller defaulting to `"admin"` when the key was absent — so the Art. 28 register recorded "admin" as the approver of every processor regardless of who clicked. Fixed: the five questionnaire-engine fields and the four processor outcome/attribution fields are `READ_ONLY`; create **rejects** a non-initial status naming `/score`; `/approve` and `/score` stamp the authenticated principal (`assessedBy`/`assessorName` had **no setter anywhere in the platform**, which is why the register's "assessed on <date> (<assessor>)" line always rendered an em dash); and `TprmService` refuses a manual score **only** while a template owns the row — `riskScore` stays free-form for manual assessments, because refusing it outright would be the F2-40 trap. **CRIT 19 → 18** (the two TPRM rows correctly stay: `riskScore` is still bindable *by design*). Live **39/39**, `DerivedOutcomeBindingTest` 5/5. ⚠ **The probe also falsified the fix one layer down:** the engine's own `scoredPoints` "manual override" had no bound, so 5 points on a BOOLEAN question (max 1.00) drove `computedScorePct` to **−400.00**, which `bandFor` duly called LOW. Locking the field is worth nothing if the sanctioned path can drive the same number anywhere — *a derivation is only as trustworthy as its inputs* — so the override is now bounded to `[0, maxPoints]`. ➕ Found **F2-48** (P1, closed): `CapaTaskService#generateExternalId` took `substring(0, 6)` of an 11-digit hex epoch, keeping the **high-order** digits, so the id changed once per 2²⁰ ms ≈ **17.5 minutes** and every CAPA in a window collided on `uk_capa_external`. Amplified by all three `TprmAutomationService` hooks being plain `@Transactional`: their `catch { log.warn }` could not un-mark a **shared** rollback-only transaction, so the *caller* died with `UnexpectedRollbackException` → 503. Net effect, measured: a processor assessment **could not be recorded as failing at all**, and no auto-CAPA hook in the platform had ever created a row. Now 6/6 succeed and 10 CAPAs were actually created. **Tranche (v) DONE 2026-07-28 — and it exhausts the CRIT tier. The result is the census, not the fix.** All 18 remaining CRIT rows were read service-by-service, and **exactly one was a real bypass**: `EthicsCaseService.apply` copied `status` *and* `outcome` straight from the body on create and update, so `POST /api/v1/ethics/cases {"status":"CLOSED","outcome":"UNSUBSTANTIATED"}` filed a **whistleblower report already investigated and dismissed**, and a PUT moved any case anywhere. Skipping the chain is the lesser half — `transition()` is the only thing that stamps `triagedAt`/`investigationStarted`/`investigationEnded`/`closureDate`/`regulatorNotified`, so a body-closed case carries a verdict with **no triage date, no investigation window and no closure date**, which on a whistleblowing register is indistinguishable from a report quietly made to go away. **CM-C3 had already fixed exactly this on `ChangeRequest#apply`, comment and all** — nothing generalised it. The other 17 verdicts: **inert** — Change create/update (CM-C3's whitelist excludes `status`; `outcome` not copied either), `NdiComplianceAssessment#update` (whitelist + `applyRules`), Climate + TP-cyber (recompute on every write); **by design** — Cyber `maturityLevel` ×4 (an assessor's rating; no service derives it and the SPA posts it), IamJmlEvent ×2 (a JML *ingestion* record whose `outcome` the source system reports — the service reacts to it via a prior-state snapshot), NdiGap ×2 (`status` is cooperative: the CAPA roll-up advances it and explicitly respects an operator's `CLOSED`/`ACCEPTED_RISK`), CommitteeResolution (F2-40 settled it), TPRM ×2 (`riskScore` free-form, tranche (iv)). Fixed with `requireLifecycleIsNotBodySet`: create rejects a non-initial status, update rejects a **changed** one naming the current state + `/transition` + the legal targets — but **tolerates an unchanged echo**, because `EthicsPage`'s edit modal seeds from `reqFromDto(c) = {...c}` and PUTs the case's own status back, so refusing any present value would 400 every metadata edit past INTAKE (*the UI is what falsifies a "just refuse the caller's value" fix* — tranche (ii)'s standing lesson, and this time it changed the design). Live **26/26** as `compliance`, `EthicsCaseServiceTest` 24/24, suite **2,586/0/0**. ⚠ **A documented tested behaviour was deliberately changed:** `create_respects_explicit_status_severity_intake` asserted a create could supply `status="TRIAGE"` (a case "arriving already triaged") — real intent, but it produced TRIAGE with no `triagedAt`. The flow survives as create-then-transition with *complete* data; the test is rewritten to state the change, not deleted. ⚠ **Needs the user: 11 of 30 dev cases are stuck.** Both seeders wrote `INVESTIGATING`/`TRIAGED`, which are **not in the state machine's vocabulary**, so every transition from them was *already* refused — the PUT bypass was the only thing repairing them, and it is now closed. Seeders corrected here (ethics rows only; the fraud module's own `INVESTIGATING` untouched); the existing rows need one normalising UPDATE, which is a data mutation and so the user's call. There is also **no CHECK constraint on `ethics.cases.status`**, which is why the drift was storable. **Tranche (vi) DONE 2026-07-28 — the five DTO paths, which closes the recorded follow-up list.** These are the paths that *correct this row's own premise*: converting an endpoint to a DTO does not fix mass assignment if the DTO carries the same field, and because the request type is a dedicated `TransitionRequest` the ranking cannot see them at all. **Eight fields across four services**, all on `/transition`: `CapaTaskService` `verifiedBy`; `VulnerabilityService` `verifiedBy` + **`riskAcceptedBy`** + **`triagedBy`**; `BoardPackService` `approvedBy` + **`reviewedBy`**; `TreatmentPlanService` `approvedBy`. **Three of the eight were not on the recorded list** — the same companion-field miss as `decidedByName` in (iii) and `approverName` in (iv), three tranches running: *a hand-written list of field names is always short.* ⬅ **The finding was two-sided, and the second side is what made the fix safe:** not just forgeable but **empty in practice** — no UI sends any of them (`cyberLifecycle.ts` mirrors `{ status }` only; `riskLifecycle.transitionPlan` passes `by` as `undefined` from every caller), so **2 of 9** APPROVED/PUBLISHED board packs named an approver and the single APPROVED treatment plan named none. `TreatmentPlanService` was worse than forgeable: it assigned **unconditionally**, so the omitted field wrote `NULL` *over* the approver. All eight now come from `CurrentActor.username()` (the existing shared helper — no new lookup invented), each service **refuses** rather than recording an anonymous decision, the fields are **removed from the request DTOs** so the contract stops advertising what it ignores, and the CAPA/treatment audit messages now read the actor off the **saved entity** instead of echoing the caller's claim. Live **15/15** with records created by `admin` and transitioned by a different persona (`compliance`, or `so_officer` for cyber — the only other `CYBER:WRITE` holder), every body carrying `"the_ceo_who_never_saw_this"`. ⚠ **`CptyService` was deliberately NOT changed** — it has `approvedByUserId` on credit ratings and SAMA large-exposure limits but **no approve endpoint**, so the field records an *offline* credit-committee approval as data; forcing the actor would make the clerk entering the limit appear to have approved it (the F2-40 trap). Correct treatment is an F2-40-style provenance label — recorded, not fixed. The tell that settled it: `CptyService` stamps `assignedByUserId` from the actor on the line immediately above. ➕ **F2-48 turned out to be systemic, found by this slice's own probe:** the truncated id generator was copy-pasted at **nine** sites, not one — approval requests (the approvals kernel), audit engagements, audit findings, **BCM crisis invocations**, cyber vulnerabilities, KB articles, **PDPL DSR cases** (a statutory clock), regulatory changes and CAPA. Seven have the unique index and so were all capped at one new record per org per ~17.5 min. Now one shared `common/util/ExternalIds`. **Tranche (vii) DONE 2026-07-28 — the last ranked tier, and it retires the ranking as a progress metric.** **`KycService.recordHit` saved the body wholesale**, so an OFAC/PEP screening hit could arrive already carrying `decidedAt`/`decidedByName`/`dispositionNotes` **and `sarFiled`+`sarReference` — a claim that a Suspicious Activity Report was filed with the regulator**. `/hits/{id}/disposition` also read `decidedBy` **from the body** (DF-3, on the register where "who cleared this match" is the whole audit question). On the privacy side `DpiaService.addReview` took `reviewerId`/`reviewerName`/`decidedAt` from the body — and since there is **no separate decide endpoint**, `addReview` *is* the review, so the actor is the reviewer by definition; `addMitigation` took `verifiedAt`. Fixed: the decision block is scrubbed on create (`sarFiled` forced false), `disposition`/`addReview` record `CurrentActor.username()` and refuse when unauthenticated, `verifiedAt` is stamped only when the status is VERIFIED. ⚠ **`disposition` (the classification) stays caller-set on purpose** — the screening feed classifies the match and `recordHit` *reacts* to `TRUE_MATCH` by flagging the subject sanctioned/PROHIBITED; refusing it is the F2-40 trap. **By design, unchanged:** `BiaRecord.status` ×2 (the legacy flat record — the wizard is `BcmBiaAssessment`, gated in (ii)) and `ChangeTask.status` ×2 (a checklist toggle whose `completedAt` is already derived on DONE); both verdicts are **asserted** in the probe, not just claimed. Live **17/17**, suite **2,586/0/0**. ⬅ **The correction that matters most: the ratchet's tier count was never a progress metric, and I had been quoting it as one.** It measures what *Jackson binds*, not what the *service writes* — so tranches (ii)–(iv) (`READ_ONLY`) moved CRIT 54 → 18 while (v)–(vii) (service-side whitelist / scrub / reject) moved it **not at all**; `EthicsCaseController` still reads CRIT with its bypass closed. Rather than re-derive the census next time, it is now **durable and enforced**: `MassAssignmentRatchetTest#ADJUDICATED` records the verdict for all 18 CRIT endpoints (**4 CLOSED · 5 INERT · 9 BY DESIGN**), the emitted `target/mass-assignment-risk.txt` carries it as a column under a header explaining why the count lies, and **a new unadjudicated CRIT row now fails the build** — so the next entity-bound write gets read rather than annotated blind. ➕ Recorded for later: **`NdiGap.riskScore` is stale by construction** — nothing ever computes *or* updates it (`updateGap`'s whitelist omits it), so a gap's score is frozen at create even as `severity`/`likelihood` change. Not a bypass; a dead number in a register. *(**F2-22b's ranked work is complete** — every CRIT/HIGH/STATE endpoint carries a recorded verdict. What remains on this row is optional DTO conversion, which the census shows is unnecessary for 14 of the 18.)* "Highest-risk" is now measured, not guessed: `MassAssignmentRatchetTest#riskProfileIsRecordedForF2_22b` emits `target/mass-assignment-risk.txt` by reflection over each body type's *own* declared fields (F2-22a's nine `BaseEntity` fields excluded, since they no longer bind). Of the 214: **54 CRIT** (tenant **and** an authority/outcome field), **3 HIGH**, **148 TENANT**, **4 STATE**, 5 LOW. ⚠ **The dominant exposure is `organisationId` on 148 of 214** — it is declared per-entity and `BaseEntity` never carried it, so F2-22a did not cover it; 441 `setOrganisationId(` call sites overwrite it from `TenantContext`, which is why nothing has broken, but "every service does" is unverified. **Start the conversion at CRIT, and check the UI first every time** — the ranking cannot tell a *designed* assertion from an unguarded one, which is exactly the trap F2-40 walked into (`CommitteeResolutionController#create` ranks CRIT on `voteFor`/`outcome`, and those fields are the minute-taking form working as intended; the fix was provenance, not a DTO). ➕ First finding out of the ranking: **F2-40**, closed the same day | L | — |

### Wave 3 — Change Enablement as a first-class module *(see **§6c** for the target structure)*

**Elevated 2026-07-25 at the owner's direction:** Change Enablement becomes a properly structured module of
its own — change record + lifecycle + authority + **CAB** + **committee integration** + **workflow** — and is
lifted out of the Risk grouping in the UI. §6c specifies the structure; this table sequences it.

| Stage | Src | Action | Gate | Status | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **CE-0** | new | **Lift Change out of the Risk nav group** — `Sidebar.tsx:106` placed `/changes` inside `// 1. Risk Management (CRO)`. Promoted to its own `navChange` group. **This is the whole of "not under risk"** — the backend was never under risk (§6c) | none | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-29** (no migration). New group `change` / `navChange` = **Change Enablement** sits between Risk and Compliance with the two entries that resolve **today**: the register (`/changes`) and **Calendar & freeze windows** (`/changes?tab=governance`). The CAB queue and decision register are deliberately absent until S7/CE-13 give them routes — nav-to-nowhere is the F2-02 defect two slices of this programme just removed. **Measurement changed the shape of the job twice.** (1) There is only **one** change route: register, kanban, workflow, analytics, integrations, governance and comms are all *tabs* of `ChangesPage`, which already honours `?tab=`; so "calendar/FSC as a nav entry" is a query link, not a new screen — and the codebase already had the pattern (the NDI group ships 7 query-tab children). (2) That pattern existed only for **children**: `NavLink` ignores the query string, so two top-level siblings on the same path both highlighted. `queryAwareChildActive` was generalised to `queryAwareActive` and applied to rows of either level, gated on a sibling set actually mixing query and bare paths, so **no existing group changes behaviour**. Browser-verified in both languages: on `/changes` the register row is active and the calendar row is not; on `/changes?tab=governance` exactly the reverse, with the page's Governance tab open on its live approval matrix; the Risk group no longer lists `/changes` (8 rows, all risk); clean console; `dir` flips rtl↔ltr. ⚠ **`UX_REDESIGN_BLUEPRINT.md` amended in the same commit and now held there by a test** — it filed `change_mgmt` under workspace 12 *Governance & Administration*, and since Wave 4 **generates** the sidebar from that table, a stale row would have silently deleted this group and re-filed Change under an admin catch-all. `navChange` is now workspace 13. `navChangeGroup.test.ts` (4 tests, both directions **mutation-proven**: restoring the Risk row fails, reverting the blueprint fails) + `routeReachability` still 4/4, FE suite **71/71**, `tsc` clean, `vite build` clean |

A snapshot is also the right semantics — an agenda records what was tabled *under the title it was tabled under*. **No backfill, by design**: the queue read requires the snapshot, which is what keeps both orphans off the agenda **for a stated reason rather than by luck**. `subject` is a required parameter rather than an overload, so a caller cannot silently produce an invisible item. ⚠ **S5a's projection ratchet caught the one thing I forgot** — three new entity fields not carried by `ReferralView`, which would have vanished between the engine and the frontend. **And the two columns S6 deferred to "S7's agenda build, their only consumer" are refuted, not built**: `CommitteeReferral.committee_id` is `NOT NULL` so the referral *is* the assignment and `scope_json` would be a second home for it; and `CommitteeMeeting` has **no publication act at all**, so `notice_period_hours` has no event to measure from — both pinned, with the test failing if a publication field ever appears. Produced **F2-101** (the matrix `gate` column is advisory — 8 changes in each DB authorised with a CAB/ECAB gate and no committee; enforcement left as an owner decision because it would refuse that path) and **F2-102** (a referral outlives its record). | **S7e** | CE-5 *(the conditions register)* | **Approve-with-conditions becomes a recorded, enforced obligation.** G-5 of the uplift plan reads *"no DEFER / MORE_INFO / **approve-with-conditions**; conditions not recorded or enforced before implementation"*. S7c closed the first half; this closes the rest, and the measurement is **total absence** — zero tables named `%condition%` platform-wide, zero conditions concept in `change_mgmt/**` or `admin/committee/**`, in both databases. So a CAB approving a change *"subject to the DR rehearsal being re-run"* had one place to put that: `cab_decision_notes`, free text nothing reads — and the change then proceeded to `IMPLEMENTING` with the obligation unrecorded, unowned and unchecked | S7a–S7d | ✅ **DONE 2026-08-04** (**V352**, `change_mgmt.change_condition`, shipping **empty**). ⚠ **Three decisions, each pinned by a test because each is the kind a later tidy-up reverses.** (1) **A new table, not `change_tasks` — and the data settles it, not the taxonomy:** tasks are the implementation runbook (41 rows in each database — *"Cut DNS to KSA region"*, *"Promote KSA replica"*), and **24 of 41 in dev / 23 of 41 in prod are `PENDING`**, correctly, because a runbook step is *supposed* to be pending when implementation begins. A guard over `change_tasks` would refuse every change the platform holds, forever. Conditions also need a **waiver** (an approval act with an author and a reason) and a closing author distinct from the owner — the seam S8's evidence requirement grows into. (2) **The parent is the change, not a decision row:** the plan names the table `change_decision_condition` and hangs it off *"the effective decision"* — **there is no `ChangeDecision` entity**, S7c shipped the write-back as columns on `change_requests`, so a decision row created solely to own conditions would be a second home for facts the change already carries. Same trap CE-7 walked into naming `ck_ca_source`: the spec's parent did not exist. (3) **No `CONDITIONAL` status or outcome:** an approve-with-conditions change *is* `CAB_APPROVED`, and conditionality is **derived** from carrying conditions — a literal stored beside the register can disagree with it (a `CONDITIONAL` row whose conditions were never entered reads conditional with nothing to satisfy), and V352's self-check **THROWs** if `ck_change_status` ever names it. **The gate ships with the register, not with S8:** the plan lists `ConditionsSatisfiedGuard` under S8 and S8's version adds a real requirement (the satisfaction must carry *verified* evidence), but a register nothing consults is the F2-62/F2-95/F2-89 shape this programme has spent five slices closing — so `SCHEDULED → IMPLEMENTING` is a **409 naming the outstanding obligations** from today, and S8 strengthens what counts as satisfying one. Measured safe: zero conditions exist, so it can refuse no change that exists, which is also why V352 refuses to let the table ship seeded. **The authorisation split is F2-29's classification applied to a new surface rather than discovered on it later:** imposing and **waiving** demand `CHANGE:APPROVE` (adding an obligation as the price of approval, and forgiving one, are approval acts); recording one as **satisfied** needs only `CHANGE:WRITE` (doing the work is operational). A waiver additionally needs a reason (`ck_cc_waiver_reason` too) and **may not come from the change's requester or owner** — resolved on the user id first, name only as the legacy fallback (DF-4/F2-24). ⚠ **`MassAssignmentRatchetTest` rejected the first draft by name** — it bound `ChangeCondition` from the request body and scrubbed it in the service; the fix is `ChangeConditionRequest`, because *a scrub is a list of fields somebody remembered and a DTO is the set of fields that exist*. Three other ratchets fired and were satisfied properly, never by raising a baseline: the table register (regenerated), the entity count and the FE page count. Suite **2,976 → 3,000/0/0**, IT **8/8**, FE **214 → 219/219**, live **43/43** with three real personas, and browser-verified end to end in Arabic: two open conditions, the UI's own advance button **409**, both satisfied from the card, the badge flipping to *جميع الشروط مغلقة*, and the change then reaching `IMPLEMENTING` | **V352** |
| **S7f** | CE-14a | **The meeting's own records — an agenda pack and minutes, and neither may launder an assertion into evidence.** G-14: *"No agenda pack / minutes / decision-register exports for change governance."* The CAB pack (CH-11) is a pack for one **change**; the committee module had **no export at all** beyond a blank form template — while holding **34 meetings in dev and 32 in prod**, 16/15 of them `MINUTES_APPROVED` with real agenda and decision content. A secretary could neither circulate an agenda nor file a minute | S7a–S7e | ✅ **DONE 2026-08-04** (no migration). Two endpoints on the existing export surface — `/committee/meeting/{id}/agenda.pdf` and `/minutes.pdf`, RTL + bilingual through the same Amiri generators. ⚠ **The provenance is the substance of the slice, not a flourish.** An export is where a governance record becomes evidence, and measured across both databases: `quorum_source` is **15 NULL / 17 ASSERTED_LEGACY / 2 COMPUTED** in dev and **0 COMPUTED** in prod; `quorum_present` is populated on **2 of 34** dev rows and **0 of 32** prod; `tally_source` is **20 ASSERTED_LEGACY / 2 COMPUTED**; and `committee_resolution_vote` holds **4 rows in dev, 0 in prod**. So a minutes PDF printing *"Quorum: 4 of 7, required 4"* would **invent numbers for 32 of 34 meetings**, and *"For 0 · Against 0"* would read as a unanimous abstention that never happened — both the F2-42 shape. F2-28 added `quorum_source` so the difference is *sayable*; this export says it, per meeting and per resolution, and renders the figures **only where they were recorded**. Everything absent is stated in words rather than left blank (*"the agenda has not been built yet"*, *"No attendance was recorded"*, *"No votes recorded"*), an unapproved minute marks itself **DRAFT** on the page and not only in the KPI band (F2-98's lesson), a recorded **dissent** is carried through rather than collapsed into the tally table, and a malformed `*_json` snapshot degrades its own section instead of 500-ing the document. **Guarded `ADMIN:EXPORT` and `ADMIN:READ`, not CHANGE** — the committee engine is generic (19 committees in dev, of which the CAB and ECAB are two) and its screens already carry `hasPermission('ADMIN', …)`; measured before choosing, `ADMIN:EXPORT` is 7 roles, `ADMIN:READ` is 21, and **no role holds EXPORT without READ in either database**, so F2-61(c)'s composed-guard invariant loses nobody (exports composing a READ: 36 → **38**, uncomposed still **0**). Suite **3,000 → 3,012/0/0**, IT **8/8**, FE **219/219**, live **37/37** — and the probe parses every PDF with **pypdf** and asserts the extracted text in both languages, because a 200 with a branded cover and an empty body is exactly what this slice exists to avoid. ⚠ **Its own first run taught the Arabic lesson:** the generators *shape* Arabic, so pypdf extracts Presentation Forms-B and a search for plain `جدول` fails against a perfect document — the probe now NFKC-normalises, which makes the assertion prove the text is both present **and** shaped | — |

| **S8a** | CE-9 | **A change carries evidence — hashed, versioned, independently reviewed — and a condition may only be recorded satisfied when that evidence is APPROVED.** G-9, the only **Critical** left in S8: *"No evidence/attachment integration at all — test results, approvals, back-out proof are free text; ISO 20000/9001 documented-information not satisfiable."* Measured 2026-08-04: the subsystem is not missing, it is **thriving**, and change is the one governance domain that never joined it — `evidence.evidence_items` **78 live** rows (29 APPROVED / 29 EXPIRED / 15 PENDING_REVIEW / 4 DRAFT / 1 REJECTED — identical in both databases), `evidence_versions` 60, `evidence_reviews` 19, and `evidence_links` **21** rows across **eight** modules (RISK, SUSTAINABILITY, PRIVACY, COMPLIANCE, AUDIT, CYBER, INCIDENT, BCM) — while `rollback_plan` and `test_plan` sat on `change_requests` as `NVARCHAR(MAX)` prose. An auditor asking "show me the back-out proof for CHG-2025-0002" got a paragraph somebody typed | S7 | ✅ **DONE 2026-08-04** (**V353**, `CK_ev_links_module` **9 → 10**). Fourth ADR-012 port (`EvidencePort` + `EvidenceAdapter`), two verbs, four endpoints (`CHANGE:READ` to read, `CHANGE:WRITE` to attach — **not** APPROVE: attaching an artefact is clerical, and what makes it count is the subsystem's own review). ⚠ **Three decisions worth recording.** (1) **Not `change_mgmt.change_links`**, which already exists and whose frontend vocabulary even offers an `EVIDENCE` entity type — using it would have been the mistake, because it is an un-hashed, un-reviewed, un-versioned pointer with no expiry, *precisely the deficiency G-9 describes*. Evidence lives in the evidence subsystem or the word means nothing. (2) **The migration exists because the store demanded it, not because the slice did** — the same question S7d answered the other way: `capa.tasks.origin_type` has no CHECK, `evidence_links.linked_module` is a closed CHECK of 9, so joining genuinely required widening it. Ask per store, not per slice. (3) **The satisfaction gate fails CLOSED while every read fails open** — `requireVerifiedEvidence` treats an absent port as *cannot verify* and refuses, because a guard that passes when it cannot see the evidence is **F2-95's defect verbatim**; the escape hatch is the auditable one that already existed (an independent `CHANGE:APPROVE` holder may waive with a stated reason, and waiving is deliberately untouched by the gate — otherwise a deployment without the evidence module could neither satisfy nor waive, and `IMPLEMENTING` would be unreachable for every conditional approval). `verified` is **APPROVED and nothing else**: counting PENDING_REVIEW would let evidence somebody merely uploaded discharge a CAB obligation, counting EXPIRED would let evidence that has aged out do it — and EXPIRED is **29 of the 78** live items, so neither is hypothetical. ⚠ **My own census was wrong first time and is corrected in the commit**: it reported 104 items / 34 APPROVED because a bare `COUNT(*)` swept in 25 **soft-deleted** rows. Filtering `is_deleted` in native SQL is landmine #6 on this platform's own list, and a census is native SQL. The corrected figures make the argument stronger, not weaker. **F2-106 (P2), found by writing the adapter over the peer:** `EvidenceItemService.addLink` validates `linkedModule` with `@NotBlank` only (an unknown value is a **500** from the CHECK — the `ck_ca_source` landmine in a second place) and never checks that `evidenceId` exists or belongs to the tenant, so a link row pointing at another tenant's item persists. S8a defends the change side and regression-tests it; the peer's endpoint is **open**. Measured safe: **zero** conditions and **zero** CHANGE links existed in either database, so no recorded satisfaction is invalidated. The honest denominator is changes that reached a terminal status: **66.7%** dev, **50.0%** prod. The probe proves it by computing the old figure alongside the new. ⚠ **And my own first drafts made the same mistake twice in the same hour** — "0 conditions overdue" over 0 conditions and "0 terms expiring" over an empty roster, both clean bills of health for registers nobody had written to; both caught by the live probe, not by review, and both now `NO_DATA`. That is F2-95's `advisory` javadoc again — the word in the comment **was** the finding. All four gated claims were **mutation-proven**: renaming a state, changing the published KPI count, quoting the retracted bypass sentence and renaming an endpoint each fail the build. ⚠ **The endpoint check's first version could not fail** — it let any route placeholder absorb any cited literal, so `governance-health` → `governance-healthz` passed; a declared `PATH_PARAMETER_VALUES` list gave it teeth. ⚠ **The test's first run caught the documentation quoting its own defect**: the contract reproduced the "bypasses CAB" sentence verbatim to explain what had been wrong, and a skimmer cannot tell a quoted retraction from a current claim — it now lives in the test's javadoc instead. ⚠ **And reconciling the CE plan's §0 status table was itself a finding**: all **seventeen** rows read NOT STARTED while **eleven** epics had shipped, in the table whose header calls it the *"single source of truth for progress"*; its `V322–V331` reservation was void too (delivery landed on V345, V348–V353). **The live probe is of the documentation, not the code** — 57 legs, each citing the section it verifies: the fail-closed refusal fires and names the empty roster in the exact words the runbook prints, the freeze gate refuses and the conflict report does not, the export guard is EXPORT∧READ (viewer 403), the 9 `CHANGE:APPROVE` roles the runbook publishes are the 9 in the database, and 7 KPIs report `NOT_MEASURABLE` with a blocker. ⚠ **My own first probe was the S8b mistake a third time**: it asserted "an unconstituted CAB refuses the approval" against `TOP 1 status='CAB_PENDING'` — change 4, `STANDARD`/`LOW`, whose matrix cell is **AUTO/min 0** and which correctly needs no committee at all. | **S8d** | CE-14b | **The decision register — the last of G-14's three artefacts, and the sources the plan named do not exist.** §9.3 sources it from `change_decision` and `change_decision_condition`; S7c wrote the decision back as **columns on `change_requests`** and S7e hung conditions off the change, so the row shape had to be measured rather than transcribed. ⚠ **The measurement is the design.** In dev, **11** changes have passed a decision point and **2** carry a decision timestamp, **1** a decision author; there are **31** legacy votes across 12 changes and **4** engine resolution votes (3 FOR, 1 RECUSED). In the deployed database the author columns **do not exist yet** (V345+ pending), so 10 rows carry 1 timestamp and no author at all. A register printing one row per *stamped* decision would hide ten real governance events; one printing eleven attributed rows would **invent ten decision authors** | S8c | ✅ **DONE 2026-08-04** (**no migration**). Two axes per row, arrived at from a third measurement rather than by analogy: **`basis`** — `RECORDED` (the change carries the act) vs `INFERRED_FROM_STATE` (a change in `IMPLEMENTING` cannot have got there without an approval, so the state *proves* one happened without saying when or by whom) — and **`evidence`** — `RESOLUTION` (the system of record, printed with its code, outcome, tally **and the tally's source**), `RECORDED_VOTES` (the legacy tally with named approvers), or `NONE`, which is **correct** for an `AUTO`-gated change because no committee was ever asked. The header publishes how many rows are attributed, since *"12 decisions"* alone invites a reader to count twelve recorded acts — F2-107's shape in table form. Unattributed cells print **"not recorded"**: in a decision register a blank reads as *no objection*. ⚠ **Deferrals and MORE_INFO are in the register**, pinned by a test — S7c exists because a committee that defers had nowhere to say so, and dropping those rows would reopen the same hole from the reporting end. ⚠ **Homed in `module/reporting`, not `change_mgmt`**: the register joins change rows to committee referrals, resolutions and votes, so building it inside the module would need either a new `CommitteePort` method or more boundary-crossing native SQL (F2-108) — reporting already imports both sides, which is where S7f put the committee generators for the same reason. Produced **F2-109**: **nothing generates `change_code`** — the only `setChangeCode` in `module/` is a merge of a client-supplied value — so every change created through the API is unciteable in every export, and both databases look clean only because their rows were seeded with codes. The register mitigates with a `#<id>` fallback; the generator needs an owner decision about backfill. **A new invariant closes the loop the other way**: `ChangeDocumentationFreshnessTest.everyChangeExportIsDocumented` fails on a `CHANGE:EXPORT` handler no document mentions — mutation-proven, along with the existing dangling-path check. ⚠ **And the probe's audit leg reported a FAILURE against a working audit trail**: it counted rows the instant `urlopen` returned, and `ExportAuditInterceptor` writes in `afterCompletion`, i.e. after the response is committed — the client always wins that race. Bounded polling, not a removed assertion. | **S8e** | CE-2 | **"Pre-approved" was a word anyone could type.** G-2: *"No change model catalogue — STANDARD is an unbacked string, so 'pre-approved' is a claim with no record."* Measured 2026-08-04 in both databases: **0** tables that could serve as a change-model catalogue (13 hits platform-wide, every one another module — `ai_governance.ml_model`, `bcm.plan_templates`, `tprm.questionnaire_templates`…), **5** STANDARD changes, **5** of them on the `AUTO` cell with `min_approvals = 0`, and **0** ever referred to a committee. `ck_change_type` makes `STANDARD` representable to any caller, so a change was pre-approved because somebody typed two words — and the CE-16 probe walked exactly that path end to end: create STANDARD/LOW, advance to CLOSED, zero committee involvement, entirely per the matrix, entirely unbacked. `category` (8 uncontrolled values) is a label, not a catalogue | S8d | ✅ **DONE 2026-08-04** (**V354**, `change_model` + `change_model_usage`, both shipping **empty**). ⚠ **The migration exists for one constraint.** `ck_cmodel_preapproval` makes `pre_approved = 1` unrepresentable unless the row is APPROVED and names **who** authorised it, **when**, and **until when** — the `ck_change_ratification_clock` pattern, and V354 does not merely assert the constraint exists: **self-check 4 inserts a bad row inside a rolled-back transaction and THROWs if the constraint accepted it**, because a CHECK that is present but disabled is a comment. ⚠ **An expiry is the difference between a control and a label.** A pre-approval that never comes due is F2-62's shape, so `validForDays` is mandatory and **bounded 30–730** — "what stops a tenant choosing 3,650 days?" is the same examiner question ADR-017 D-3 asked of the ratification SLA. `isLivePreApproval` is APPROVED **and** flagged **and** unexpired, and nothing reads the flag alone; counting an expired one is the mistake S8a refused for EXPIRED evidence, which is 29 of the 78 live items. ⚠ **Three acts, not one**: authoring is `CHANGE:WRITE` (a DRAFT), granting the pre-approval is `CHANGE:APPROVE` **and may not be the author**, applying is `CHANGE:WRITE`. And the request DTO is what makes the CHECK sufficient — it carries no `status`, no `preApproved` and none of the four `authorised*` fields, because the constraint checks that they *agree*, not who set them, so a DTO that carried them would let a WRITE holder self-issue a pre-approval past a green CHECK. ⚠ **The enforcement flag arrives INERT and that is measured, not claimed**: `change_authorisation_matrix.requires_model` is per tenant AND per cell, defaults to 0, and V354 THROWs if any row already reads 1 — 5 STANDARD changes exist in each database and **3 are in flight**, so a default-on guard would refuse work already under way (S6c's `quorum_requires_chair` reasoning, where default-on would have refused 17 of 18 quorate meetings). What IS strict from day one is applying a model that is not APPROVED or has expired — zero models exist, so it can refuse nothing that exists (S7e's pattern). **Deviation breaks the backing**: `change_model_usage` carries `deviation_notes`, and a change that cited a procedure then departed from it is not model-backed, which is the difference between a catalogue that documents practice and one that launders it. Four ratchets fired and were satisfied properly, never by raising a baseline: migration count/head, entity files **and** classes, the deployed-pending arithmetic, and the table register (regenerated — 470 rows). Only then delete `mockChanges.ts` | S7 | ✅ **DONE 2026-08-05** (no migration). **The row asked for three screens that already existed.** Agenda builder, voting panel and minutes are all in the **committee** workspace — because under ADR-017 D-2 the CAB *is* a committee, named per tenant by `admin.committee_gate_map` (dev: CAB → committee 13 *IT Steering*, ECAB → 12 *Executive*), and S6 measured `cab_profile`/`cab_member_role` 12-of-15 and 6-of-7 subsumed and **pinned them against being built**. Rendering them again on the change side would have put a second answer to "was this decision quorate?" on the platform, which is exactly what `CommitteePort`'s javadoc forbids. **So what shipped is the thing that was actually missing: the queue, and a signpost.** Measured first — `awaitingDecision` (the CAB queue) was reachable over **no HTTP route at all**, its only caller the agenda builder; referral is **manual**, so nothing in `transition` tells a committee a change is waiting, and dev held **2 changes at CAB_PENDING with no referral** while the CAB's only 2 open referrals named change ids that exist in **no** organisation, and its next sitting's agenda named **no change**. New: `GET /api/v1/changes/cab-queue` (CHANGE:READ) + route `/changes/cab` + the sidebar row **CE-0 deliberately withheld** until it had one. Three populations kept apart (F2-94's distinction, third outing): a change nobody referred, a referral nobody can table (`SUBJECT_NOT_STATED`/`SUBJECT_NOT_FOUND` — the check the committee module is *structurally unable* to make, since S5a took its `change_requests` imports to zero), and an item the committee holds. **Two things the slice's own verification changed.** (1) The ladder's first rung keyed on the gate map's `configured` flag, and the live probe caught it reporting every row unfixable on a gate whose referral returned **201** — that flag folds F2-96's three faults together and dev's CAB is the third (correctly mapped, zero voting seats), so `mapped` and `canDecide` are now separate and only the first gates the ladder. (2) The browser showed *Change items tabled: 1* on a sitting tabling nothing — a charter standing item carries a `sourceModule` too (**F2-111**), so referral lines now carry a `decisionRef`. Also **F2-110**: the agenda is written in two key shapes and each reader handled one, so 24 of 34 dev agendas rendered as empty bullets on screen while every agenda the builder produces printed dashes in the pack. Suite **3,169/0/0** · FE **300/300** · live **31/31** · browser both languages, one nav row highlighted per location. ⚠ `mockChanges.ts` **deliberately NOT deleted** — see the CE plan's row: it is 1 of **15** peer fallbacks under an explicit owner-decision-pending ratchet, and deleting one module's copy is F2-15's job, not CE-13's | — |

⚠ **S6 is gated on F2-28, and this is not negotiable.** CE-3 makes the CAB a governed body *over the
committee engine*, and CE-4's decisions are asserted on quorate minutes. The quorum denominator is
currently caller-authored (F2-28/DF-5). Building CAB governance on it would **industrialise** the defect:
every CAB decision would inherit a forgeable validity precondition, and the CAB pack export would present
it as evidence. Close F2-28 first — it is 3–5 days and already sequenced ahead.

| **V330** | **`FRAUD_INVESTIGATOR` — the persona V328 declined to pick (ADR-017 D-9).** V328 catalogued `FRAUD:READ`/`WRITE` and refused to anoint an investigator because three roles were equally plausible. Now decided on measurement: **`FRAUD:WRITE` was held only by GRC_ADMINISTRATOR and ORG_ADMIN, which also hold 10 and 8 `APPROVE` permissions respectively** — so the only two roles that could investigate a fraud case were also the platform's broadest approvers, which is the SoD problem stated concretely rather than asserted. SAMA fraud-control expectations and the AML/CTF Law treat fraud investigation as distinct from compliance and from approval authority. The three cyber candidates were rejected on measurement: FORENSICS_LEAD (15 perms), IR_LEAD (17) and SECURITY_ANALYST (18) all hold **zero** APPROVE — correctly investigator-shaped — but all three are *cyber incident* personas, and fraud is financial crime. Creates **one** global, system, **non-privileged**, bilingual role holding **exactly `FRAUD:READ` + `FRAUD:WRITE`**. ➕ Three field choices were measured, not assumed: `organisation_id = NULL` because **47 of 58 existing roles are scoped to org 1 and so do not exist for orgs 2–3**; `is_privileged = 0` because the **only** reader of that column is `BreakGlassEventRepository`, so flagging it would file every fraud investigator as a break-glass subject; and `display_name_ar` is mandatory. Six verifications throw, including **it must hold no APPROVE permission anywhere** and **GRC_ADMINISTRATOR must still hold `FRAUD:WRITE`** — revoking would leave a tenant unable to work any fraud case until the new role is assigned, which is an outage, not a control. ⚠ **This creates the persona; it does not enforce SoD** — measured: `FraudService` has no self-check or four-eyes gate on `setOutcome`/`close`, so one principal can still open and resolve the same case. Maker-checker on fraud outcome is a separate slice | ✅ **dev ×2** (2026-07-30; second pass 0 changes) · **prod pending the merge** |
| **V331** | **Board-pack `audience` aligned to the committee vocabulary — the prerequisite for ADR-017 D-11, and the measurement that stopped D-11 from shipping.** D-11 decided board-pack reads should be scoped by `audience` bound to committee membership, default deny. **Measuring it first killed it:** only **5 of 31** committee member terms are linked to a platform user and **zero** on the Board and Audit Committee, so default-deny would have made **13 of 16 packs unreadable by any non-admin principal, all 7 BOARD packs among them** — the board locked out of board packs. And that is not a data-quality wait-it-out problem: **F2-26 deliberately supports directors who will never have a login**, so membership-by-login is *structurally* incapable of being the whole control. ➕ **The join was broken independently:** three vocabularies, two wrong — the DTO `@Pattern` accepted 4 values, the table held 5, `committee_type` has 17; one pack held `CYBER_COMMITTEE` **which the API could no longer create**, and neither `CYBER_COMMITTEE` nor `EXEC` matched any committee, so `audience = committee_type` resolved **3 of 5** and would have *silently* mis-scoped the rest. What shipped: the two drifted values re-pointed (`CYBER_COMMITTEE`→`CYBER_SECURITY`, `EXEC`→`EXEC_COMMITTEE`), the pattern widened to the committee vocabulary, and a THROW if **any** stored audience fails to resolve — unresolved **5 → 0**, no read behaviour changed. Four verifications throw, one of them anti-vacuity (an empty table would pass the audience check having verified nothing). ⬜ **Enforcement deferred on a stated condition, not a hope:** it needs a committee lookup behind a port (ADR-012 — and **no architecture test enforces ADR-012 today**, so that boundary is a decision without a control) and a product call on who reads a pack whose committee has no linked members, which cannot be *nobody*.
