| **V339** | **`platform.audit_log.user_role_source` — the ledger says how it knows who acted (F2-54).** `user_role` held **16 distinct values across 6,065 dev rows and 12 across 3,989 prod rows, of which ZERO were role names** — 13 permissions (`BCM_APPROVE` alone on 1,665 dev rows) and 3 `ROLE_`-prefixed. Labels history rather than rewriting it: 3,317 → `LEGACY_UNRECORDED` (the old code could not distinguish *no principal* from *no authorities*, so neither does the label), 2,748 → `LEGACY_AUTHORITY_SAMPLE` (**do not reconcile these against the role matrix**). ⚠ **Deliberately NO CHECK constraint:** the `ck_ca_source` landmine is that a CHECK over a value-set the code keeps extending turns each new label into a 500 — and here a rejected INSERT is a **lost audit row**. The label set is pinned by test instead. ⚠ **A "THROW if audit_log is empty" anti-vacuity check was written into this file and then removed:** on a fresh production database Flyway runs it against an empty ledger where empty is *correct*, so it would have failed every new install on migration 339 — **the exact failure the empty-DB spine exists to catch, authored into the migration meant to be careful.** The claim moved to the probe, which can make it truthfully | ✅ **dev ×2** (2026-08-01; second pass 0 changes) · **prod on its next restart** |
| **V340** | **`platform.audit_log` hash chain — tamper-evidence for the ledger every control cites as proof (F2-31).** Adds `row_hash` / `prev_hash` / `hash_alg` / `sealed_at` plus two filtered indexes. `hash_alg` is per-row on purpose: without it a key rotation would make every historical row fail verification, and **an integrity alarm that fires on a routine operation is one operators learn to ignore**. ➕ **No back-fill, and that is the design:** sealing pre-V340 rows would produce a chain verifying cleanly over rows that were never protected — a forged assurance, exactly what F2-21 showed this ledger would corroborate if asked. Sealed and unsealed counts are reported separately so the unprotected span stays visible instead of being absorbed into a verdict. Verification asserts **shape only** (4 columns, 2 indexes) — V339's lesson applied one file later: a migration may only assert what is true of every database it will ever meet, including an empty one | ✅ **dev ×2** (2026-08-01; second pass 0 changes) · **prod on its next restart** |
| **V341** | **The 67 JPA-mapped tables and 14 schemas the migration set never created — F2-70.** **Generated**, not hand-written: `schema_from_catalog.py` reads the live dev catalog (`sys.columns`/`sys.indexes`/`sys.key_constraints`), because V338 proved that copying stale DDL files reproduces a stale schema and 67 copies would be 67 repeats. 1,666 columns, 112 indexes, 86 PK/UNIQUE constraints. ⚠ **Declares all 14 schemas its tables live in, not just the 11 no migration covers** — the first emission did the latter and the empty-database proof failed on the very first `CREATE TABLE`, because `ai_governance` is declared in an *earlier* file. A migration must declare the schema it needs. ⚠ **Also do not write DDL keywords in this file's prose:** the header once read *"the 11 `CREATE SCHEMA` statements"* and `SchemaContractRatchetTest`'s regex read a schema literally named `statements` | ✅ **dev ×2** (2026-08-01; second pass 0 changes) · **prod on its next restart** |
| **V342** | **Foreign keys for the V341 tables — F2-70.** Separate file so declaration order never matters. **Both directions, and the inbound half was nearly missed:** 7 FKs point out of the 67 and **15 point in**, of which **9 exist only because the Python bootstrap made them** (`risk.risks`, `ethics.aml_alerts/cases/disclosures/kyc_subjects`, `privacy.dpia`, `compliance.controls/findings` → `compliance.frameworks`). Their parents *are* migration-created, so emitting only the outbound half would have produced a schema that passes a table-level check and still lacks the referential integrity dev has. Every statement is guarded on **both** endpoints existing, and the verification counts only FKs whose endpoints are present — V339's lesson one file later: a migration may assert only what is true of every database it will meet | ✅ **dev ×2** (2026-08-01) · **prod on its next restart** |
| **V343** | **`INTEGRATIONS` becomes the 31st permission module — ADR-017 D-8.** The 20 role-name guards in `module/integrations` recited **nine role names of which six do not exist** in `iam.roles` (verified absent on **both** databases): `SOC_AUTOMATION`, `INTEGRATION_SERVICE`, `SOC_ANALYST`, `SOC_TIER_2`, `INCIDENT_COMMANDER`, `BOARD_MEMBER`. `hasAnyRole` gets no PLATFORM_ADMIN bypass, so integration administration was reachable by **two personas** — one of which bypasses in code — and `splunk/test-push` by the administrator alone. **So this migration WIDENS access, and is justified and probed as a re-grant** per D-10's rule. `READ` → ORG_ADMIN, GRC_ADMINISTRATOR, CISO, CYBER_MANAGER; `WRITE` → ORG_ADMIN, CISO. Seven verifications throw, three of them anti-vacuity in **opposite** directions: granted-to-nobody fails; any of VIEWER / REGULATOR_VIEWER / EXTERNAL_AUDITOR / RISK_ANALYST / SUPPLIER_MANAGER holding it fails (that population is the entire reason `CYBER:READ` did not fit, so if the new module reaches it nothing was gained); and **GRC_ADMINISTRATOR or CYBER_MANAGER holding `WRITE` fails**, because reading whether a feed is healthy and choosing to transmit out of the Kingdom are different acts. ➕ **It also asserts `WRITE` ⊆ `READ`** — ADR-017 D-10c's containment generalised one permission pair later: a principal that may push to a third party and is refused the health of the feed it pushed to is not a coherent authorisation state. ➕ **And it THROWs if any of the six ghost names ever appears in `iam.roles`** — seeding one to make an old guard fire is §7's standing exclusion, which is now enforced rather than documented | ✅ **dev ×2** (2026-08-01; second pass 0 changes) · **prod on its next restart** |
| **V344** | **W2-0's grandfather licence backfill — every organisation × every ratified SKU.** `LicenseGuardAspect` is fail-closed and, measured identically in dev and prod, **org 1 held 20 legacy codes while orgs 2 and 3 held one row each (`SOCOPS`)** — so annotating controllers first (F2-04, ~2,138 endpoints) would have blacked out two tenants on everything but security operations. **22 → 57 rows, 35 created.** **Grant-all-then-tighten, not license-what-has-data**: inferring entitlement from row counts false-negatives on read-only, empty and seasonal usage, and every false negative is a 403 for a paying customer. Every created row is tagged in `notes` as a grandfather grant, because the day after this runs *granted administratively* and *sold* are otherwise indistinguishable and the “then-tighten” half becomes a guess. Eight verifications throw, several anti-vacuity in **opposite** directions: an empty `platform.organisations` fails (a backfill with no tenants is an unverified migration, not a no-op); a remaining (org, SKU) gap fails; a row that exists but is **disabled or out of window** fails, because `enabledModulesFor` filters on all three and a row is not an entitlement; the count of rows *not* carrying the marker must be unchanged, so the file can only INSERT and never overwrite a commercial fact; the 7 Base-retired codes' population must be unchanged (ADR-018 R4); **`NDI` → `DATA_AI_GOV` is asserted specifically** even though it falls out of the general rule, because a required grant that is only *implied* disappears the next time the general rule is edited (R3); and `SOCOPS` must come through with the same row count and its original notes — it is the control group. ⚠ **Every join against the two table variables carries `COLLATE DATABASE_DEFAULT`**: `module_code` is `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_SC_UTF8` while the database default is `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS`, and a table-variable column takes the default. It failed on exactly the five join predicates and on none of the literal comparisons — literals are coercible-default, an explicitly collated column is not | ✅ **dev ×2** (2026-08-01; second pass 0 rows created) · **prod on its next restart** |
| **V345** | **A17-D3's ECAB ratification SLA** — `change_mgmt.change_ratification_policy` (per tenant, `sla_business_days` **bounded 1..10 by `ck_ratification_sla_bounds`**, escalating to `CISO`), nine columns on `change_requests` (six-state `ratification_state` + due/ratified/escalated + **`cab_decision_by_id`/`_name`**, F2-93), two CHECK constraints, the grandfather pass, and the `CHG-RATIFY-BREACH` F8 rule. **The bound is in the CHECK and not only the DTO** because ADR-017 D-3 amendment 2's examiner question (*"what stops a tenant choosing 365 days?"*) must have an answer that survives a direct UPDATE and a support script — both proven refused live. `ck_change_ratification_clock` makes PENDING-without-a-due-date impossible, which is what keeps the breach predicate total: the one legacy EMERGENCY row has **no implementation timestamp at all**, and against a NULL due date the comparison is UNKNOWN and reports it compliant forever. Four self-checks THROW, including one asserting the rule carries **`to_role`** — the key `AutomationActionExecutor` actually reads — because the first cut used the seed convention's `"roles":[…]` and the escalation reached nobody (**F2-94**) | ✅ **dev ×2** (2026-08-02; second pass idempotent) · **prod on its next restart** |
| **V346** | **F2-94's recipient-key normalisation** — rewrites `AR-148`'s audience from `escalateTo`+`cc` onto `roles` (the one row the code fix cannot reach: those two keys appear on this rule alone, and reading them would reopen the vocabulary for a single row), and catalogues **`AUTOMATION:READ`** + grants it to exactly the roles already holding AUTOMATION WRITE/DELETE. ⚠ **V158 is deliberately not edited** — it is applied in dev and prod, and changing an applied migration changes its Flyway checksum; repairs go forward. Four self-checks THROW: no NOTIFY/ESCALATE rule left without a readable key (bar the allow-listed `AR-149`/F2-97), AR-148 specifically rewritten, the permission catalogued, **and granted to at least one role** — a permission nobody holds makes the endpoint PLATFORM_ADMIN-only, which is the defect not the guard. Scope stated honestly: this makes AR-148's audience *readable*, not *reachable* — all three of its names are ghost roles, and inventing them would fabricate an access model | ✅ **dev ×2** (2026-08-03; second pass idempotent) · **prod on its next restart** |

✅ **Next free number is `V343`** (verified on disk 2026-08-01: `V342__spine_bootstrap_foreign_keys.sql` is the highest file — read by numeric sort, because a lexicographic `ls | tail -1` answers `V9__…`). ⚠ **This line said `V338` while `V338` was already on disk and applied, and the table had no row for it — my own drift, found by merging the very branch that existed to fix the previous instance of it.** The lesson is not "be careful": it is that *this* number belongs in the same commit as the migration file, which is why §6d wants it generated rather than typed.

🔑 **Unstarted work no longer reserves a version — it holds a position.** X-2 was reassigned nineteen times, every reassignment costing a cascade through four other blocks, and the second-model review found the §3 copies of those numbers still pointing at *applied* migrations — the exact author-below-an-applied-number trap that produced F2-49. Rows marked **V-next** take their number when they are written. **This is the §6d remedy, finally executed.**

⚠ **This table has now been renumbered by delivery ten times.** That is the scheme working as designed —
but it is also the strongest argument for the structural finding §6d accepted: a hand-maintained number
ledger across four documents will keep drifting. The mechanical check below is the only reliable authority.

**Reservations are advisory; ordering is not.** A number is only real when the file exists. Treat every row
above as *intent*, and immediately before creating a file run:

```bash
ls tss-grc-backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/sqlserver | sed 's/^V\([0-9]*\)__.*/\1/' | sort -n | tail -1
```

**Never author a number below an already-applied migration** — Flyway applies in version order and would
skip it. Corollary the council had to point out: **if item A is delivered before item B, A takes the lower
number**, no matter which has the earlier ID.

**Rules.** Additive only unless explicitly authorised. Idempotent. Applied to dev **twice** via pyodbc
(Flyway is off in dev). Prod picks them up via Flyway on the next jar — **the baseline is `V292`** (not V312: that was merely the head the day this was written), and as of the 2026-07-31 16:47 restart **prod's Flyway head is V336**, so the pending set is **V337 … V344 — eight files** (re-measured 2026-08-01 closing F2-69; it read "V337 + V338" until then, i.e. this paragraph went stale again within a day of being corrected, which is precisely why the number is now build-enforced by `MeasuredBaselineFreshnessTest` rather than maintained by hand). **280 of the 332 files on disk sit at or below the baseline and therefore never execute against production at all.** ⚠ **This paragraph said "baseline is V312, so V313–V317 all land on the next restart" — wrong number, wrong pending set, and stale by a restart that had already happened. It is verbatim the defect F2-69 files against `MEASURED_BASELINE.md`, living inside the plan that files it.** The baseline is load-bearing: it decides which migrations can *never* run, which is the whole of RC-A. Read it from `platform.flyway_schema_history`, never from prose. `flyway.clean` is disabled; never run it. Every destructive change needs
a reverse recipe in the file header and the user's explicit OK.

⚠ **Reserved numbers move.** `V316` was reserved for `ck_change_status`, then taken by DF-4; `V317` was
reserved for the entitlement backfill, then taken by the D-7/D-8 grants. Read this table, not an older
plan section, before writing a migration — and re-check it immediately before the file is created.

## §5 Definition of done — per slice, not per stage

A slice is done when **all** of these hold. This is the standard every completed programme in §0 met.

1. **Full backend suite green** before and after (`mvn -o clean package`, ~2,250+ tests; grep the log for
   `BUILD SUCCESS` / `Tests run:` — wrapper exit codes lie).
2. **Live-probed against the running stack** — Python `urllib` + `pyodbc`, through the Vite proxy (`:5173`)
   when verifying browser behaviour. Guard **403**s and SoD **409**s are part of the probe, not exceptions
   to it; four-eyes paths need **two users** (`admin` + `so_officer`).
3. **The net is proven real** — temporarily break the new guard/check and confirm a test fails, then
   restore. A test that cannot fail is not evidence.
4. **Migrations applied to dev twice** and idempotent.
5. **Ratchets not worsened** — authorisation coverage ≤ 21, plus (once added) tightness, boundary edges,
   mock imports, page size, i18n, accessibility.
6. **Bilingual** `*_en` + `*_ar` on any new user-visible record, client-gated in the form.
7. **Committed per slice** on a feature branch, with the probe result in the message.

## §6 Open decisions

**Six of eight are now decided** (2026-07-25), on the owner's instruction to adopt the leader-aligned
best-practice default rather than keep a third of the plan blocked. Each carries an ADR with the
comparator evidence, the alternatives, the trade-offs accepted and its reversal cost.

### Decided

| # | Decision | Answer | ADR |
|---|---|---|---|
| **D-3** | Per-module SKUs or suite-only? | **Per-module SKUs, one deployment, one database**, enforced by the `@RequireModule` aspect that already exists; a **mandatory Base Platform SKU**; the catalogue moves from `LicensingService.KNOWN_MODULES` (`Set.of`, needs a recompile) into the module registry; **grandfather backfill first** — orgs 2/3 hold one licence row each, so annotating before backfilling is an outage. Pricing explicitly out of scope | [ADR-013](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-013-PER-MODULE-SKU-AND-ENTITLEMENT.md) |
| **D-4** | `incident` — separate SKU or part of Cyber? | **Dissolved.** A SKU is a commercial view over packages, so this was never architectural. `incident` joins the **Cyber & SOC** SKU; the package does not move. **F2-06b is no longer gated on this** and is judged purely on ADR-011 | ADR-013 |
| **D-6** | Does `PLATFORM_ADMIN` bypass entitlement? | **No — never, for any role.** The question dissolves: platform administration lives in the mandatory Base SKU, so there is nothing to bypass. One rule beats a role-dependent one, and it is what Salesforce does | ADR-013 |
| **D-7** | Which roles may cast a committee vote? | **Roster confers the vote; the grant opens the door.** `ADMIN:APPROVE` created (**15 roles**); `ADMIN:WRITE` cut to the **secretariat (3)** because it also appoints member terms — the same grant did both, so any voter could add themselves to a roster and manufacture a quorum. `ADMIN:READ` **21**, `ADMIN:EXPORT` **7**. `INTERNAL_AUDITOR` gets READ+EXPORT but **no vote** (IIA 1110 independence) | [ADR-014](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-014-COMMITTEE-VOTING-RIGHTS.md) |
| **D-8** | Which roles may cast a CAB vote? | **Guard stays `CHANGE:APPROVE`; the grant was the defect.** Created the missing ITIL **`CHANGE_MANAGER`** role and took `CHANGE:APPROVE` from **3 → 9**: +CHANGE_MANAGER, SERVICE_OWNER, CISO, CYBER_MANAGER, COMPLIANCE_OFFICER, EXECUTIVE_REVIEWER. Safe to widen because DF-4's provenance controls carry the load | [ADR-015](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-015-CHANGE-AUTHORITY-AND-CAB-VOTING.md) |
| **Doc governance** | How is the 335-document corpus governed? *(was "provisional")* | **Docs-as-code, one canonical doc per subject, machine-checked.** Status header on every `.md` (`AUTHORITATIVE`/`GENERATED`/`HISTORIC`/`SUPERSEDED-BY`), supersede-never-delete, generated files never hand-edited, every quantitative claim cites its command, register CI-enforced. Grounded in ISO 27001 §7.5 — *the clause this product sells* | [ADR-016](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-016-DOCUMENTATION-GOVERNANCE.md) |

**D-5** (which Saudi integration pack generation governs — root 20 files vs `docs/` 33) is **downgraded
from a decision to a task** under W0-6: ADR-016 R2/R3 supply the rule, so it needs a content diff and a
`SUPERSEDED-BY` header, not a judgement call.

### Still open — these need tenant-specific business input and cannot be defaulted

| # | Decision | Blocks | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | Which committee constitutes CAB / ECAB **per tenant**? Orgs 2/3 have **no** committees and were deliberately **not** fabricated | S6 | GRC owner |
| D-2 | ECAB emergency-ratification SLA (default 5 business days) — per tenant? | S7 | GRC owner |
| **O-8** | **Authorise a push, and decide whether it is standing.** Local `master` is ahead of `origin/master`, so **this machine holds the only copy** of D-5, F2-58, F2-60, F2-56 and migrations V331–V334 — several of them authorisation fixes. Merge and push have been separate authorisations all along and that is deliberate; the risk is that the gap now compounds silently, and **no `W-*` or `F2-*` id owns it** (the second-model review found this by counting commits, not by reading any row). Either a one-off "push" or a standing rule; the plan cannot assume either | Deployment durability | Product owner |
| **D-4** | **Is there an invitation subsystem, or is onboarding step 3 a checklist?** Found in F2-33 (2026-07-29). A real invitation means token + mail + accept + set-password, **plus** a decision on who may invite: `POST /api/v1/admin/users` is `hasRole('PLATFORM_ADMIN')` today, so the tenant admin running onboarding cannot create users at all. The alternative — generating other people's initial passwords and displaying them — is a credentials decision and is not a UI fix. The false promise is already removed either way | F2-33's capability half | Product owner |

D-1 and D-2 are per-customer charter facts. Inventing them would be fabricating governance data, which
is the defect F2-02 exists to record. D-4 is a product decision about a capability, and the same rule
applies: the screen now describes what it does rather than what it would do if the subsystem existed.

### Follow-ups these decisions created

| # | Item | From |
|---|---|---|
| F2-23b | Split a `COMMITTEE` permission module out of `ADMIN`. `ADMIN:*` gates the 7 committee controllers and **nothing else** — 59 methods — so the module name is a misnomer that couples committee grants to unrelated admin screens | ADR-014 |
| F2-13c | Per-change-type change authority (standard / normal / emergency) in data, sourced from the CH-17 authorisation matrix. Would let `EXECUTIVE_REVIEWER` leave the normal-change voting set | ADR-015 |
| W2-0 | **Grandfather licence backfill** — must land before any `@RequireModule` sweep | ADR-013 |

## §6b Reconciliation with the strategic frame — the 16 `F-0NN` findings, judged

**This closes the reconciliation the Bible's `README` marked "provisional and unconfirmed".** Every finding
in [`TSS_GRC_PLATFORM_AUDIT_REPORT.md`](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/TSS_GRC_PLATFORM_AUDIT_REPORT.md) §10 was
re-derived from source and from the running system on 2026-07-25, using that document's own truth labels.
Nothing was accepted because it was written down, including the parts that flatter this plan.

| # | Claim | Label | Judgment | Lands as |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F-001** | Change approval identity is client-controlled | `VERIFIED` → **CLOSED** | True when written. Closed at `21c83d09`: actor from `Authentication`, DTO with no approver field, SoD on the resolved user id, `CHANGE:APPROVE`, `V316` unique index → 409. 7 of its 9 P0-A steps done; steps 8–9 (named commands, ECAB ratification) are F-002's | **DF-4 / F2-24 ✅** |
| **F-002** | Generic transitions bypass approval semantics | `PARTLY CONTRADICTED` | **The exploit is closed; the design debt is real.** `CAB_APPROVED` now requires a quorate committee resolution (Track D) *or* ≥N recorded `APPROVE` votes with zero `REJECT` (CM-C2 + the CH-17 matrix); `EMERGENCY_APPROVED` requires `changeType = EMERGENCY`. So a `CHANGE:WRITE` holder can *advance* a legitimately-decided change but cannot *manufacture* the decision. The literal claim — a generic transition guarded by `WRITE` reaching privileged states — is still true. **Critical → P1, downgrading to P2 only when F2-28 closes.** The council overturned an unconditional P2: `CAB_APPROVED` has two legitimating legs — recorded votes (sound after DF-4) *or* a quorate committee resolution — and the second leg rests on `quorumMet`, which F2-28 proves is caller-authored. Until V319 lands, one of the two paths that make this "closed" is itself forgeable | **NEW F2-29** (below) |
| **F-003** | Committee votes **and quorum** can be manufactured | `VERIFIED` — **half open, and the open half is P0** | Vote identity closed (`f3ea92fc`, V315, V317). **The quorum half was never closed and this register's first pass missed it.** Attendance is caller-authored free text, the denominator is computed *from that sheet*, and `quorumMet` can be set directly with zero attendance | **DF-5 / F2-28 — new P0** |
| **F-004** | Package licensing is not platform-wide | `VERIFIED` | Exact: 22 `@RequireModule` controllers of 252, all `socops`. Un-gated by ADR-013; needs W2-0 backfill first | **F2-04** (Wave 2) |
| **F-005** | Module boundaries are not executable | `VERIFIED`, numbers corrected | Re-measured: **754** peer imports (audit: 734), **128** directed pairs (136), **13** bidirectional (14). Volume up, tangle down — and still no architecture test | **F2-08b** (Wave 1) |
| **F-006** | IA exposes implementation sprawl | `VERIFIED` | **329** routed pages in a 925-line `App.tsx` | **F2-07 / F2-07c** |
| **F-007** | Arabic completeness claims exceed enforcement | `VERIFIED`, and sharper than stated | `ChangeRequest.titleEn` is `nullable = false`; **`titleAr` has neither `nullable = false` nor `@NotBlank`**. ### The audit's own verification claims, re-run

| Claim | Then | Now | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Backend suite | 2,316 | **2,460 / 0** | grew; both figures plausible at their commit |
| Authorisation ratchet | "threshold of 62" | **21** | **stale in the audit** — it had already been ratcheted |
| Module directories | 58 | **58** | exact |
| Controllers · repositories · services | ~252 · 423 · 313 | **252 · 423 · 314** | essentially exact |
| `@RequireModule` controllers | 22, all SOC Ops | **22, all SOC Ops** | exact |
| Cross-module imports · pairs · bidirectional | 734 · 136 · 14 | **754 · 128 · 13** | corrected |
| Routed frontend pages | "more than 300" | **329** | exact |
| Live two-principal probes | "remain open" | **run** | **closed** — 15/18 through the Vite proxy |

**Verdict on the strategic frame: sound, and worth having.** Its structural measurements reproduce almost
exactly. Three findings needed correction (F-002, F-009, F-011), one was materially understated (F-008),
one is unverified (F-010) — and **one caught a live P0 that this register's own first pass missed** (F-003's
quorum half). That is the second time the two passes have caught different instances of the *same* defect
class in different code, which retires any argument that one of them is redundant.

### Rating, re-judged

The audit's 3.0/5 was fair at its baseline. On measured change since:

| Dimension | Audit | Now | Why |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Security / control integrity | 1.8 | **2.3** | 2 of its 7 critical weaknesses closed with mutation proof and live probes, 1 mitigated — but a live P0 forgery chain on the governance backbone (F2-28), a platform-wide least-privilege inversion (F2-27) and 214 unaudited mass-assignment sites do not support 2.6. Council: "2.2–2.4 is the defensible band"; I had written "movement is real but narrow" and then awarded +0.8 |
| Commercial packaging | 2.4 | **2.6** | still 22 of 252 controllers, but the decision is made (ADR-013) and the mechanism is proven fail-closed |
| Quality / operations | 3.2 | **3.4** | +144 tests, adversarial governance tests, mutation-proven guards; operational evidence still absent |
| Enterprise architecture | 2.6 | **2.6** | unchanged — pairs and cycles improved marginally, no architecture test yet |
| **Weighted** | **3.0** | **≈3.05** | Movement is real but narrow. **Still `Qualified`** |

**Market verdict, changed in one place:** the audit made a controlled pilot conditional on Criticals
closing. F-001 is closed and F-002 is mitigated — but **F2-28 is a new open Critical**, so *"pilot after
Criticals close"* still holds and now has a specific, costed blocker: 3–5 days, sequenced behind F2-25 (2 h)
and F2-26 (1 d). General availability, government final acceptance and per-module sale verdicts are
**unchanged**.

| **F2-40** | carried from F2-28 | **20 resolutions carry seeded scalar tallies with zero vote rows.** `vote_for/against/abstain` were seeded directly, so any path that calls `recomputeTally` rewrites them to `0/0/0 DEFERRED` — silently replacing recorded governance outcomes with an empty tally. Same class as F2-28 on the *numerator of a resolution* rather than the quorum of a meeting, and the disposition should match: a `tally_source ASSERTED_LEGACY / COMPUTED` marker rather than a backfill or a flip. Belongs with **F2-29** (named change commands), which is where resolution outcomes are consumed | S | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-26** `V322` (**P1, not S-cosmetic** — `recomputeTally`'s only caller is `recordVote`, so **one member using the shipped voting panel** would have rewritten a 5/0/0 APPROVED board resolution to 1/0/0, or to `0/0/0 DEFERRED` on a RECUSED vote, silently). The prescribed marker was right; **the prescription's framing was wrong for the second time today.** "Seeded scalar tallies … caller-authored" invites *refuse the caller's tally* — and `CommitteesPage` has a secretariat resolution form that posts `voteFor`/`voteAgainst`/`voteAbstain`/`outcome` off the minutes, which is how a board that votes in the room records its decision. Ignoring those fields would have silently discarded the recorded decision: F2-22a's `@JsonIgnore` trap again. So an asserted tally stays legal and becomes **labelled** — `ASSERTED` + the authenticated recorder, `COMPUTED` when derived from vote rows, `ASSERTED_LEGACY` for the 20 pre-V322 rows (numbers and `APPROVED` kept, **no attribution invented**) — and `recordVote` now **409s** on an asserted resolution instead of converting it. Four `tally*` fields are `READ_ONLY`, so provenance cannot be posted (the DF-3 rule). | **F2-41** | carried from F2-27 | **Export History records that an export happened, not what left the building.** `ExportAuditInterceptor` already fires on every file-returning endpoint, but it captures no **row count** and no **data classification**, so the audit trail cannot answer the only two questions a DLP or PDPL enquiry asks: *how much* was extracted, and *how sensitive was it*. F2-27's recommendation #4 asked for both; it is a separate concern from authorisation (40 endpoints, each needing to report its own row count through the interceptor) and was deliberately not bundled. Now that `MODULE:EXPORT` is enforced, an export event also has a meaningful *authority* to record alongside the volume | S | ✅ **VOLUME DONE 2026-07-28** (no migration) · ⬜ classification map needs the user. Three parts: **`ExportAuditContext`**, a request-thread channel the generator writes and the interceptor reads — because the interceptor sees a byte stream and only the generator knows it wrote 12 rows or 12,000; the interceptor now records **`bytes`** (from `Content-Length`, so free and covering **every** endpoint) plus `rowCount`/`sheetCount`/`rowCountSource`; and instrumentation **at the generators, not the 40 controllers** — `TabularExportService` (xlsx+docx), `RegisterExport` (xlsx+pdf), and the row-loads in `ExportService` + `DocxExportService`, 16 insertions, no controller touched. **Row-count coverage 4/21 → 16/21, `bytes` 21/21**, and the coverage is *measured*: the probe hits 20 real export endpoints enumerated from the controllers. The 5 uncounted are **asserted** to be board packs and ZIP bundles — documents, not row registers, where rows are not the unit. ⚠ **Absent is not zero** — an uninstrumented export reports `rowCount: null` + `rowCountSource: UNINSTRUMENTED`, never `0`, because to a DLP enquiry "exported nothing" and "we did not measure" are opposite answers (third finding this week on that distinction, after F2-40 and F2-46). ⚠ **The thread-local is the risky part** — a leaked count would be attributed to the *next* request on that pooled thread; the interceptor clears in a `finally` on every path, pinned by a unit test and a live probe leg. Live **11/11**, `ExportAuditInterceptorTest` 8/8, suite **2,590/0/0**. ⬜ **The classification half needs YOUR decision and was deliberately not guessed:** `module/dataclass` defines the NDMO 4-level scheme but applies it to `DataAsset` rows, not modules, so labelling an export needs a **module → level policy map — which is a customer classification policy, not an engineering default**. Plumbing is in place; inventing the mapping would be the F2-40 mistake of asserting a value the platform cannot derive. A volume threshold alert (e.g. > 10,000 rows) is now expressible and is the natural next step |

Flyway **skips** an unparseable name instead of failing, which is why nobody noticed: they were added **2026-05-11** and migrations through **V312** have applied since, so no boot ever choked on them. Both are packaged in the **deployed** jar (302 migrations in it), so any environment that trusts Flyway is missing their contents. Impact is bounded — both are seed/demo data, not schema (V46a back-fills `INC-{id}` cross-references for the cyber→PDPL bridge; V47a injects a deliberately broken evidence chain for the chain-of-custody demo) — but the **class** is what matters: a file that looks like a migration, sits in the migration folder, and silently is not one. Fix is a decision, not a rename: renumber them to free versions (**V324/V325**, since 46/47 are long applied and re-running them out of order is not available), or delete them as spent demo seed. `/actuator/info` now reports them under `unversionedFiles` and the W0-7 probe fails if the two sides disagree, so they cannot hide again while the decision waits | XS | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-07-30 — DELETED, and the class is now ratcheted** ([ADR-017](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-017-OWNER-DELEGATED-DECISIONS.md) D-6). **Measured both databases before choosing, rather than reasoning from the filenames:** dev `TSS_GRC` and deployed `TSS_GRC_Deployment` each already hold 4 `SEED_DEMO_V46` bridge-log rows, 3 `CYBER_TO_PDPL_V1` breaches, 2 incidents flagged `affects_personal_data` and 1 evidence item at `chain_status='BROKEN'` — the content is present on both sides (prod was restored from a dev dump; `FLYWAY_BASELINE_VERSION=292`), so deleting loses nothing. ⚠ **And renumbering would have been actively harmful**, which the row above did not see: both files are *demo seed data*, so giving them valid versions would have made Flyway inject fabricated PDPL breach records and a **deliberately corrupted chain of custody** into every future production database — in a GRC platform, where `chain_status='BROKEN'` is what a regulator reads as a custody failure. **The bad filename was accidentally load-bearing.** New `MigrationFilenameRatchetTest` (2 tests) asserts every file matches Flyway's versioned pattern and that no two claim the same version — **mutation-proven** by adding `V99z__mutation_probe.sql` and confirming the failure names it. Applied dev **twice from the measured baseline of 1** (1 → 12 → 12, exact set asserted, grants 1453 → 1464). Live **5/5** — `viewer` creates a list **201** then deletes it **403**, `compliance` deletes the same list **204**; the pre-fix 204 is **derived** (the same token still gets 201 on this controller's `POST`, which carries the identical WRITE expression the delete had). ⚠ **Consequence worth naming:** the creator can no longer delete their own list without `WORKFLOW:DELETE` — "creator deletes own" is an *ownership* rule, not a permission one, and inventing it would have been a feature. `WorkflowRecipientListGuardTest` 3/3, suite **2,634/0/0**. **Section C now fully swept: 14 rows → 1 fixed, 2 confirmed deliberate (`EVIDENCE:EXPORT`/`ADMIN:EXPORT` are documented `ExportAuthorizationRatchetTest.ALLOWLIST` entries — do not 'fix' them), 11 genuinely feature-absent.** ⬜ **Those 11 need YOUR call, not a code change:** each is build-the-export or revoke-the-permission, and **revoking is a data change**; `PRIVACY:EXPORT` in particular looks like a missing feature given PDPL RoPA reporting. | **F2-52** | measured opening F2-05 | **Guards authorise by role names, and 40 of the 74 they recite do not exist.** Reflection over the compiled `@PreAuthorize` set joined to live `iam.roles`: **647 pure role guards** (role leg, no permission leg) across **38 module packages**, naming **74 roles**, **40 absent from the 57 in `iam.roles`**. `hasAnyRole('X')` tests for the authority `ROLE_X` — a name that matches nothing is a permanently false OR leg, with no compile error, no startup warning and no log line. And because a role guard never reaches `ModulePermissionEvaluator`, **it never reaches the PLATFORM_ADMIN bypass either**, so the population that passes is exactly the surviving real names: **361 of 647 lose at least one leg**, and **40 are left admitting `PLATFORM_ADMIN` alone by attrition** (vs 31 that name it deliberately). Neither existing instrument could see this — the authorization ratchet counts a role guard as *guarded*, and `GRANT_TIGHTNESS.md` joins **permissions**, which these name none of. Worst ghosts: `CRO` 72 sites, `TPRM_LEAD` 45, `AUDIT_COMMITTEE_MEMBER` 43, `BOARD_MEMBER` 41, `CIO` 41. **First module fixed:** `audit_lc/ia_plan` — `IaController`'s 26 endpoints recited 12 roles of which 5 are ghosts, leaving **11 PLATFORM_ADMIN-only**, so *an internal auditor could not create the annual audit plan, build the audit universe, or transition either* (live **403**). Converted to `AUDIT:READ/WRITE/DELETE` — the permission the module's own two sibling controllers already used — plus **V326** so CISO/RISK_MANAGER/COMPLIANCE_OFFICER keep the read they had. Live **22/22 before → 22/22 after**, 5 legs flipping, the *before* run taken with the controller stashed. Two permanent instruments: `RoleGuardConversionRatchetTest` (647 → 621 → 582 → **572**, may only decrease) and `Appendix/ROLE_GUARD_CENSUS.md`. **Slice 3 (`fraud`) added the lesson the first two did not:** its ten guards lived in **two constants**, so the whole module was one edit wide — which is why reflection found it (a source regex had already missed 8 constant-valued guards in F2-27) and why `FraudControllerGuardTest` pins each endpoint to a **tier** rather than asserting the constant is permission-shaped, an assertion that would still pass if `#close` were re-pointed at the read constant (mutation-proven: 2 of 4 tests fire). `FRAUD` was also the first module with **no permission to inherit** — absent from `iam.permissions` entirely, so V328 had to create it. ⚠ **And the conversion was not finished at the controller:** `AttachmentAccessPolicy` (SCR-004) had faithfully mirrored the old guard into its `FRAUD_CASE` entry, ghosts included, so converting the controller alone would have let a `GRC_ADMINISTRATOR` open a fraud case and then **403 on its attachments** — the exact failure that class's own union rule warns about. Moved together, and live-proven with a purpose-built single-role user, because no seeded persona isolates it (`compliance` holds `COMPLIANCE_OFFICER` too, so it satisfied the old role list anyway). The existing `AttachmentAccessPolicyTest` had been asserting that `ROLE_FRAUD_INVESTIGATOR` **grants** fraud-attachment access — a green test over a path no real token could take. ⚠ **Do not seed the 40 missing roles to make the old guards work** — the permission catalogue already carries the intent. ⬅ **Slice 5 (`board_pack`) inverted the finding's own premise, and it is the most transferable result so far.** Every earlier slice found ghosts *narrowing* an endpoint to PLATFORM_ADMIN by attrition. Here the read guard named six roles, **five of them ghosts** (CRO, BOARD_MEMBER, AUDIT_COMMITTEE_CHAIR, AUDIT_COMMITTEE_MEMBER, COMPANY_SECRETARY) — but it ended `or hasAuthority('REPORTING_READ')`, and `REPORTING:READ` is held by **20 roles including `VIEWER`**. So the dead names did not lock anyone out; they made a **wide** guard *look* narrow, directly above a comment asserting reads are "gated to a board/governance audience, not any authenticated principal". Live before the fix: **`viewer` read all 16 board packs, 200** — every audience (BOARD, AUDIT_COMMITTEE, RISK_COMMITTEE, CYBER_COMMITTEE, EXEC) and all 7 APPROVED + 2 PUBLISHED. **A ghost role is not always a lockout; sometimes it is camouflage** — so the census must be read for *both* directions, and a mixed guard (`hasAnyRole(…) or hasAuthority(…)`) is the shape where the two hide each other. My own note going in said this endpoint was "administrator-only", which measuring refuted. Converted to `ADMIN:READ`/`ADMIN:WRITE` — no migration, both already granted. **The write tier was evidence-led:** `ADMIN:WRITE` is exactly {BOARD_SECRETARY, GRC_ADMINISTRATOR, ORG_ADMIN}, and **4 of the 16 existing packs record `drafted_by='board.secretary@tss.com.sa'`** — the persona the old guard could never have admitted, since REPORTING:WRITE is only the two administrators and the intended executive author was the ghost `CRO`. ➕ **And the same file carried an F2-22b(vi) miss:** that slice stamped `reviewedBy`/`approvedBy` from the principal *on the transition path* and left `draftedBy` `@NotBlank` on **create**, so authorship was forgeable — proven by creating a pack claiming `draftedBy="IMPERSONATED-admin"`. It matters more than usual because `board_pack.packs` has **no `created_by`/`created_at` columns at all**, making `drafted_by` the only authorship record. Now `READ_ONLY` + stamped, live-proven with `FORGED-*` inputs on 3 accounts. ⬜ Two residual product calls recorded, not guessed: `audience` is stored on every pack and **never filters anything**, and no catalogued permission's population *is* "the board" (ADMIN:READ also admits SUPPLIER_MANAGER and REGULATOR_VIEWER) | L | 🔶 **census + ratchet DONE; 4 modules fully converted (`audit_lc`, `tprm`, `fraud`, `board_pack`) + `integrations` partially (its 4 defective sites). ✔ **2026-08-06: ghosts 14 → 11 across two more slices, and the census is now gated.** **(a) `platform/help`** — `CONTENT_EDITOR` is a ghost, so authoring every help topic was PLATFORM_ADMIN-only by attrition, and the class javadoc claimed `COMPLIANCE_OFFICER` was an author when it was only on the *list* guard: measured **200 on the whole inventory, 403 on every write**. Converted to `hasPermission('KB','WRITE')` — the permission the sibling `KbArticleController` already uses, **no migration**. ⚠ I nearly filed *“KB has WRITE and no READ”* as a gap; ADR-017 D-7 records it as a **decision**, amended after reading the corpus, because guidance readable only by permission-holders is worse than none. **(b) `bcm` crisis room** — both `active-summary` endpoints named `CRISIS_COMMANDER` and `INCIDENT_COMMANDER`, neither of which exists, **while the platform ships `CRISIS_MANAGER`** and the crisis room excluded it. Live **403 → 200 on both URLs** for a CRISIS_MANAGER-only user; `VIEWER` (a BCM:READ holder) stays **403** both before and after. `INCIDENT_COMMANDER` was **dropped, not substituted**: the nearest name, `EMERGENCY_RESPONDER`, is *“Emergency Responder (Break-glass)”* and putting break-glass on a routine command-loop read would conflate an override with daily access. 🔑 **It stays a role guard on purpose, measured**: `BCM:READ` is **17 roles including VIEWER and REGULATOR_VIEWER** (4 → 17 is a widening, not a conversion) and `BCM:APPROVE` excludes CISO. The mobile façade now **references** the canonical constant so the two cannot drift. ⬜ Residual recorded, not guessed: a `BCM:COMMAND`-style action is an owner call on the catalogue vocabulary — and [[F2-150]], filed here, is why the guard cannot be entity-bound instead. **(c)** `ROLE_GUARD_CENSUS.md` had drifted **19 ghosts** (32/66/561 published vs 13/56/532 enforced) because it says *“generated … do not hand-edit”* and nothing ran it; regenerated and gated on its four hermetic figures | **V326**, **V327**, **V328** |
| **F2-53** | found in F2-52's section E | **Knowledge-base authoring is PLATFORM_ADMIN-only, because the permission it reaches for is not a permission.** `KbArticleController#create/#update/#transition` guard on `hasRole('PLATFORM_ADMIN') or hasAuthority('KB_WRITE')`. **`KB` is not a module in `iam.permissions`** — there is no `KB_WRITE` to hold, so that leg can never be true, and `hasAuthority` does not go through `ModulePermissionEvaluator`, so nothing rescues it. Live-measured: `compliance` (**GRC_ADMINISTRATOR, 86 of 96 permissions**) → **403**; `auditor` → **403**; `admin` → **200/204**, passing on the *other* leg. Reads are `isAuthenticated()`, so every user sees the knowledge base and nobody but the platform administrator can write an article — and the persona for it, `CONTENT_EDITOR`, is one of F2-52's 40 ghost roles, so the intent is recorded twice and implementable neither way. ⚠ **This is why the census reports raw authorities separately:** the three sites were initially classified *deliberately* admin-only, because the unsatisfiable leg was spelled as an authority rather than as a role name — corrected, 29 → 32 attrition. Fix is **not** a guard edit: catalogue `KB:READ/WRITE` (or fold knowledge into an existing module's permission), grant it, then repoint — plus a decision on who authors knowledge articles. Same shape for the other 6 raw-authority sites, which are alive but bypass the admin bypass (`BoardPackController#transition` = 2 holders, PLATFORM_ADMIN **not** among them) | S | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-07-30 — `KB:WRITE` catalogued (V329)**, granted to `GRC_ADMINISTRATOR`, `ORG_ADMIN` and **`COMPLIANCE_OFFICER`** — the third being the point: without it the fix only moves authoring from the *platform* administrator to the *tenant* administrator, and neither authors PDPL/ECC/NDMO/Nazaha guidance. ⚠ **`KB:READ` deliberately NOT created, and ADR-017 D-7 is amended to say so** — the ADR first proposed granting it broadly, then reading the corpus refuted that: all 22 articles are PUBLISHED/INTERNAL staff guidance including *“Whistleblowing Channel — How to Report”* and *“Disclosing a Conflict of Interest (Nazaha)”*. **A whistleblowing procedure readable only by permission-holders is worse than none**; `isAuthenticated()` cannot wrongly exclude an employee, a grant list can only ever wrongly exclude one. So reads stay open, are **pinned by test**, and V329's 4th verification THROWs if `KB:READ` ever appears (a catalogued permission guarding no endpoint is F2-13b's anti-pattern — 15 already exist). **Not every role guard should become a permission; this is the counter-example.** ➕ **And measuring it found a defect this row never mentioned:** the by-slug route had **no status filter**, so any authenticated caller who guessed a slug — guessable by construction, `whistleblowing-channel-how-to-report` — could read an **unpublished draft**, including an author's in-progress revision of that very procedure. The list endpoint hid it by never showing drafts. Now 404 for everyone but `KB:WRITE` holders (404 not 403: whether an unpublished slug exists is itself information). Live **20/20**, ratchet 568→**565**. ⬜ Still open: **`visibility` is stored on every article and read by nothing** (`@Pattern(INTERNAL|PUBLIC|RESTRICTED)`) — board_pack's `audience` shape again; all 22 rows are INTERNAL so there is no live exposure | **V329** |
| **F2-54** | found running the council's look-back, 2026-07-30 | **The audit ledger records a permission where it claims to record a role — on every row.** Seat A's recommended look-back (*"after 24/59, internal audit's first question is: was it exercised?"*) was runnable because `ExportAuditInterceptor` covers `/api/**` — and running it produced a defect nobody had named. `platform.audit_log.user_role` holds **16 distinct values across 5,801 rows, of which ZERO are role names**: 13 are *permission* names (`BCM_APPROVE`, `SUST_READ`, `IAM_DELETE`, `COMPLIANCE_DELETE`…) and 3 are `ROLE_`-prefixed authorities (`ROLE_CISO`, `ROLE_INTERNAL_AUDITOR`, `ROLE_FRAUD_INVESTIGATOR`). It is **non-deterministic**: `admin` appears under **6 different `user_role` values**, `so_officer` under 4, `rmanager` and `auditor` under 3 — so the code is taking an arbitrary element from the authority collection. **Why it matters more than a cosmetic column:** this platform sells a SAMA/NCA/CMA-grade audit trail, and the ledger mis-attributes the actor's role on every entry — an examiner reconciling "who did this" against the role matrix gets an answer that is not merely wrong but *differently wrong each time*. Fix: record the resolved role set (or the primary role), pin it with a test that asserts every recorded value is in `iam.roles`, and decide whether to relabel history or leave it labelled-unreliable (the DF-3/V322 precedent says label, do not fabricate). ➕ The look-back's substantive result, stated honestly: **72 board-pack exports exist, 37 by `admin` and 4 by `viewer` on 2026-07-26 — and that `viewer` traffic is almost certainly my own F2-27-window probing, not misuse.** No evidence of unauthorised use in dev. The item that carries forward is running the same query **on prod** ✅ **DONE 2026-07-30, and it came back clean**: `TSS_GRC_Deployment` holds **1** board-pack export — `admin`, org 1, 2026-07-08 — out of 33 EXPORT events (risk-register 12, packet 7, evidence-package 7, ai-model-registry 3, regulator-package 2, cyber-incidents 1). No unauthorised use, so no PDPL art. 20 assessment is triggered. ⚠ **But F2-54 itself reproduces on prod on that very row**: its `user_role` reads `BCM_APPROVE` — a permission, not a role — so the one board-pack export production holds is mis-attributed. The remaining work is the fix, not the look-back ✅ **DONE 2026-08-01 — `V339`, shipped with `F2-31` as one slice** (same table, one probe session). Re-measured first, and the finding held in both databases: dev **6,065 rows / 16 distinct values / ZERO role names**, prod **3,989 / 12 / ZERO**. `resolveRoles()` keeps only `ROLE_*` authorities, strips the prefix, de-duplicates and **sorts** — and the sort is the load-bearing half, because an unordered value makes two disagreeing rows indistinguishable from a real role change between them. Every row also carries `user_role_source` (`RESOLVED` / `RESOLVED_TRUNCATED` / `NO_ROLE_AUTHORITY` / `NO_PRINCIPAL` / `SUPPRESSED_BY_DESIGN`), because *"no role recorded"* and *"role deliberately not recorded"* are different facts — and the whistleblower portal, **the second and only other writer of this table**, now stamps the latter, so the one place that withholds identity on purpose stops looking like a gap. History is **labelled, never rewritten**: 3,317 rows → `LEGACY_UNRECORDED`, 2,748 → `LEGACY_AUTHORITY_SAMPLE`. Suite **2,727/0/0** (+10), live **26/26** — `so_officer` records all **three** of its roles sorted (`CISO,SECURITY_ANALYST,SUSTAINABILITY_OFFICER`), identical across two calls, every component present in `iam.roles`, none permission-shaped. ⚠ **Two corrections to this row's own prescription:** it said *"the resolved role set (or the primary role)"* — there is no such thing as a primary role here, `so_officer` holds three and the four-eyes flows turn on which acted, so *a* role was never an acceptable answer. And CLAUDE.md describes `so_officer` as the CISO + SUSTAINABILITY_OFFICER persona; `iam.user_roles` says three, and the table is the authority. 🔑 **The keeper is what the fix declined to do:** a permission does not determine which role the actor held, so back-filling real roles would be fabrication — **a ledger that edits its own past to look clean is worth less than one that admits a gap** | S | ✅ **V339** |
| **F2-55** | found refuting a council claim, 2026-07-30 | **The board pack is not board MI — it is an operational extract with a board cover.** Measured while checking Seat A's claim that the pack leaks named AML alerts (it does not — ethics and AML enter as counts only): `reporting.BoardPackService` pulls `riskRepo.findByOrganisationId(org, PageRequest.of(0, **200**))` and `findingRepo.findByOrganisationId(org, PageRequest.of(0, **1000**))`. So a single download carries **up to 200 itemised risks and up to 1,000 itemised audit findings**. Boards receive aggregated MI — counts, trends, aging, exceptions — and detail stays module-scoped behind the owning module's permission. **This is why re-tiering D-10a is necessary but not sufficient:** narrowing *who* may pull the pack does not fix *what is in it*, and the two page-limits are the reason the pack is worth over-claiming to begin with. Also a latent 1,200-row synchronous aggregate on a request thread. Decide the board-MI cut with the same rigour as the audience question, then bound the page sizes | S | ❌ **REFUTED 2026-07-31 — this finding was wrong, and wrong in an instructive way.** Those are the **page sizes fetched**, not the payload. Read further into the same two methods: risks are fetched 200, sorted by inherent score, then `subList(0, 20)` — the pack renders **twenty**. Findings are fetched 1,000 and reduced to **four aging counters** plus a table that exits on `++rowCount >= 15` — the pack renders **fifteen**. So the board pack is **20 ranked risks + aging tiers + the 15 oldest open findings**, which is precisely the bounded MI shape this finding argued a board should receive. Its headline conclusion — *"not board MI, an operational extract with a board cover"* — is refuted by the code it cites. **The error class is the one worth keeping:** I measured the `PageRequest` and reported it as the document, without reading what happened to the rows afterwards — the same mistake as counting `@RequireModule` with a grep that also matched a Javadoc mention. A `findByOrganisationId(org, PageRequest.of(0, N))` tells you the fetch, never the render. ⚠ **It travelled before it was checked:** the wrong figure reached V332's comment block and two commit messages (`67294f60`, `b8d4020f`) before anyone read past the query. V332's comment is corrected in place — safe only because Flyway has not applied it to prod yet, so there is no checksum to invalidate. **D-10a is unaffected**: it rests on 24 of 57 production roles holding a board-confidential document, which is true whatever the row count. ➕ What survives from this finding is real but different, and is now **F2-58**: the fetch-then-trim pattern makes the *ranking* unsound, and there are **two** renderers doing it independently |
| **F2-56** | found auditing this plan's own completeness, 2026-07-30 | **The authorisation model is single-tenant in practice: tenant 1 reaches 59 roles, tenants 2 and 3 reach 12.** `iam.roles` holds **47 of 59 roles at `organisation_id = 1`** and only **12 global (`NULL`)**. The 12 a newly-onboarded tenant can actually reach are `BCM_MANAGER, CHANGE_MANAGER, COMPLIANCE_OFFICER, DPO, EMERGENCY_RESPONDER, FRAUD_INVESTIGATOR, INTERNAL_AUDITOR, ORG_ADMIN, PLATFORM_ADMIN, RISK_ANALYST, RISK_MANAGER, VIEWER` — **`CISO`, `GRC_ADMINISTRATOR`, `BOARD_SECRETARY`, `CYBER_MANAGER` and `EXTERNAL_AUDITOR` are not among them.** ⚠ **This is not a seed-data curiosity; it silently breaks grant migrations, including ones already merged.** Every grant this programme has shipped resolves its target by `role_name`, which matches the org-1 row — so V320/V321/V329's grants exist for tenant 1 and **for no other tenant**. **It also falsifies D-8's grant design before D-8 is written:** `INTEGRATIONS:READ → ORG_ADMIN, GRC_ADMINISTRATOR, CISO, CYBER_MANAGER` collapses to **`ORG_ADMIN` alone** for tenants 2–3, which is precisely the outcome ADR-017 D-8 examined and rejected (*"`PLATFORM:WRITE` is `ORG_ADMIN` alone and would drop the CISO who runs the integrations"*). Same trap for D-10a: stripping or keeping `SUPPLIER_MANAGER`/`EXTERNAL_AUDITOR` is a no-op outside tenant 1. ➕ **V330 got this right by accident of being newest** — `FRAUD_INVESTIGATOR` was created `organisation_id NULL`, so the one role this programme authored is the one that works for every tenant; the 47 inherited from the Python bootstrap are the problem. Decide the rule (system personas global, customer-defined roles tenant-scoped is the SailPoint/Saviynt shape), then make every grant migration state which it targets — and add a test, because a grant that silently reaches one tenant is F2-23's shape at tenant scope. Also: **all 7 dev users are org 1**, so no probe has ever exercised a second tenant's authorisation | M | ✅ **DONE + MERGED 2026-07-31** (**V334**, suite **2692/0/0**, FE **76/76**, live **20/20**). **46 → 0 tenant-scoped system roles; 12 → 58 global; D-8's grant targets 1/4 → 4/4.** 🔑 **The rule did not need deciding — the schema already declared it.** This row said "decide the rule (system personas global, customer-defined tenant-scoped is the SailPoint/Saviynt shape)". That rule is already `is_system_role`: `deleteRole` refuses to retire one ("System roles are templates"), the UI calls null-org roles read-only templates, and **exactly one of the 59 rows is non-system** (`REVIEW_TEST_ROLE`, soft-deleted, present in prod too). The 46 rows simply contradicted the model the rest of the code assumes. ⚠ **What this row did NOT anticipate, and what measuring first caught: `mustOwnRole` refused every write to a global role — for EVERYONE, `PLATFORM_ADMIN` included.** So the permission-matrix editor was *already* dead for the 12 roles that were global, and a naive flip would have killed it for all 58. Live-probed before compiling: PUT returned **400** for BCM_MANAGER, CHANGE_MANAGER, COMPLIANCE_OFFICER, DPO, EMERGENCY_RESPONDER, FRAUD_INVESTIGATOR and **200** for the org-1 roles. ➕ That refusal is *correct* — a shared role is one row for every tenant — so the fix was to give it the platform scope it lacked, not to remove it: `PLATFORM_ADMIN` may administer a shared role, a tenant may not, and a tenant needing a variant **clones**. **`SanctionsListService.assertWritable` already applies exactly this rule** to platform-global screening lists ("visible is not writable", SEC-FIN-003) — `iam`, the module whose job is authorisation, had never adopted it. `POST /roles/{id}/clone` is new because the refusal message had recommended cloning since the day it was written and **the only occurrence of "clone" anywhere in `iam` was that message**. ➕ **First second-tenant authorisation probe on this platform** (this row's own closing complaint): a throwaway org-2 user, created and removed, authenticates and sees **58 roles where it would have seen 12**, can appoint all five previously unreachable personas, and cannot see org 1's private clone. ➕ V334 asserts **invariants, not counts** — dev ends at 58 global and prod at 56, so a hardcoded number would fail on prod for a non-defect. ➕ A dead check was deleted **because the probe disagreed with the test**: a same-name guard on clone could never fire, and its unit test passed only by mocking a repository state that cannot occur. ➡ **This unblocks D-8** (its grant list no longer collapses to `ORG_ADMIN` outside tenant 1) and removes the "⚠ Read F2-56 first" caveat from D-10a, F2-59 and W2-0 |
| **F2-57** | found ratifying the SKU catalogue, 2026-07-30 | **Two of the fifteen SKUs have entitlement boundaries that are not package boundaries — and F2-01's registry, as specified, cannot express either.** ADR-017 D-1 made board governance and access governance **paid**. Measurement then showed where their code actually lives: `BOARD` = `board_pack` (6 files) **+ `admin/committee` (51 files, inside a kernel package)**, and `ACCESS_GOV` = **`iam/governance` (22 files) and no top-level package at all**. `@RequireModule` is a class/method annotation, so *enforcement* is fine. The problem is everything keyed on packages: **F2-01's module registry (one row per package, one SKU per row) would have to file `admin` and `iam` under a paid SKU or file the paid SKUs under Base** — both wrong — and **ADR-011's schema-ownership rule has the same shape**. ➕ A third instance, worse because it is already shipped: **`reporting.BoardPackService` and the two `board-pack.*` export endpoints are `BOARD` content inside a Base package**, so the flagship paid deliverable is served from an unlicensable module. F2-08a's toolkit lift must separate them or `BOARD` is unenforceable at the exact surface the buyer opens. Fix: make the registry key an **entitlement unit** (package *or* declared sub-package), stated as rule R6 in the catalogue so F2-01 is not designed wrong; then decide whether `admin/committee` and `iam/governance` are promoted to top-level packages (cleaner, costs a refactor + schema question) or stay as declared carve-outs (cheaper, permanently non-uniform) | S | ✔ **DONE 2026-08-06** (no migration). ⚠ **Half of this row was already true and the other half was the real defect.** The *enforcement* half is solved and has been since ADR-018 R6: `SkuRegistry.CARVE_OUTS` maps `admin.committee → BOARD` and `iam.governance → ACCESS_GOV`, consulted **before** the package-keyed map because the parent is Base in both cases. What was missing is what the row actually names: **the registry could not express it, and no test consulted that map.** `theHomingAgreesWithSkuRegistryInBothDirections` compares **top-level packages only**, so a carve-out could have been added or deleted in code with nothing noticing, while `module-registry.yaml` went on declaring `admin: homing: base` over **51 files of paid BOARD content**. 🔑 **A register that cannot express a truth reads as asserting its absence** — the same shape as F2-140 (tables no entity maps) and F2-141 (Arabic columns no entity maps), where an instrument's silence was read as a clean result. **Fix:** a `carve_outs` field on the two affected entries, asserted against `CARVE_OUTS` in **both directions** — deliberately *asserted*, never re-declared, because a registry that restates the homing becomes the second authority it exists to prevent (RC-A). ➕ It also asks this programme's standing question, *does the thing it names exist?* — a carve-out whose sub-package is not a directory is a paid boundary declared over no code, and that is checked on disk. **Mutation-proven 3/3**, the third needing isolation: renaming the sub-package in the YAML alone trips the *agreement* assertion and masks the directory check, so both sides had to be mutated consistently to prove it fires. Suite **3,347/0/0** |
| **F2-58** | found sizing D-5, 2026-07-31 | **The board pack is computed twice by two renderers that can disagree, and both rank a paged sample rather than the register.** Measured: `reporting.BoardPackService` (554 lines, **35** repository call sites, 11 repositories + `SustainabilityDashboardService`) and `reporting.BoardPackPptxService` (406 lines, **32** call sites, **8 of the same repositories**) each build the whole aggregate independently. There is no shared aggregate object — `BoardPackService` exposes only `generatePdf(String)` returning `byte[]`, and every `render*` method queries and renders in one pass. So the PDF and the PPTX deck **of the same board pack** are two implementations of one document and are free to drift; they already differ on population (PDF top **20** risks, PPTX top **10**) and on method (PPTX derives `openFindings` by fetching 1,000 rows and filtering in Java; the PDF builds aging counters from its own 1,000-row fetch; neither uses a `count` query, and the PPTX issues a 1,000-row page purely to read `getTotalElements()`). ➕ **The sharper defect: `findByOrganisationId(org, PageRequest.of(0, 200))` carries no `Sort`.** The 200 rows are whatever order SQL Server returns; the code then sorts *that page* by inherent score and takes the top 20. So **"Risk Register — Top 20" is the top 20 of an arbitrary 200-row sample**, not of the register — and a board receiving it has no way to tell. Same shape for findings at 1,000. ⚠ **Latent, not live, and the distinction is the point:** measured on **both** databases, the largest tenant holds **51 risks** (cap 200) and **41 findings** (cap 1,000) — dev and prod identical. Nothing is wrong in any board pack today. It goes wrong silently at risk 201, with no error and no log line, which is the worst way for a board report to fail. **This is D-5's central design constraint, which is why it was found before D-5 was built:** exposing the aggregate as JSON means choosing one of the two implementations, so the DTO extraction must **collapse both renderers onto one `buildAggregate()`** — and it is worthless if it enshrines the paged-sample ranking. Fix: rank in the database, or count with `count` queries; then have PDF, PPTX and JSON render one aggregate | M | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-31 — correctness half @`859efb2f`, duplication half @`b99a3d53` (D-5).** Ranking now happens in SQL in **both** renderers: `PageRequest.of(0, 20 or 10, Sort.by(DESC,"inherentScore").and(ASC,"id"))`. The id tiebreaker is new and deliberate — several risks share a score and the old in-memory sort broke ties by the arbitrary page order, so the pack could name a different 20th risk on two runs over unchanged data. Aging tiers now come from a new `countOpenByOrganisationIdOlderThan` over **every** open finding, and the 15-row table from `findOldestOpenByOrganisationId`, which excludes CLOSED in SQL — the old page counted only open rows toward its 15 but paged over both, so a tenant whose first 1,000 findings were mostly closed could render fewer than 15 while older open ones sat unlisted. PPTX `totalRisks` / `openFindings` / `closed` moved to `count` queries (one had been issuing a 1,000-row page purely to read `getTotalElements()`). ➕ **Proven live at the volume that matters, not asserted:** 210 `[REVIEW-TEST]` risks seeded to put org 1 at **261**, arranged so that **0 of the 9 highest-scoring risks fell inside the unordered 200-row window** — i.e. the old code would have rendered a board "Top 20" containing **none** of the worst risks. The generated PDF and PPTX were then parsed (pypdf / slide XML): all five apex risks present in both, no score-1 filler, aging **2/8/0/28**. Seed removed with `CHARINDEX`; org counts back to 51/10/10. ⚠ **Two of my own reference values were wrong and the code was right — both worth keeping.** (i) The aging reference omitted **`is_deleted = 0`**, which the entity's `@Where` enforces and which exactly one finding in the 30–59 tier is subject to — landmine 6 in CLAUDE.md, and it surfaced only because I trusted the code over my expectation instead of "fixing" the code to match. (ii) Three hand-computed cutoff dates were each a day out; the property test arbitrated. `cutoff()` is **shared, not duplicated** — same package, so the PPTX calls `BoardPackService.cutoff`. `BoardPackAgingCutoffTest` (3 tests) pins the day-vs-timestamp boundary across 4×131×3 cases, because `now().minusDays(N)` is the natural translation and is off by one for any finding created later in the day than the report runs. ✅ **The duplication half shipped too, in D-5 @`b99a3d53` (2026-07-31)** — both renderers now render one `BoardPackAggregateService.build(Scope)`, so the 35 and 32 independent call sites are gone and the PDF, the deck and the new JSON aggregate cannot disagree. **F2-58 is fully closed.** |
| **F2-59** | found scoping D-10b, 2026-07-31 | **An external supervisory authority holds a permanent, unscoped, self-service credential to extract eleven registers.** `REGULATOR_VIEWER` holds **11 `*:EXPORT` permissions in dev and 10 in prod** — RISK, BCM, CYBER, COMPLIANCE, INCIDENT, SUST, NDI, WORKFLOW, CHANGE, REPORTING, REGULATORY. Saudi supervisory practice is **requisition-driven**: SAMA, CMA and NCA inspections create an obligation to *furnish on request* through a coordinator, minuted and scoped to the requisition (BCL art. 18; Capital Market Law inspection powers; NCA ECC assessment). None of them creates a standing seat inside the entity's GRC system, and no market-leading GRC platform ships one — ServiceNow, Archer and MetricStream all furnish through a governed disclosure package or a time-boxed scoped grant. Under **PDPL** it is worse than untidy: several of those registers carry personal data (ethics cases, DSRs, roster seats), so a standing bulk-extract right held by an external party with no per-disclosure lawful-basis record is a disclosure the controller cannot account for. ➕ **The exposure is latent, and that is what makes it fixable:** the role has **zero holders in both databases**, so revocation has zero collateral — the exact inverse of D-10a, where measurement is what *stopped* a revocation. ⚠ **But it cannot be revoked first.** `regulator-package.zip` — the governed channel — is guarded by `COMPLIANCE:EXPORT or REPORTING:EXPORT`, both of which this role holds, so a blanket revoke would remove the compliant path along with the raw ones. F2-60 must land first. ➕ Second defect in the same measurement: both `REGULATOR_VIEWER` and `EXTERNAL_AUDITOR` are defined **only for organisation 1**, so **2 of 3 tenants cannot appoint an external auditor** (F2-56's tenant-scoping, now with a named consequence) | M | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-31** (**V335**, dev ×2, second pass 0 changes). **11 `*:EXPORT` → 1.** Only `REGULATORY:EXPORT` survives — the governed `regulator-package.zip` channel V333/F2-60 created — and all 13 `*:READ` grants survive, because reading a register on screen during an inspection is the normal supervisory posture while extracting all eleven as files without a requisition is not. **Collateral measured at zero in both databases: 0 holders**, the exact inverse of A17-D10a where the same kind of measurement *stopped* a revocation. Platform-wide grants 1496 → 1486, so exactly ten rows went and nothing else. ➕ **The revoke is expressed as a rule, not a list** (`action='EXPORT' AND module <> 'REGULATORY'`), which cannot drift out of sync with its own checks and covers any module catalogued later. ⚠ **A landmine in this repo's own notes is wrong and cost two failed attempts:** the house note says mixed Latin1-vs-UTF8 → `COLLATE DATABASE_DEFAULT`, but measured here the **database** default is `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS` while `iam.permissions.module`/`iam.roles.role_name` are explicitly `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_SC_UTF8` — so `DATABASE_DEFAULT` resolves to the **wrong** collation. A bare literal compared against a column takes that column's collation; when a set must survive across statements, carry **ids (BIGINT)**, never text. ➕ Noted for F2-61(c), not actioned: the role also holds `WORKFLOW:WRITE`, but that permission sits in 55 of 58 roles, so it is a grant-table breadth problem rather than a property of this persona, and fixing it here would pre-empt the amendment in a slice whose subject is exports |

This is **D-10a's exact shape on a larger artifact** — the board pack was 24 of 59 and carried one tenant's risks and findings; this bundles the requirement-to-control mapping, the evidence index **and the audit trail** into a single hash-manifested download, which is the whole assurance position of the tenant. Third instance of the pattern, so it is a pattern and not a one-off. ✅ **The instrument is measured and it fits:** `REGULATORY:EXPORT` already exists and is held by **14 roles**, and that population is almost exactly the assurance audience — AUDITOR, EXTERNAL_AUDITOR, INTERNAL_AUDITOR, REGULATOR_VIEWER, NCA_COMPLIANCE, COMPLIANCE_OFFICER, DPO, LEGAL_COUNSEL, LEGAL_REVIEWER, CISO, EXECUTIVE_REVIEWER, ORG_ADMIN, GRC_ADMINISTRATOR (+ VIEWER, which is one wrong grant row rather than a 23-role over-grant).
