| **F2-67** | found hardening F2-62, 2026-07-31 | **Template lifecycle protection was one method wide: a system role could not be deleted, but could be deactivated — and since F2-62 those are nearly the same act.** `deleteRole` refuses a system role outright (*"templates are templates and cannot be deleted"*) and implements retirement as `setIsActive(false)` + `softDelete`. `updateRole` refuses only the **rename** of a system role, so `isActive=false` passed straight through. Before F2-62 that cost nothing, because the flag enforced nothing; **after F2-62 it strips every holder's `ROLE_` marker and every permission on the next request** — nearly the whole blast radius of the deletion the very next method forbids. So deactivation was **the unguarded half of an operation whose other half is explicitly protected**, and F2-62 is what armed it. ⚠ **Not exploitable in either database today, and the reason is not a defence:** every system role is global after V334/F2-56 and `mustOwnRole` rejects global roles before the guard is reached — so the protection currently rests on **the shape of the data, not on design**. Production carried **46 tenant-scoped system roles until V334 ran minutes before this was written**, and it had acquired them by being *restored from a dev dump*, which a future restore can repeat. Under that shape, deactivating `PLATFORM_ADMIN` would have stripped the admin bypass from every holder with **no administrator left to reverse it** — the reason this is worth guarding explicitly rather than leaving to `mustOwnRole` and luck. ➡ **Fix — the invariant is "a system role is never inactive", enforced at **every** writer of the flag rather than only the reachable one, because "unreachable given today's rows" is the reasoning that left this open in the first place.** There are three: `updateRole` refuses `isActive=false` on any `is_system_role` row, pointing the caller at F2-56's `cloneRole` — the copy is deliberately non-system, which is exactly the population F2-62's semantics were designed for; the `createRole` **revive** branch refuses the same (reaching it needs a *retired* system role, which no API path can create since `deleteRole` refuses to retire a template, and which exists in neither database — belt-and-braces, stated as such in code); and the fresh-role path cannot break it at all because it forces `isSystemRole=false`. **Reactivation (`isActive=true`) stays allowed**: it can only ever restore access, and refusing it would make a template that somehow became inactive unrecoverable through the API. **A null flag still means "leave it alone"** — guarding on `!Boolean.TRUE.equals(...)` instead of `Boolean.FALSE.equals(...)` would have turned every ordinary display-name edit on a template into a 400, which is why that case is pinned by its own test. | S | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-31** on `fix/f2-67-system-role-deactivation` — **no migration** (service guard only; the data is already correct in both databases). Suite **2,708/0/0**. **Seven tests added to F2-56's existing `AccessGovernanceRoleScopeTest` rather than a new class** (same service, same subject — a second ratchet class over one rule is the duplication D-5 already had to delete once), and they use a **tenant-scoped** system role deliberately: a global one is stopped earlier by `mustOwnRole`, so testing that shape would have been vacuous. **Verified the guard bites: exactly 1 of the 5 `updateRole` tests fails with it removed** — the other four pin behaviour that already held (delete refused, tenant-owned still deactivatable, reactivation allowed, null flag a no-op), which is the correct result and the reason to check rather than assume. ➕ **Live-proven on :8094** — a spare port, so the `:8084` instance three sibling sessions were using was left alone — by seeding the shape the API cannot produce (`createRole` always sets `is_system_role=0`) as a `[REVIEW-TEST]` row: system deactivate **400**, reactivate **200**, display-name-only edit **200**, tenant-owned deactivate **200** *(confirmed `is_active=0` in the table, not just in the response)*, delete **400**. Cleanup by `CHARINDEX` restored the baseline exactly (58 live roles, 0 tenant-scoped, 0 inactive). ⚠ Landmine re-run: `JAVA_HOME` does not fix a bare `java` — the jar died on `UnsupportedClassVersionError` (class file 61 vs 55) until invoked as `"$JAVA_HOME/bin/java"`. |
| **F2-42** | found in W2-1a-ii | **Two board packs — the real one on the server, a fabricated one on screen.** `/board-pack` renders `buildBoardPack()` from 8 hardcoded arrays while `GET /api/v1/reporting/board-pack.pdf`/`.pptx` are built from 8 live repositories and already linked from the dashboard. Same class as PM-C1. **Fix is consolidation, not migration** — expose the server aggregate as JSON and render it, or retire the page. Sample-data banner shipped as the interim. Full 18-field spec in the register | M | ✔ **DONE — the row still read ⬜ READY on 2026-08-06 with the work already in the tree.** Verified rather than assumed: `buildBoardPack()` is gone, `lib/boardPackAggregate.ts` replaced it, and `boardPackProvenance.test.ts` asserts nothing calls the old builder — every surviving mention is prose explaining the history. ⚠ The register Status said OPEN too, so **both documents lagged the same change** (see the status-drift note below). Original text kept: **not decision-blocked, and this row was wrong about that.** ADR-017 is **Accepted** and its header names F2-42 among what it closes; §D-5 is titled *"F2-42: expose the server aggregate as JSON and render it; retire the fabricated page"*. The feed also already **shipped** with D-5 (`/board-pack.json`). What is left is an S–M engineering slice: render the JSON, delete the 8 fabricated arrays. ⚠ One consequence to accept with it — the JSON is guarded by `BOARD:EXPORT` (8 roles), so the screen's audience narrows from everyone who could open it to those 8. *(The trailing “blocked on a product decision” that used to end this cell was stale text left over from before the row was corrected — it contradicted the row's own first sentence, and it is removed.)* <br><br>✅ **DONE 2026-08-01** — branch `feat/f242-board-pack-aggregate`; backend **2779/0/0** (+3), FE **175/175** (+15), `tsc` clean, `vite build` clean; live **17/17** with a control group; **no migration**. ⚠ **The consolidation was NOT a swap, and this row assumed it was.** The two packs shared **2 of 10 sections**. The fabricated page showed appetite breaches, board approvals, audit engagements, PDPL 72h posture, CCM freshness, asset blast-radius, chain fires and a regulator tally; the server aggregate carries top risks, findings aging, oldest open findings, KRI bands, framework coverage, change outcomes, ethics and sustainability. So the sections on the page are now **different sections**, not the same ones re-sourced — *because the fabricated eight had no real data behind them to re-source from.* ADR-017 D-5 chose exactly this (“retire the fabricated page”), so the outcome is the ratified one; what was wrong was the expectation that it would be a field-for-field rewire. ⚠ **Worse than the row states, too.** `lib/boardPack.ts` imported from three modules *named* `mockCompliance`, `mockPrivacy` and `mockWorkflows`, plus a function called `synthesiseAllBreaches()`. “8 hardcoded arrays” undersells it: some of it was synthesised at render time. ➕ **Both of D-5's binding preconditions were checked before writing code, not assumed.** (i) *“D-5 must not ship before D-10a”* — D-10a landed 2026-07-30, so all three routes now carry `BOARD:EXPORT` (8 roles, down from 24 including `VIEWER` and `SUPPLIER_MANAGER`); shipping earlier would have replaced a **labelled mock with an unlabelled leak**. (ii) The JSON writes the export-audit record — already true via `ExportAuditContext.declareExport("json")`. F2-55, which D-5 flags, is **refuted**, so it blocks nothing. ➕ **The sample-data banner was removed, and only because the figures became real** — a test asserts the *pairing* (banner absent AND `fetchBoardPack` present), because banner removal alone is the dishonest half of this change. ⚠ **Deliberately NOT fetched through `useApiList`.** That hook substitutes supplied mock data whenever a request fails or returns zero rows. On a board pack that is **F2-42 rebuilt behind a different door** — so the page uses a plain fetch with explicit loading / **forbidden** / error states, and a test asserts the hook is absent. The error state shows *no figures* rather than estimated ones. ➕ **A 403 is rendered as the control working, not as an outage**, and the 403 branch is asserted to be checked *before* the generic error branch — one is a permissions conversation, the other a bug report. The nav entry carries a new per-item `gate: () => canExport('BOARD')`, so the 51 roles the server will refuse are not offered the link. **Entitlement and permission are independent gates** and a test pins that either alone can hide the row: `BOARD` the SKU answers *did this tenant buy it*, `BOARD:EXPORT` answers *may this user see it*. ➕ **Cross-language ratchet — the part that keeps it consolidated.** Consolidating once does not stay consolidated: the next field added to `BoardPackAggregate` is invisible to the screen, and the screen cannot notice, because JSON just carries a key nobody reads. Repeat that and the page is again a partial, quietly-stale view — which is how the divergence arose, one section at a time. `BoardPackWireContractTest` reflects **78 wire fields** across 10 nested records (including those reached only through a `List<>` generic) and fails if any is absent from `lib/boardPackAggregate.ts`. Mutation-proven by renaming one TS field: the failure names `topGapCount`. ⚠ **My own provenance test reproduced F2-04a's regex-phantom class — third instance in this programme.** It asserted `buildBoardPack(` and `useApiList` were absent from the page and **failed on the page's own javadoc**, which names both in prose explaining what was replaced. Fixed by stripping comments before matching, and by additionally asserting the prose *is* still present so the test cannot pass by someone deleting the explanation to satisfy it. **Live**: `/board-pack.json` → 200 with all 10 sections and real figures (51 risks, 3 critical, 38 open findings, 20 KRIs, 35 controls, 30 ethics cases, 1 STR, 10 AML alerts), server-formatted risk references (`R-00002`…); the browser renders **39 KPI tiles whose first nine values are byte-for-byte the API's `executive` block**, 11 sections, 4 tables, no banner. `viewer` (no `BOARD_EXPORT`) → **403**, the *Board-confidential* screen, **zero KPI tiles** (nothing leaked) and the nav entry gone (132 links vs 133); the same viewer still reads a Base endpoint at 200 (control group). `board-pack.pdf` still returns a real 40,405-byte PDF, so the JSON did not become a *third* board pack. **No migration** |
| **F2-30** | F-007 | Enforce the bilingual invariant `CLAUDE.md` already claims: `@NotBlank`/`nullable=false` on `*_ar` for material records, starting with `ChangeRequest.titleAr`; a test that fails when a material entity has a mandatory `*_en` and an optional `*_ar` | S | 🟡 **TRANCHE 1 DONE 2026-07-26** (no migration). Suite **2,525/0**, ratchet **157 asymmetric of 574 pairs**, live **6/6**, browser-verified. **Scope: 157 asymmetric pairs, not one** — and **zero entities in the platform carried a single `@NotBlank`**, so nothing checked house rule 7 at any layer. **The fix needed a prerequisite nobody had noticed:** the write paths that most need the constraint bind the JPA entity with no DTO and no `@Valid` (the F2-22a class), so the entity constraint is the only lever — and an entity constraint fails at *flush*, so Spring wraps it in `TransactionSystemException`, which **nothing handled**. `@NotBlank` alone would have turned "you forgot the Arabic title" into an opaque **500**; the pre-existing `handleConstraint` only catches the *unwrapped* `@Validated` case despite a javadoc claiming otherwise. Handler fixed first, then 8 fields annotated across 7 entities (change + committee governance backbone — **a board resolution can no longer be recorded in English only**). **35 pairs holding 293 blank-Arabic rows are deliberately NOT enforced** — bean validation fires on update, so the constraint would make those records uneditable, and who supplies the Arabic is a product decision. Enumerated with counts in the ratchet; blank rows by schema `risk` 130 · `sust` 74 · `evidence` 49 · `policy` 13. ⚠ **My source regex under-counted again** — 103/375 by grep vs **157/574** by reflection, the same error in the same direction as F2-27 the same day. F2-25's deferred `memberNameAr` stays deferred: that table has no `*_ar` column, so it needs a migration |

**Sequence:** F2-25 (2 h) → F2-26 (1 d) → **F2-28 (3–5 d)** → F2-27 (1 d) → F2-30 (S) → F2-29 (P2, later).
F2-28 depends on both roster items: the roster cannot be the quorum denominator while appointment 500s and
every seat is unlinked.

## §6c Change Enablement — a separate, properly structured module

**Owner's direction, 2026-07-25:** *"split change management to be a separate module, not under the risk
module, with more well structure including committee and CAB management and workflow."*

### First, the premise — measured, because it changes the size of the job

**Change management is already separate from Risk in the backend.** Verified:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Backend package | `module/change_mgmt` — its own top-level package, 2,157 LoC, 7 entities, 30 endpoints, 49 tests |
| Schema ownership | **all 7 entities write to `change_mgmt`** — no risk tables |
| `change_mgmt` → `risk` imports | **0** |
| `risk` → `change_mgmt` imports | **0** |
| Peer imports | `platform` 6, `admin` 3, `incident` 3 — **fan-out 3, fan-in 1**, one of the cleanest modules in the platform |
| Frontend route | `/changes` — a **top-level** route, not nested under `/risk` |

**What *is* under Risk is the navigation.** `tss-grc-ui-v2/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx:106` places
`{ id: 'changes', to: '/changes' }` inside the group commented `// ─── 1. Risk Management (CRO)`. So every
user opening the sidebar sees Change Management as a Risk sub-item, and that is a real product defect —
ITIL change enablement reports to a service-management or IT-governance function, not to the CRO.

The consequence is favourable: **this is an information-architecture and structuring job, not a backend
extraction.** There is no risk-coupling to unpick, no schema to move, no data migration. CE-0 (lift the nav
group) is hours. The substance of the work is the *internal* structure and the CAB/committee/workflow depth
— which is CE-1…CE-16, already specified and now elevated.

### Target structure

Backend, from
[`CHANGE_ENABLEMENT_MODULE_SEPARATION_AND_CAB_UPLIFT_PLAN.md`](CHANGE_ENABLEMENT_MODULE_SEPARATION_AND_CAB_UPLIFT_PLAN.md)
§5. The schema name **stays `change_mgmt`** — renaming a schema is destructive with no functional benefit.

```
module/change_mgmt/            <!-- ADR-017 D-4: the package name stays -->
├── package-info.java   module contract + allowed dependencies (the ADR-009 registry entry)
├── request/            the change record — ChangeRequest, ChangeComment, ChangeLink
├── lifecycle/          ChangeStateMachine (pure, no I/O) + guards:
│                       FreezeWindow, Conflict, PirRequired, CabDecision, ConditionsSatisfied
├── authority/          ChangeAuthorisationMatrix — type × risk → gate + min_approvals  (CH-17)
├── model/              change models / pre-approved standard-change templates      (CE-2)
├── cab/                ⭐ CAB profile, agenda, decisions, conditions, ECAB ratification (CE-3…CE-6)
├── schedule/           FSC · PSO · freeze windows · collision detection             (CE-8)
├── task/               ChangeTask + SLA scheduler                                   (CH-5, shipped)
├── pir/                post-implementation review                                   (CE-7)
├── assurance/          unauthorised-change reconciliation                           (CE-11)
├── analytics/          ITIL/ISO KPI pack + CAB throughput                           (CE-12)
└── spi/                ⭐ OUTBOUND PORTS — interfaces only, no peer imports
                        CommitteePort · IncidentPort · EvidencePort · WorkflowPort

module/change_adapters/ adapters live OUTSIDE the module, so neither side depends on the other
```

**The rule that makes the separation real (ADR-012):** `module/change_mgmt/**` may import only itself,
`common.*`, `security.*`, `platform.*` and its own `spi` interfaces — **never another `module.<peer>`**.
Enforced by `ChangeModuleBoundaryTest`, ratcheted. Today's 3 peer imports (`admin`, `incident`) become
2 ports plus 2 adapters.

### How committee, CAB and workflow relate — the three the owner named

These are three different things and the structure must keep them distinct:

| Concern | Owner | Relationship |
|---|---|---|
| **Committee engine** | `admin/committee` — **kernel**, in the mandatory Base Platform SKU | Generic: charters, member terms, meetings, attendance, quorum, resolutions, e-signed minutes, gate→committee routing. Consumed by **5** external packages — `bcm`, `change_mgmt`, `policy`, `risk`, `workflow` *(`grep -rl "import com.tss.grc.module.admin.committee" src/main/java`)*. ⚠ **An earlier revision of this row claimed "14 modules" — that was the *workflow*-engine adoption count transplanted into a sentence about the *committee* engine, an ADR-016 R5 violation committed the day ADR-016 was accepted.** The conclusion is unchanged and rests on a stronger fact: the **kernel workflow engine structurally depends on committee** — `WorkflowRequestService` implements `COMMITTEE` as a first-class recipient type with roster fan-out and the SLA scheduler reads `CommitteeMemberTerm` — so the engine cannot move into a commercial package without dragging the kernel with it. Four SKUs consume committee referrals |
| **CAB** | `module/change_mgmt/cab` — **the Change Enablement SKU** | A CAB *is* a committee with change-specific behaviour: a queue-driven agenda, change decisions, conditions, ECAB ratification. So `cab/` holds the change-specific profile and workflow and reaches the generic engine through **`CommitteePort`** — it does not reimplement quorum or minutes |
| **Workflow engine** | `module/workflow` — **kernel** | Change authorisations become workflow requests via **`WorkflowPort`** (CE-10, the 15th adopting module). The change module owns *what* needs authorising; the engine owns routing, SLA and escalation |

**The design decision, stated so it is not re-litigated:** CAB is a *profile over the committee engine*,
not a parallel implementation. The alternative — a self-contained CAB with its own membership, quorum and
minutes — was rejected because it duplicates the engine (F-014's defect class), doubles the surface where
quorum can be forged (F2-28), and would give the platform two answers to "was this decision quorate?".
Track D already routes change and policy approval through committees with quorum, SoD-recusal and e-signed
minutes; `cab/` builds on that.

### Frontend

`pages/changes/ChangesPage.tsx` splits into a **Change workspace** and a **CAB workspace** (CE-13), and the
sidebar gains its own group (CE-0):

```
Change Enablement            ← its own nav group, NOT under Risk Management
├── Change register          the record: raise, assess, link, tasks
├── CAB                      agenda builder · voting panel · minutes · decision register
├── Change calendar / FSC    forward schedule, freeze windows, collisions
├── Change models            pre-approved standard changes
└── PIR & assurance          post-implementation reviews, unauthorised-change register
```

### Commercial position (ADR-013)

**Change Enablement is its own SKU** — already in the ratified catalogue
(`Architecture/MODULE_SEPARABILITY_AND_SKU_MODEL.md` §6, comparator: ServiceNow ITSM). It depends on the
Base Platform SKU for the committee engine, the workflow engine, evidence and audit — which is exactly the
kernel-plus-package shape ADR-013 requires, and the reason the committee engine must not move into it.
`@RequireModule("CHANGE")` lands with F2-04.

### Sequence

**CE-0 (hours, do now)** → F2-25 → F2-26 → **F2-28 (unblocks S6)** → S5 restructure → S6 CAB as a governed
body → S7 meeting cycle → S8 workflow/evidence/FSC → CE-13 frontend split.

## §6d Council review — 2026-07-25 (three rounds, ten Fable 5 seats, adversarially briefed)

Three rounds of reviewers with deliberately conflicting mandates read this plan against the repository;
several were briefed to argue *against* positions already committed here. Aggregate verdict:
**ACCEPT-WITH-CONDITIONS as a programme** — and round 3 fairly objected that the previous revision
enumerated *no conditions*, making the verdict unauditable. **The conditions are:**

1. **O-2 merge authorisation**, without which no provenance fix reaches production (§1b).
2. **F2-28 closed** before S6 — non-negotiable, now sized M→L with a 16-item acceptance checklist.
3. ~~**F2-35 ratified** before W2-0/F2-04 are written.~~ ✅ **SATISFIED 2026-07-30** — ADR-018 + `Product/SKU_CATALOGUE.md`, with `SkuCatalogueRatchetTest` (4 tests, both mutations proven) holding the code to it.
4. **A retained acceptance-evidence artefact** per slice (probe output + environment fingerprint),
   replacing evidence that currently lives only in commit messages.
5. **W0-7 shipped** before this plan again asserts what a deployment contains.
6. **The four acceptance gates with no owner** (pen test, witnessed restore, WCAG conformance, Saudi
   content provenance) either get an owner in §1b or are restated as accepted risks.

Two corrections to the previous summary, both round 3's: the defects were **not** "almost entirely in the
status layer" — items 7 and 9 below (an unconditional downgrade of a forgery-adjacent finding, and a +0.8
self-rating with an open P0 on the same surface) are **judgment** errors, and calling them clerical
flatters the process. And the claims that verified were the *code* claims: **no round verified an artefact
until round 3, which found the plan's deployment claims false** (§1).

### Errors of mine they caught, now fixed above

| # | Error | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **§6c claimed the committee engine is "used by 14 modules". It is 5.** That was the *workflow*-adoption count transplanted — an ADR-016 R5 violation (uncited number) committed the day ADR-016 was accepted | §6c corrected, command cited, conclusion re-grounded on the kernel workflow engine's structural dependency |
| 2 | **`V318` was claimed by two rows** — Wave 3 for `ck_change_status`, §4 for F2-28 | Wave 3 → V319; **then both moved again** when F2-26 shipped first and took V318 (F2-28 → V319, Wave 3 → V320). The collision was a symptom; the cause is that reservations are ID-ordered and Flyway is delivery-ordered, so *any* reordering of delivery renumbers the tail |
| 3 | **The §4 reservation scheme violated its own Flyway rule** — CE-2 held V320 while delivered in S8, *after* V321–V325 would be applied, so V320 would have been skipped | §4 re-reserved in **delivery order** |
| 4 | **§8 omitted F2-28**, the plan's own highest-open P0, while listing six lesser items | Inserted as slice 3 |
| 5 | **F2-08a was already complete** (`313dd6dd`, `reporting` fan-in 25 → 0) yet listed `⬜` in Wave 0 *and* as next-action #6 in a §8 rewritten the same day — the sixth instance of F-013, mine | Marked ✅; removed from §8 |
| 6 | **The header promised every `F2-*` row is specified in the assessment.** F2-25…F2-30 are not in it — F2-28's only spec was one table row | Header scoped; register named as the source above F2-24 |
| 7 | **F-002's P2 downgrade was unconditional** when one of its two legitimating legs (quorate resolution) rests on the forgeable `quorumMet` | Now **P1 → P2 on F2-28 closure** |
| 8 | **F-009 was labelled `CONTRADICTED`** by tests written the same day | Relabelled `OVERTAKEN` |
| 9 | **Security re-rated 1.8 → 2.6** while a P0 forgery chain was open | **2.3** |
| 10 | **Wave 2 was "un-gated" in §2 and "(gated)" in §3**; F2-06b still posed the dissolved D-4 question | Both corrected |
| 11 | Four stale migration cells, a stale boundary seed (734/14), the tree stamp 10 commits behind, DF-4 "12 vs 13 tests", and number drift (routes 328/329, pages 5,583/5,582 and 4,970/4,969) | All normalised to the re-measured values |

**A finding of mine the second round disproved.** Round 2's spec-readiness seat live-probed F2-25's security claim — `{"id": 424242, "organisationId": 2}` as `compliance` → **200, saved as `id: 78, organisationId: 1`** — and `CommitteeMemberRegistryService.appoint()` has nulled `id` and stamped the tenant since `8373d6f8`, with a comment saying so. **F2-25's cross-tenant mass-assignment claim was false and is withdrawn; the finding drops P1 → P2.** The 500 is real but caused by two NOT NULL date columns and no `@Valid`. I diagnosed it from the controller signature plus an observed 500 and never opened the service, which was one file away and refuted the inference. *A defect inferred is not a defect verified* — the mirror of this review's own "not found is not does not exist" lesson.

**One round-1 council claim I checked and rejected:** the delivery seat reported that the pre-swap safety jar
does not exist and that the `Move-Item` restart would destroy the only copy of the running jar. **False.**
`D:\Deployment\tss-grc\backup\jar-before-restart-20260725.jar` is present at 104,933,172 bytes — byte-size
identical to the live jar — alongside three further copies from earlier deploys
(`.pre-authfix-20260723_201100`, `.pre-S0-20260725_173748`, `.pre-deploy-*`). Its directory listing missed
them. Recorded because a subagent's confident negative is not evidence.

### New findings the council produced

18-field specs are **not yet written** for these; they are stated here and must be added to
`FINDINGS_REGISTER_V2.md` before any is scheduled.

| ID | Finding | Sev | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **F2-31** | **The audit ledger has no tamper-evidence of its own.** Committee minutes carry a SHA-256 signature; `audit.log` — which every control cites as proof — has no hash-chaining, no WORM/retention policy and no protection from administrative deletion. F2-21 already demonstrated that this ledger will faithfully corroborate a forgery. NCA ECC **2-12** requires event-log protection and ≥12-month retention; also ISO 27001 A.8.15, SAMA CSF logging | **P1** — ⚠ *with a caveat round 3 added: hash-chaining an audit ledger is near-theatre while the DBA, platform admin and deployment operator are the same person on the same SQL box. The control is defeated by its own threat model until key custody separates, so pair it with an off-box or append-only sink or state plainly that it is deterrence, not prevention.* | GRC seat → ✅ **DONE 2026-08-01 — `V340`, shipped with `F2-54` as one slice.** The caveat was taken as the specification, not as an objection: the boundary is stated in the code, the migration, **the API response itself** and `external.properties.template`. Rows are sealed with an HMAC over their content chained to the tenant's previous sealed row; `GET /api/v1/audit-log/integrity` names the first broken id, `POST .../integrity/seal` (`AUDIT_LOG:WRITE`) seals on demand **so the control can be exercised rather than only observed — DF-1 is what an unwatched scheduled sweep costs**. The key lives in `external.properties`, never in the database, which is exactly what makes a database-only compromise unable to forge a chain. **Detects** modification, mid-chain deletion, insertion. **Does not detect** tail truncation (impossible from inside the same database — needs an external anchor, not in this slice and not claimed) and does not bind an actor holding both the database and the key. ➕ **Sealing is a periodic PASS, not a hash-on-insert, and the reason is concrete:** audit rows are written on every mutation across 57 modules concurrently, so two racing inserts would claim the same predecessor and **fork** the chain — *a control that reports ordinary concurrency as tampering is worse than the gap it closes.* ➕ **No back-fill:** sealing history would produce a chain verifying cleanly over rows that were never protected — a forged assurance, which is precisely what F2-21 showed this ledger would corroborate. Suite **2,744/0/0** (+17), V340 dev ×2, live **33/33**. 🔑 **Both of this slice's real defects were found by running the probe, not by writing the code:** the first cut answered `verified: true` while skipping 5,000 of 6,088 rows for an algorithm mismatch — **claiming assurance over rows it had not checked, this programme's own signature failure, committed inside the fix for it** — so `verified` (was everything re-checked) and `tamperDetected` (did anything fail) are now separate questions; and `sealTenant` silently assumed every unsealed id exceeds every sealed one, which the tempting "re-seal history after a key rotation" move breaks. ⚠ **And the accident that exposed the first one is its own landmine: a sibling session's backend shares this tree's `target/classes`, so `mvn test` made DevTools hot-restart it into this class before it was finished and it sealed 5,000 rows with NO key into the shared dev database. Port isolation protected my requests, not the database** — see `F2-74` |
| **F2-32** | **The licence lifecycle has no data plane.** ADR-013 gates HTTP controller entry only. The aspect fails closed when `TenantContext` is empty off-request, so it **cannot** be extended to schedulers as designed — and today no scheduler, workflow fan-out, event listener or auto-CAPA path checks entitlement: unlicensed modules keep computing, breaching SLAs and raising CAPAs. De-licensing has no semantics either: `CommitteeReferral` rows live in the **`admin`** schema keyed by `sourceModule='CHANGE'`, so a lapsed licence leaves e-signed minutes and ledger entries referencing records nobody can open, with no answer for a PDPL erasure request. **Plus: nothing creates licence rows when an organisation is created**, so after a fail-closed sweep every new tenant is born blacked out | **P1** | Architecture seat |
| **F2-33** | ⬇ **P2** *(round 3: real but over-rated at P1 — nothing downstream consumes it as evidence, unlike F2-02's executive surfaces; P1 only if onboarding sits in a sales-demo path)*. **Onboarding step 3 "Invite teammates" is localStorage-only** — invited users are never created. ✅ **HONESTY FIXED 2026-07-29; the invitation itself is a decision (D-4).** The copy was worse than the row says: it stated *"They will receive an email with a one-time sign-in link"* **and** *"In production they trigger an email via your configured provider (AWS SES / SendGrid)"* — the second being the more damaging, since it describes a config toggle that does not exist, on a screen a prospect gets shown. ⚠ **It cannot simply be wired, and the measurement is why:** `POST /api/v1/admin/users` is `hasRole('PLATFORM_ADMIN')` — not a tenant admin — and `UserRequest` requires a username, a `@NotBlank` full name and a `@StrongPassword` initial password on create, while the step collects an email and a role slug. Bridging that means an invitation subsystem (token + mail + accept + set-password) or generating other people's passwords, which is a credentials decision, not a UI fix. The platform *can* send mail (BCM crisis comms and scheduled reports use `JavaMailSender`) but nothing invites. **Shipped:** truthful bilingual copy, a link to `/admin` (which opens on its Users tab), and a copy-list action so the collected rows carry across instead of being retyped — the plan's other branch, cutting the step, would have discarded work the operator had already done. `onboardingInviteHonesty.test.ts` is a **wording ratchet** with negation-aware patterns and a self-check against the four sentences that actually shipped, in both languages — W2-1b's `/mock/i` rule cannot see this defect class at all, because nothing is imported and no fake data appears; the screen simply asserts something false about the world | **P1** | Product seat |
| **F2-34** | **Kernel purity, and the rule contradiction that blocks F2-08b.** 40 kernel→business import lines (`platform`→`risk` 14, `privacy` 3, `bcm` 2, `ndi` 2, `audit_lc` 2, `privacy_lc` 2; `integrations`→`socops` 6, `cyber` 2; `dashboard`→`risk` 4, `compliance` 2, `incident` 1), concentrated in `platform/calendar`, `platform/completion/*`, `dashboard` and the `integrations` connectors. The kernel participates in **6** bidirectional pairs on the architecture seat's count, against the **13** the plan's own graph reports — the two use different methods and reconciling them is part of this item. Either way the kernel is the largest cycle participant, so ADR-002's "kernel boots alone" is unachievable and had **no backlog item**. And `MODULE_SEPARABILITY` §2 says kernel imports don't count while §6c's rule says only `common`/`security`/`platform`/`spi` are legal — under the first reading `CommitteePort` is unnecessary. **The boundary ratchet cannot be seeded until one reading wins** — ⚠ **PRICED 2026-08-06, see F2-133.** The contradiction is one ambiguous word (*platform* names both a 5-file top-level package and a 115-file module) and the two counts are simply the two readings: **A = 13 bidirectional** (which the plan reports), **B = 4**. Reading B is **already enforced** by `ChangeModuleBoundaryTest`, so the decision has de facto been taken and merely not recorded. The inversion has grown **40 → 55** while this sat. **Recommendation: ratify B, say so in §2 and §6c, and rename one namespace** — the ambiguity recurs every time the rule is quoted. Still an owner decision; the measurement no longer blocks it | **P1** | Architecture seat |
| **F2-36** | **CI never runs where the work happens.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` triggers **only** on push to master and PRs to master — so the 20 pending commits, including DF-3 and DF-4, have **never executed in CI**; `tsc` is deliberately excluded (~45 errors); and the backend job defines no SQL Server service container, so "CI green" and the local 2,460-test green are not known to mean the same thing. Every item saying "CI fails / CI rule" (F2-01, W2-1b, F2-07b, F2-09, F2-19b, F2-11, F2-A11Y) assumes an enforcement point that fires only **after** merge | **P2** | ⚠ **PARTLY CLOSED, and the review's recommendation to close it outright is REFUTED — measured in `ci.yml` on 2026-07-31.** One of the three legs is fixed: `npx vitest run` now runs there (added today; six FE test files, including the route-reachability ratchet, had never executed in CI). **The other two stand verbatim:** `on:` is still `push: [master]` + `pull_request: [master]`, so **a commit on a feature branch still runs nothing**, and there is still **no SQL Server service container** and **no `tsc` step** — so "CI green" and the local 2,713-test green remain different statements. 🔑 **A row is not closed because part of it was: the cheap leg being fixed is the reason to re-read the other two, not to stop reading.** |

Fold into F2-15 | **P3** | Round 3 |

(B) `Product/PRODUCT_STRATEGY_PACKAGING_AND_SALES.md` — 18 packages that sell **`ACCESS_GOV`** and **`GOVERNANCE` (committees, board packs, signatures)** as *paid*, contradicting ADR-013 and §6c which place them in the mandatory **free** Base. (C) `LicensingService.KNOWN_MODULES` — 20 code-shaped codes. **7 packages appear in no SKU: `ndi` (7,717 LoC, 127 endpoints — the SDAIA/NDMO differentiator), `reporting`, `governance`, `board_pack`, `knowledge`, `workflow_submission`, `datagov`.** Org 1 already holds `EVIDENCE`/`REPORTING` licence rows for capabilities ADR-013 calls mandatory-Base. **W2-0 and F2-04 are unwritable until one catalogue is ratified** — the backfill content differs ~3× by grain | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-07-30 — [ADR-018](TSS-GRC-Platform-Bible/Decisions/ADR-018-SKU-CATALOGUE-RATIFICATION.md)**, decided on the owner's delegation (best practice / market leaders / Saudi fit). 15 SKUs; A's grain won, B's four splits rejected and its three additions adopted, C demoted to a legacy code registry. All 7 orphans homed — `ndi`+`ai` = the new **`DATA_AI_GOV`**; `reporting`/`knowledge`/`governance`/`workflow_submission` = Base; `board_pack` = the new paid **`BOARD`**; `datagov` **explicitly unassigned** (delete or fill). Measurement changed two answers: `governance` is *not* the committee engine (that is `admin/committee`, 51 files in a kernel package), and two SKUs therefore have boundaries that are **not** package boundaries → **F2-57** | Product + Architecture seats |

### Recommendations accepted, pending your call

- **Rename the SKU "Change Enablement" → "Change Governance & CAB".** Under the ITSM name it competes with
  ServiceNow/Jira on CMDB, service catalogue and release pipelines and loses on brand. What §6c actually
  builds is change *governance* — CAB quorum integrity, SoD-recusal, e-signed minutes, evidence-bound
  conditions, an unauthorised-change register — sold to the CISO/CAE/CCO as SAMA CSF and NCA ECC change
  control. Same code, different buyer, winnable deal. **Your decision (D-9).**
- **CE-0 and F2-07c contradict each other.** `UX_REDESIGN_BLUEPRINT.md:68` places `change_mgmt` inside
  workspace 12 "Governance & Administration" and there is **no Change workspace among the 12** — so
  Wave 4's registry-generated navigation would delete CE-0's group and re-file Change under an admin
  catch-all, silently reversing the owner's direction. **Fix in the same commit as CE-0:** amend the
  blueprint table (Change becomes workspace 13) and give F2-01's registry a `workspace:` field from day
  one, so the decision lives in data rather than in a `.tsx` file Wave 4 retires.
- **W1-5 (golden-path committee integration test) becomes a hard S6 gate** beside F2-28 — a CAB built on
  an engine proven only at unit level repeats F2-18.
- **Grandfather by grant-all-then-tighten, not by data presence.** "License every SKU a tenant has data
  in" false-negatives on read-only, configured-but-empty and seasonal usage → 403s for paying customers.
- **F2-01 becomes a two-stage registry.** v1 enforces only package↔entry parity, schema agreement and
  module-count claims. `sku`/`owner`/`test_floor` become enforceable as they are actually decided —
  otherwise a CI-enforced registry stuffed with placeholder SKUs is the F2-14 anti-pattern.
- **Pull i18n forward.** It was silently moved from Wave 1 (where the UX blueprint put it) to Wave 4 with
  no stated reason, on a product whose headline claim is Arabic-first with 49 zero-Arabic pages. Minimum:
  the ESLint ratchet plus the pages on the five persona journeys. Same for **F2-A11Y's token-level
  contrast fix** — days of work, parked behind two waves.
- **State a contention rule:** if Wave 1 and Wave 3 compete for the same engineer, **F2-03 Tier-1 floors on
  `data_officer` and `outsourcing` outrank S6–S8.** W3-5 ("no zero-test module in any commercial scope
  statement") makes the flagship Saudi SKU unsellable until F2-03 lands, so it is revenue critical-path.

### The structural finding — accepted, not yet acted on

Round 3's strongest argument is that this file's **error rate is a data-structure problem, not a diligence
problem**: the same author catches and creates these errors within hours. v2.2 is simultaneously a backlog
(§3), a status dashboard (§1), an owner register (§1b), a migration ledger (§4), a decision log (§6), an
audit adjudication (§6b), an architecture spec (§6c) and council minutes (§6d) — with the backlog living in
**four** places and **three sequences that disagree** (§6b: F2-25→26→28→27→30→29 · §6c: "CE-0 do now" ·
§8: CE-0 last, F2-30 absent). Six revisions in under three hours is how the Wave-3 cells, the W0-8 phantom
and the 328/329 remnant all shipped stale *on the evening the council fixed their predecessors*. Also
unfalsifiable: the preamble claims to merge "stages S0–**S9**", and **S9 is defined nowhere in the repository**.

**Accepted remedy (not yet done — it is real work, and half-doing it would make things worse):** one
machine-checkable backlog containing every open item exactly once (id, wave, size, status, blocked-on,
migration) that F2-01's CI can lint; §4 as the only migration ledger, with **migration numbers deleted from
§3**; review rounds appending to `FINDINGS_REGISTER_V2.md` only, never new plan sections; and §1's numbers
generated by script per F2-19b rather than typed. The plan already prescribes every one of these mechanisms
for *other* documents.

### Absences the council named that this plan had not consciously excluded

- **Data import / time-to-first-value.** Nothing anywhere imports an existing risk register, control
  library or obligation list. Every GRC displacement deal opens with "load our register"; pilots that
  start from a blank screen stall. Not in scope, not out of scope — simply absent.
- **The locked-module upsell surface.** F2-04 hides unlicensed modules; per-module SKUs on one deployment
  is an *expansion-revenue* architecture, so the merchandising half (a visible locked workspace with
  "licensed under X — request a trial") should exist. One component.
- **Mobile approvals and signatures** — the highest-frequency executive touch, with no item and no
  out-of-scope entry, which by this plan's own standard ("absence reads as a decision") it must have.
- **Day-zero empty states** across ~300 registers, and **nobody owns a curated demo tenant + script**.
- **Retention, disposition and legal hold** (PDPL storage limitation; Companies Law minute retention).
- **Witnessed restore/failover, an independent penetration test, and a WCAG conformance statement** have
  no owner — §7 calls them out of scope, which the GRC seat fairly reads as *silent rejections of three
  of the audit's own §11 acceptance gates*.
- **An acceptance-evidence dossier.** The §5 definition of done is strong engineering evidence but wholly
  **self-attested and unretained** — probe output lives in commit messages, mutation proofs leave no
  artefact. Adding a retained pack (probe output + environment fingerprint + reviewer sign-off) is
  actionable now; the *independence* half is a business decision, not a schedulable task.

---

## §7 Deliberately not in scope

Recorded so absence reads as a decision, not an oversight:

- **Microservices extraction.** `ADR-001` rejects it; the ratcheted boundary test achieves the discipline
  without the operational cost.
- **A visual redesign / component-library replacement.** Contradicted by measurement — 99% shared-component
  adoption and exact 5,677-key Arabic parity. The IA is the work; the design system stays.
- **Building a new entitlement layer.** One exists (F2-04); a second would create two sources of truth.
- **Back-filling provenance onto historical votes** (DF-3) or **fabricating committees** for orgs 2/3
  (V314). Both would make an absent control *look* present — the exact failure mode of F2-14.
- **Re-adjudicating the 599-line vendor findings register** finding-by-finding — W3-4 reconciles it
  against this backlog instead.
- **A penetration test, a WCAG conformance statement, and a vendor BCP.** Real gaps, outside engineering
  scope; escalate as commercial commitments.

### Absences named by the 2026-07-30 council that are **not** decided out of scope

Filed here to stop them being *read* as exclusions. Each is a gap this plan had not recorded at all — which
is F2-19's failure mode, so they are written down before any of them is judged.

1. **Enterprise identity federation — SAML/OIDC SSO, enforced MFA, SCIM/AD provisioning.** Seat C: page one
   of every Saudi bank RFP. **Verified genuinely absent**: `saml`, `oidc`, `sso`, `scim`, `federat` and `mfa`
   match nothing in this plan — not in the backlog, not in §7. Local username/password JWT accounts alone
   fail that page. This is the largest unrecorded commercial gap in the document.
2. **Vendor privileged access — the 60th role.** Everything in Wave 0b retightens 59 customer roles;
   `PLATFORM_ADMIN` **bypasses permission checks in code**. SAMA outsourcing-style vendor risk asks which
   vendor staff can reach tenant data, how it is logged, and whether the customer can see that log. The
   bypass is deliberate and documented — the *absence of a customer-visible record of its use* is not.
3. **Fix-to-production latency as a scoreable control.** Seat C's sharpest observation: prod runs the
   2026-07-23 jar while authorisation fixes sit staged behind a manual elevated restart. Questionnaires ask
   for patch SLAs; *"criticals wait for an owner's shell session"* is itself a finding, and it is the reason
   Wave 0b ships as one batch instead of six slices. **O-1 is not just a chore — it is a control.**
4. **A per-connector data-transfer register.** D-8 creates the permission that protects the egress record;
   nobody created the record. Destination country, PDPL art. 29 transfer basis, outsourcing materiality and
   no-objection reference, per Splunk/ServiceNow/webhook target. Seat A: this is the artefact SDAIA and SAMA
   outsourcing supervision actually inspect, and D-8 leaves their real question unanswerable by design.
5. **Recertification evidence over the new grants.** ECC 2-2 / ISO 27001 A.5.18 want proof of *periodic
   access review*, not a better matrix. After D-10a and D-8, run the platform's **own** IAM recertification
   campaign over the EXPORT and INTEGRATIONS grants and retain the minutes — the module already exists, so
   this is cheap and it makes the platform its own reference customer.
6. **Independent assurance artefacts** (ISO 27001 / SOC 2 / a current pentest letter) — already in §7 above
   as owner-owned, restated here only because Seat C put it *before* the demo in a bank's TPRM gate, which
   is earlier than this plan had it.

### Next engineering slices, in order

**0. Resolve F2-28's open design questions first.** ⚠ *Corrected: a reviewer said F2-28's "whole
specification is one table row" and I repeated it — **false**. A full 18-field spec has been in
`Registers/FINDINGS_REGISTER_V2.md` since `c8f672dc`, with the endpoint inventory, the roster-snapshot
recommendation and the `quorum_pct` trade-off.* What is genuinely missing is narrower and must be settled
before V319 is authored: **is the denominator the active roster or those present** (charters differ, and the
register's own trade-off note flags it); how **external directors with no platform login** are represented;
what happens to the **20 existing resolutions** whose tallies were seeded rather than computed; how
apologies and non-voting invitees count; and whether the roster snapshot is a new table or a JSON column.

1. ~~**F2-25**~~ ✅ **DONE `55fcbe25`** — DTO + `@Valid` + in-tenant committee check. The
   foreign-`organisationId` probe this row demanded was run and **disproved the finding's security half**;
   the FE had also gated both dates already, so only API callers ever saw the 500.
2. ~~**F2-26**~~ ✅ **DONE 2026-07-26** — seats link to platform users, with an explicit third state for
   directors who will never have a login. Proven by the probe that had been failing since D-7: `so_officer`
   casting their **own** vote, `on_behalf = 0`. **Two of F2-28's five open questions are now answered by
   construction:** *"how are external directors with no platform login represented"* → the declared-EXTERNAL
   state, which also leaves the coverage denominator; and *"do apologies and non-voting invitees count"* →
   the linkage report already separates voting from non-voting seats, so the denominator question is now only
   about **presence**, not about **eligibility**.
3. ~~**F2-28 / DF-5**~~ ✅ **DONE 2026-07-26 `V319`** — was the highest open P0, and absent from this list until
   the council caught it. Roster-bound attendance, denominator from the roster, client `quorumMet` refused,
   arithmetic persisted, replacement audited. *(V318 went to F2-26 — Flyway applies in version order, so the
   number goes to whatever ships first, per §4.)*
   **All five open questions are now answered**, four by construction and one deferred with a reason:
   - *roster-vs-present denominator* → **the roster.** That is what quorum means; a rule counting only those
     present is a different rule, and per-committee quorum policy is S6/CE-3's work.
   - *external directors with no login* → F2-26's declared-EXTERNAL state, plus first-class **guests** here.
   - *apologies / non-voting invitees* → non-voting seats never enter either side; a guest enters neither.
   - *snapshot as a new table or a JSON column* → **neither**: five persisted columns. Once rows are
     seat-bound the sheet *is* the per-seat snapshot, so a table would have duplicated it.
   - *the 20 resolutions whose tallies are seeded scalars with zero vote rows* → **still open, and it is not a
     quorum question.** It is the *vote-tally* equivalent of the same defect and belongs with F2-29. Any code
     path calling `recomputeTally` still rewrites those to `0/0/0 DEFERRED`. **Carried forward as F2-40.**
   ➕ F2-26 handed this a guarantee it would otherwise have had to build: one person cannot hold two ACTIVE
   seats on one committee, so a roster-derived denominator cannot double-count a member.
4. ~~**F2-27** — enforce `MODULE:EXPORT` on every export endpoint, per-tenant impact query first.~~
   ✅ **DONE 2026-07-26** (`V320`, ~4 h). 40 handlers / 18 controllers — **not** the 24 sites in 17 files the
   round-2 note recorded, because `CONTENT_DISPOSITION` literals under-count by 15 and a source regex cannot
   read `@PreAuthorize(READ_GUARD)` constants. **The impact query falsified both design calls the plan had

   deliberately not; the one ADMIN export is a **blank form template** → allowlisted rather than break 14 roles

   silent-failure argument for catalogued permissions over role lists, and F2-05's case in one line.

   pairs and not one `@NotBlank` in any entity. Two transferable results: **(a)** the enforcement mechanism was broken

>
> **F2-04 split into (a) server and (b) frontend, deliberately, and the order is not arbitrary.** (a) is the P0: until the server refuses, the commercial model is unenforceable and no FE change can make it enforceable. (b) is UX — hiding what a tenant has not bought — and it is now *safe to defer* precisely because (a) landed: an unentitled module is denied at the API whether or not the sidebar still offers it. Deferring them the other way round would have shipped a paywall that a browser devtools console removes.
>
> ⚠ **Reordered 2026-07-31 (third time today) and this time the ordering PRINCIPLE changed, not just the sequence.** Every previous pass sequenced **findings**. Eleven items closed today, and the ones that mattered clustered: **six of them share one cause**. Sequencing findings meant re-solving the same cause six times and discovering it again each time. **The queue is now led by cause — see §1c.**
>
> 🔑 **The spine is ONE slice: script the missing DDL from the live dev catalog, then prove it on an empty database.** ⚠ **Re-scoped 2026-07-31 after the review; the previous framing — `F2-72 → F2-70`, convert 67 tables by hand with a column comparator guarding the copies — is kept below because the reasoning that produced it was sound and still wrong.** V338's failure was copying stale *files*; a generator that reads `sys.schemas`/`sys.columns`/`sys.indexes` **cannot be stale**, and dev is the authoritative schema (prod is its clone). So the comparator is not the gate — it falls out of the generator's own diff nearly free, and stays afterwards as the standing instrument for the exposure it correctly names (dev applied by hand via pyodbc, prod by Flyway). Hibernate's `ddl-auto` generation is the *weaker* generator here and was rejected on that basis: it would drop the DB-side CHECKs, filtered indexes and defaults the bootstrap added, which the entities do not declare. **Per-module archaeology buys 67 chances to repeat the defect; one catalog script plus one empty-database proof buys none.**
>
> ➕ **Two facts that make F2-70 bigger than its row claimed, both verified here rather than accepted:** **(i) 11 of the 14 schemas the 67 live in are created by no migration at all** — `cpty, data_officer, dataclass, fraud, openbanking, opres, outsourcing, reginspect, regreporting, rrp, sharia` — so the table count could be driven to **zero** while an empty-database build still died on the first `CREATE TABLE`; that hole now has its own ratchet. **(ii) A full V1→head run on an empty database does not complete minus-67 — it aborts no later than V47** ⚠ **MEASURED 2026-08-01 and this was optimistic by 45 files: it aborts at `V2`, and the V47 guard was never reached — `F2-77`. The V3 schema hole (`F2-75`) sits between them. An estimate stated with a version number reads like a measurement; this one was reasoning about one file that happened to be wrong about the other 46.**, whose guard is `IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.columns WHERE object_id = OBJECT_ID('evidence.evidence_versions'…))`: on an empty database `OBJECT_ID` is NULL, the guard passes, and the `ALTER TABLE` beneath it fails. **A column-existence guard is not a table-existence guard.** Prod's baselined run works only because it runs over the restored dump.
>
> 📜 **Superseded framing, kept because it was honestly reached:** the spine was `F2-72 → F2-70`, and that dependency was discovered by getting it wrong. F2-70 is *convert 67 bootstrap-only tables into migrations* — the work that makes the platform deployable to a customer at all. The obvious way to do it is to copy the existing DDL, and **V338 proved that fails**: re-issuing V12–V14 verbatim produced a schema three months stale and Hibernate rejected it on the first call (`Invalid column name 'deleted_by'`). Doing that 67 times by hand is 67 chances to ship the same defect. **So the column-comparison tool (F2-72) is not a nice-to-have after F2-70; it is the only way to do F2-70 correctly.** One migration taught it; the tool generalises it.
>
> ➕ **F2-54 stays first** because it is half-written and small, and because every row it mis-attributes is evidence this product sells to an examiner. Finish what is in flight before starting a spine.
>
> ➕ **F2-61(a) moves ahead of F2-63 and F2-61(c)**, unchanged from the last pass: the amendment gates both, and drafting it is engineer work under ADR-017's standing delegation, not an owner wait.
>
> ➕ ~~**A17-D8 needs nothing further** — F2-56 made its four grant targets global (1 of 4 → 4 of 4), so the per-tenant workaround the second-model review proposed is moot.~~ ✅ **Right, and confirmed by measurement before the grant was written**: all four targets are `organisation_id IS NULL` on **both** databases, and V343 THROWs if a grant ever reaches no global role. **Shipped 2026-08-01.** What this note did *not* anticipate is that 5 of the 20 guards should stay role guards — converting them would have revoked `RISK_MANAGER`'s vulnerability risk-acceptance. See the Wave 0b row.
>
> ❌ **What did NOT move, and why that is the harder judgement.** W2-0/F2-04 (the entitlement revenue leak) stays behind the schema spine even though it is the biggest *commercial* item, because F2-70 is the bigger *existential* one: **you cannot sell a SKU to a tenant you cannot deploy.** And A17-D2 stays last: it is spine work whose absence blocks nothing that is currently scheduled. ⚠ **Both halves of that sentence were wrong, and A17-D2 closed on 2026-08-01 in one slice with no migration.** It was not spine work — the per-tenant config table had existed since V310 — and its absence was not blocking nothing: orgs 2 and 3 were approving CAB-routed changes with no committee at all (**F2-87**). *"Blocks nothing scheduled" measures the roadmap; it does not measure the platform.*
>
> **Reordered again 2026-07-31, on the second-model review, and two of its three arguments were right.**
>
> ➕ **Accepted: a deployment item belongs in the queue, and it belongs first.** Prod has served 2026-07-23 code for eight days while merged fixes for forgeable votes and self-approval sit undeployed; the staged artifact was *silently rebuilt* on 07-30 and this plan described it wrongly for six days. The restart is the scarce resource, so **W0-2b-final** (rebuild from master → restage → verify with `deployment_identity.py` → hand O-1 the elevated command) goes first and everything else rides batch 2. Nothing waits on it.
>
> ➕ **Accepted: F2-61 is not an owner gate, and parking D-10b(b) behind it was wrong.** The review's sharpest point: ADR-017 D-10 already ratified an export tier rule under standing delegation, so F2-61 is an *engineer-drafted amendment* to a decided rule, not a fresh policy call — **and its proposed default-deny for generic personas contradicts that rule's "EXPORT follows READ", since `VIEWER` holds READ nearly everywhere.** That contradiction has to be *reconciled in an amendment*, not restated as a new principle. Meanwhile D-10b(b) needs no policy at all: its rationale (external supervisory authority, requisition-driven furnishing, **zero holders in both databases**) is complete in ADR-017 D-10b and F2-59. So the split is **F2-61(a)** draft the amendment (does not gate anything) → **F2-61(c)** module-sized revocations, later.
>
> ❌ **Rejected: that D-8 should stay blocked on F2-56 and answer it "in-migration" via the V332 pattern.** That was sound advice against the file as it stood, but F2-56 is now **fixed outright** (V334) — so D-8 needs no per-tenant workaround, no org-scoped duplicate rows, and no THROW-if-no-global-holder hedge. Its four grant targets went from **1 of 4 global to 4 of 4**. Fixing the cause beat encoding the symptom into one more migration.
>
> **F2-56 was taken ahead of all of it and that is worth defending**, because it was not in the queue at all: it is upstream of A17-D8, A17-D10a, F2-59 *and* W2-0, each of which carried a "⚠ Read F2-56 first". No reordering can put a fix after its own dependents.
>
> **The order changed on 2026-07-31, and the reason is the reason to keep re-deriving it.** D-10b was re-scoped by measurement into a shippable half and a deferred half; its shippable half became **F2-60** and is done. Its remaining half — revoking `REGULATOR_VIEWER`'s standing register exports — **is an instance of F2-61's policy, so it must follow it.** Doing the instance first would set a precedent for 24 modules by accident, in a slice whose stated subject is one role. **F2-61 is now ahead of D-10b(b), and D-8 has not moved.**
>
> *D-10a and D-5 both landed 2026-07-31 (@`17d7d0d9`…`b99a3d53`). The pair held: one surface, one probe set, one restart, and D-5's JSON route inherited the tier D-10a had just narrowed — had the order been reversed it would have exposed the aggregate on the 24-of-59-role disjunction. **Next is D-10b.***

⚠ **`D-n` means two different things in this file, and it has bitten already.** §6 numbers the *assessment's* decisions (ADR-013/014/015); Wave 0b and this section number **ADR-017's**. Five tokens collide: **D-2** (ECAB SLA · per-tenant CAB config), **D-3** (per-module SKUs · ECAB ratification SLA), **D-4** (incident SKU, *dissolved* · invitation subsystem, *open* — a collision **inside §6**), **D-5** (Saudi-pack governance · board-pack JSON), **D-8** (**CAB voting rights, ADR-015** · **`INTEGRATIONS` catalogue**). This file renamed `W0-1` out of "D-1" specifically because it collided with a decision id, then reintroduced the class at five times the scale. **I hit it myself this session** — reading "D-8" in the queue and pulling up the CAB-voting decision. Until the rename lands, **every ADR-017 item in Wave 0b and §8 should be read as `A17-D<n>`**, and the queue below is written that way. ➡ Filed as part of the §6d structural remedy.

Three things about that order are worth carrying in the head rather than looking up:

- **D-10a and D-5 are one slice-pair, not two neighbours.** Same surface, same probe set, same restart.
  My first draft put D-8 between them; that was the council's headline correction and it was right.
- **D-10 was split** into the shippable narrowing (D-10a) and the unbuilt mechanism (D-10b). A decision
  record that bundles "delete these grants" with "invent an assurance-mandate entity" is not one slice, and
  treating it as one is how a slice silently becomes a wave.
- **D-3 left the queue's spirit even though it kept its slot**: it is re-homed into the S6/S7 rebuild, since
  its escalation target does not exist. A control whose consequence has nowhere to land is a report.

**Two of my own recorded positions were falsified in the course of ordering this** — the same rate the rest of
the programme runs at, which is the argument for measuring before sequencing and not only before building:
ADR-017 D-5's safety reasoning named the wrong module, and my claim that D-8 "narrows a PDPL surface" had the
direction backwards. Both are corrected in §3 Wave 0b and in the ADR.
